### Cold plate subproject update
Speakers: Emily Clark (Dell), Berhanu Wondimu (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8590/
This is a presentation to provide updates on the ongoing activities within the cold plate subproject throughout the year

### Considerations for Shipping and Storage of 1-Phase Cold Plate Liquid Cooled Racks & Servers
Speakers: Cheng Chen (Meta), Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8893/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/considerations-for-shipping-and-storage-of-1-phase-coldplate-liquid-cooled-racks-and-servers-final-march-2026-pdf
Liquid cooled AI infrastructure requires individual racks and servers to be shipped and stored correctly to the final deployment site. One important aspect is whether the liquid cooled racks and servers should be shipped filled, partially filled, dry or semi dry. Each scenario has its benefits and drawbacks which are demonstrated in this presentation. We also consider best practices to reduce unwanted failures during shipping and storage. This presentation walkthroughs the guidelines for the shipment and storage of liquid-cooled IT servers and racks. It addresses both technical risks and operational considerations, while introducing best practices to mitigate potential issues such as thermal expansion, corrosion, and coolant degradation.

### Fluid Velocity and Erosion Updates
Speakers: Pradip Sairam Pichumani (Meta), Sung A Kim (bp Castrol)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9004/
As AI workload density drives coolant flow rates higher across direct liquid cooling deployments, the erosion behavior of PG25 glycol formulations against copper cold plate surfaces becomes a critical — and largely uncharacterized — design constraint. This presentation describes a controlled experiment designed to establish the maximum allowable fluid velocity for PG25 in copper cold plates before erosion onset, with particular focus on inlet impingement zones and microchannel distribution geometry. Conducted at a dedicated test facility with hyperscaler collaboration, the study applies incremental velocity profiling and rigorous post-test inspection methodology to bracket erosion thresholds with defined confidence. Results will provide the industry's first empirically grounded velocity limits for PG25 in copper DLC systems — directly applicable to procurement specifications, system design envelopes, and coolant management protocols.

### Cooling the 448G Frontier: Thermal Architecture for 500 W-Class HSIO Using Direct-to-Chip Liquid Cooling
Speakers: Divya Bandaru (TE Connectivity), Sridhar Mukthineni (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9178/
The rapid growth of AI clusters and high-performance data center fabrics is driving a fundamental shift in how the industry approaches power delivery, signal density, and thermal management at the interconnect level. As Module TDPs for extremely dense 448G HSIO implementations move beyond 500 W, conventional cooling methods are reaching their limits, making direct-to-chip liquid cooling an increasingly necessary solution. This session will share key learnings from evaluating cold plate concepts for emerging HSIO architectures, focusing on cooling performance, system integration, and deployment readiness as high-speed interconnects begin to approach compute-class thermal loads.

### Pushing the Single-Phase Cold Plate Limit: Design Practice, Field Learnings, and the Runway Before Two-Phase Scales
Speakers: Weihua Tang (Google), REMCO VAN ERP (Corintis), Oscar Farias Moguel (Microsoft), Yin Hang (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9230/
Two-phase cooling is often called the endgame for AI accelerator thermal management, but it's a future prospect, not today's reality. Fluid qualification, serviceability, supply chain, and standardization mean hyperscale deployment still needs time. Meanwhile the single-phase cold plate remains the workhorse, even as accelerator power climbs. The real question isn't whether to move to two-phase, but how to intentionally maximize single-phase. This session convenes cold plate designers, integrators, and hyperscale thermal specialists to share methods and field experience for pushing single-phase limits--emerging geometries, flow and pressure-drop allocation, manifolds, and die-level hotspot management in large multi-die packages. Panelists give candid insight from real deployments: model-vs-reality gaps, reliability and fouling surprises, and the role of manufacturing tolerances. We'll frame single-phase advances as a deliberate bridge--optimizing headroom while two-phase matures.

### Latest Advancements with Thermal Interface Materials
Speakers: Ryan Mayberry (Indium Corporation), Milad Samie (CoolIT Systems), Jake Terracina (actnano), Darshan Ravoori (Graphcore), Stephen Stagon (Boston Materials)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9019/ | materials: https://www.linkedin.com/posts/robcurtisatx_amd-datacenterworld-aiinfrastructure-activity-7453547409479012353-Pjv7?utm_source=share&utm_medium=member_desktop&rcm=ACoAAAB0OfQB43TCvCUhOYdxKNVAdsjby9XWxRs | pdf: https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/11038300
Enabling Liquid Metal TIMs for the AI Era: Characterizing Warpage and Preventing Shorts As AI accelerators move past 2000W, thermal interface materials are becoming one of the main limits on package performance and reliability. This talk covers three areas. 1. Liquid Metal TIM Advantages Presented by Indium Corp and CoolIT Liquid metal (LM) TIMs have ultra-low thermal resistance compared with conventional TIMs. They are self-healing, so they don’t dry out or pump out the way greases and gels do. They also conform to complex die surface topology under load. Together, these properties make LM TIMs a scalable choice for extreme power densities. 2. In-Situ Warpage Characterization Presented by Boston Materials and Graphcore To design an LM TIM well, you need the real geometry of the gap it fills. Boston Materials present Fit-Freeze-Measure (FFM), which uses an LM preform that conforms to the loaded die and then solidifies. This preserves the true in-situ geometry. Multi-point mapping then shows the actual TIM cavity space, not an idealized one. 3. Shorting Protection for LM TIMs Presented by actnano Gallium-alloy migration can short PCB traces. actnano introduced a barrier material that prevents LM shorting events. It has been validated across metals, substrates and thermal cycling, and it drops into existing package assembly flows. Attendees will leave with a practical path to deploying LM TIMs reliably at scale.

### Reducing Operational Complexity Through Standardized Two-Phase Liquid Cooling for AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Jake Hsieh (Wiwynn Corporation), Powpow Wu (ASRock Rack Inc.), REMCO VAN ERP (Corintis), Jiu Xu (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8891/
As AI clusters scale beyond 100kW per rack, operators face growing thermal, deployment & interoperability challenges. While 2-phase direct-to-chip cooling has demonstrated support of industry's highest power densities, implementation approaches remain fragmented, creating operational complexity and slowing adoption. This session proposes an effort bringing together industry stakeholders to define 2-phase liquid cooling deployments common architectures & interfaces. By collaborative OCP work & real-world deployments, we will present operational & thermal performance data demonstrating stable, sustained GPU operation, improved temperature uniformity, hotspot mitigation & reduced thermal throttling in AI clusters while enabling efficient infrastructure utilization. Explore how standardization can simplify deployment, improve interoperability, reduce operational risk, & accelerate the industry's transition to higher-density AI infrastructure through more predictable & scalable deployment

### A Novel Liquid Cooling Solution: A Negative-Pressure Two-Phase Water-Boiling Cold Plate with Enhanced Thermal Performance
Speakers: Liang-Ching Cheng (Wistron Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8855/
This study investigates the thermal performance of a negative-pressure two-phase cold plate using RO water as the working fluid. Under a uniform heat flux exceeding 300 W/cm² and flow rates from 0.8 to 4.0 LPM, the thermal impedance ranges from 0.23 to 0.16 °C·cm²/W. The outlet pressure is maintained at ~8 kPa, corresponding to a boiling temperature of 42 °C. Compared to positive-pressure single-phase operation, the proposed design improves thermal performance by 5–15%, depending on flow rate. To achieve a thermal impedance of 0.16 °C·cm²/W, the required flow rate is reduced by 0.92 LPM. A PUE of ~1.03 is achieved at an inlet temperature of 40 °C. Future work will focus on higher inlet temperatures and increased heat loads.

### System-level Challenges and Considerations in Transitioning from Single-Phase to Two-Phase Cooling
Speakers: Jake Hsieh (Wiwynn Corporation), Yuzhou Lin (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9041/
The rapid growth of artificial intelligence, high-performance computing, and cloud services is driving unprecedented heat loads in data centers. Two-phase liquid cooling is a promising approach for next-generation thermal management, enabling high heat transfer through latent heat utilization while mitigating leakage-induced damage due to the dielectric nature of the working fluid. However, its deployment at data center scale introduces significant system-level challenges. This study investigates key challenges in transitioning from single-phase to two-phase cooling, including pressure drop, flow stability, and configuration design. Among these challenges, flow distribution in parallel configurations, such as between cold plates, servers, or racks, remains one of the most critical and difficult aspects for practical implementation of two-phase systems. Technical insights are provided to address these challenges, support practical deployment, and highlight future research directions.

### Chemical-Free Water Chemistry Management for Reliable Operation of Copper Cold Plates in Data Center Liquid Cooling
Speakers: Stirling Boston (EasyWater - A Watts Brand), Fei Dai (Goaland), Kelley Mullick (Avayla)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9040/
A closed-loop purification system integrating ion-exchange purification, dissolved oxygen (DO) control, and ultraviolet sterilization was developed to investigate the effects of water chemistry parameters on copper corrosion behavior. During testing, the system maintained a pH of 7.5 ± 0.5, conductivity ≤ 2 μS/cm, dissolved oxygen concentration ≤ 0.20 ppm, and total microbial counts ≤ 100 CFU/mL. Under these conditions, the corrosion rate of copper coupons remained between 0.50 and 1.00 μm/y. The proposed approach has been validated through field deployments in two data center liquid-cooling pilot projects, demonstrating long-term stable control of coolant conductivity, dissolved oxygen concentration, pH, and microbial levels. Results show that the approach eliminates the need for chemical additives, reduces waste-fluid generation, simplifies maintenance, lowers operating costs, and offers strong potential for scalable deployment and replication.

### Deep Dive: Biomanagement for Liquid Cooling
Speakers: Dakota Bunn (Ecolab), Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10154/
Deep Dive: Biomanagement for Liquid Cooling

### Fluid Monitoring: From Sensors to Insights
Speakers: Mike Murcia (Ecolab), Sean Barlett (Switch), Pascal Michel (Nordran, Inc.), Sreya Dutta (Dynalene Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10155/
Real-time monitoring of propylene glycol-based coolants is critical to reliable liquid-cooled data center operation. Periodic sampling and offline analysis leave gaps in which contamination, corrosion, or degradation can progress undetected. Online sensors provide continuous visibility, but their diagnostic value depends on the fluid type and upset condition. This presentation provides practical guidance for online coolant monitoring and evaluates sensor response to upsets across IAT, OAT, and low-conductivity coolants. It covers vendor-agnostic selection and deployment considerations, including accuracy, material compatibility, flow, communications, installation approach, serviceability, and leak management. Controlled-upset results are compared with lab data to show how coolant chemistry influences sensor readings and which measurements provide the greatest diagnostic value.

## Cooling: Immersion

### OCP Immersion Project: Scaling Open Collaboration for Immersion Cooling Adoption
Speakers: Rolf Brink (Promersion), Jessica Gullbrand (Eaton)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8553/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/wiki/Cooling_Environments/Immersion
The OCP Immersion Project has become a central collaboration platform for advancing immersion cooling across the data center ecosystem. As liquid cooling moves from early adoption toward broader deployment, the industry needs more than individual technology solutions. It needs shared requirements, guidance, open specifications, qualification processes, and alignment between IT equipment, fluids, materials, facility integration, operations, and sustainability goals. This session will provide an overview of the Immersion Project’s structure, priorities, active workstreams, and current areas of contribution. It will explain how the project supports industry readiness by creating a neutral environment where end users, IT vendors, cooling solution providers, fluid suppliers, component manufacturers, researchers, and operators can work together. Attendees will gain insight into project progress, relevance to the broader liquid cooling transition, and pathways for effective engagement.

### Advancing Immersion Cooling Safety: From Fluid Qualification to System Compliance
Speakers: Lizzy Fernando (UL Solutions), Mark Olson (UL Solutions)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9168/
As immersion cooling adoption accelerates, the industry must define consistent, scalable safety and performance requirements for immersion fluids. While standards like IEC 62368-1 provide a strong system level safety foundation, they do not fully address the material, chemical, and thermal risks of dielectric fluids. This session presents UL 2417, an emerging standard focused on evaluating critical fluid properties and enabling component level qualification. We show how UL 2417 complements IEC 62368-1 and highlight the role of industry collaboration in shaping practical, globally relevant requirements. Drawing on engagement with the OCP community through the Fluids-Specifications and Industry Liaison workstreams, we will demonstrate how real world feedback, supplier insights, and shared data can actively influence the evolution of UL 2417 and ensure it keeps pace with innovation in immersion cooling.

### OCP/SwRI Collaboration - Data Center Immersion Fluids Consortium
Speakers: Pete Morgan (SwRI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8965/ | materials: http://www.dcif.swri.org/
In collaboration with OCP, Southwest Research Institute (SwRI) is initiating the Data Center Immersion Fluids (DCIF) Consortium. The goal for this consortium is to provide the industry with an independent test house which is aligned with OCP and to help fill the gaps regarding hardware/fluids test methods relating to immersion technology. This presentation will give an update on the goals, members, and research topics for the consortium.

### Thermochemical Reliability Workstream Update
Speakers: Samantha Yates (Intel), Brian Kinkade (FUCHS LUBRICANTS CO.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9469/
Speakers include : Samantha Yates (Intel), Brian Kinkade (FUCHS Lubricants), Betsy Cossette (ExxonMobil), Bobby Glidwell (Magnalytix), Punith Shivaprasad (Shell) and Beau The Immersion Reliability Committee addresses the specific needs and challenges associated with assessing the quality and reliability of IT hardware deployed in an immersion solution. The committee focuses on developing industry standardized testing guidance to measure and detect material compatibility and thermochemical reliability risks and failure modes related to the interaction of materials and hardware with an immersion fluid. The Reliability Committee includes the Material Compatibility, Component Compatibility, and Thermochemical Reliability Workstreams. The new contributions from these workstreams will be presented to the community and the future initiatives will be discussed.

### Material Compatibility and Long-Term Hardware Integrity of Enterprise SSDs in Two-Phase Immersion Cooling
Speakers: Youngnam Kim (Samsung Electronics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9039/
AI workloads require two-phase immersion cooling, making hardware-fluid compatibility vital for open infrastructure. This session presents a material integrity validation driven by fellow OCP partners. Samsung will share data from 10,000+ hours of enterprise SSD operations using Chemours’ 2P50 fluid in a 48U infrastructure developed with LiquidStack and Chemours. While facility operations used a third-party framework, Samsung focused on hardware optimization and reliability analytics. Testing confirmed flawless compatibility with zero leaching into the 2P50 fluid. Evaluations of PCBs and connectors proved coolant properties remained uncompromised with no degradation. Aligned with OCP values, we introduce the design guidelines enabling this synergy. Combining storage expertise with Chemours' fluid chemistry and LiquidStack's infrastructure, we provide actionable blueprints for scaling liquid-cooled storage.

### Rack Scale Reliability Demonstration Testing of an Intel Datacenter Certified Immersion Solution
Speakers: Samantha Yates (Intel), Betsy Cossette (ExxonMobil)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9748/
Rack scale reliability demonstration testing of the complete immersion solution is critical to assess the performance and reliability of the IT hardware over the intended service lifetime to provide end user confidence the solution will meet their requirements. We will present a methodology to measure the thermochemical reliability of IT hardware at the rack scale and share test results from the Intel Datacenter Certified solution in ExxonMobil EM DC 3235. We will discuss solutions to challenges encountered during testing and present options to customize the test methodology for end user workloads and applications.

### Community Update - Immersion Fluids Workstreams Update
Speakers: Kris Fletcher (Independent), Spencer Kerns (Chevron Phillips Chemical Company)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9628/
Standardized fluid specifications are foundational to scalable, interoperable immersion cooling deployments, yet the industry lacks consensus on the fundamental parameters that define fit-for-purpose immersion fluids. The Fluid Specifications Workstream is addressing this gap through coordinated development of standard test method identification and equivalency frameworks applicable to both single- and two-phase immersion fluids. This session provides a status update on workstream progress, outlines the path toward OCP-inspired fluid product designations, and invites broader participation from fluid suppliers, system integrators, and data center operators invested in building a rigorous, vendor-neutral specification foundation for the immersion cooling industry.

### Before the Fluid: How Pre-Immersion Cleaning Chemistry Shapes Long-Term Electronic Reliability
Speakers: Chelsea Jewell (KYZEN Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9480/
Immersion cooling reliability is typically evaluated from the moment a board enters the fluid. But what happens before immersion, specifically, what cleaning chemistry was used and how completely it was removed, may be among the most consequential reliability variables in the deployment chain. This presentation reports on leakage current measurement findings showing that pre-cleaning chemistry type, residue load, and rinsing effectiveness are detectable as electrical degradation signatures in immersion environments. Boards with nominally equivalent fluid exposure and component populations showed divergent EID trajectories attributable to upstream cleaning process differences. The implications extend to procurement, inbound inspection, and fluid qualification protocols — and suggest that cleaning for immersion is a distinct process requirement.

### Moving Beyond Interchangeability: Characterizing Esters among Coolants for Immersion
Speakers: Stefan Maier (Evonik Operations GmbH), Beau Van Vaerenbergh (Oleon)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9046/
We treat immersion coolants as interchangeable although they are not entirely so. We routinely group esters and hydrocarbons into one category, yet their molecular structures drive different thermal, dielectric, and material compatibility behaviour. When properly understood, those differences can act as a true design lever for operability. This contribution puts that premise to the test through an objective, data-driven characterization of ester coolants in single-phase (1P) immersion cooling (IC) of servers, combining comparative laboratory tests with server-rig validation. We report on thermal and fire safety behaviour, dielectric performance, material compatibility limits, sustainability aspects and handling over the service life, including identified areas for further improvement. The overall aim is to create a common, evidence-based framework for selecting and standardizing 1P IC coolants.

### Fluid Oxidation and Electronic Reliability: A Multi-Fluid EID Measurement Study
Speakers: Bobby Glidwell (fluidiQ by Magnalytix), Vanja Boyd (ExxonMobil)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8996/
As single-phase immersion cooling scales across the industry, the long-term behavior of dielectric fluids under oxidative stress remains an open reliability question. This presentation reports findings from a multi-vendor, multi-fluid investigation into how oxidative aging affects electronics reliability at the board level. Using a purpose-built continuous leakage current monitoring platform, surface degradation trends were measured across test coupons submerged in fluids at varying oxidation states. Results reveal measurable electrically induced degradation signatures correlated with fluid aging across multiple fluid classes. The study provides operators and fluid manufacturers a framework for interpreting fluid health in terms of its direct impact on electronics reliability, rather than fluid chemistry alone.

### Community Update - Immersion IT Equipment (ITE) Workstreams Update
Speakers: Austin Hipes (UNICOM Engineering)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9437/
OCP Immersion Cooling updates for the IT Equipment (ITE) Committee. This presentation includes the statuses and updates for Signal Integrity, Advanced Cooling, and Design Guidelines Workstreams.

### A Practical Infrastructure Solution for Component and Device Level SSD Qualification in Single-Phase Immersion Cooling
Speakers: Jason Brand (Solidigm Technology), Justin Boldrini (Solidigm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9720/
As immersion cooling adoption grows, most infrastructure has focused on full-server deployment, while component-level qualification methods for SSDs remain limited. This session presents a specialized single-phase immersion test tank designed for SSD-only evaluation under controlled temperature conditions, including elevated fluid temperatures. It covers key engineering tradeoffs such as thermal control, fluid quality monitoring, SSD isolation, multi-fluid support, and EHS requirements for safe operation. The result is a compact, cost-effective framework that reduces fluid volume, simplifies comparative testing, and enables longer-term qualification studies without the complexity of full-server setups. Attendees will learn how component-level immersion testing can improve repeatability, accelerate qualification cycles, and support quality and reliability studies for storage devices.

### Immersion Solutions: Integrating Cooling, Power, Hardware, and Economics
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks), Kevin Gero (Murata)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9765/
Immersion cooling adoption depends on more than individual components. It requires alignment across fluids, power, hardware, safety, management, and economics. This session provides an update from the OCP Solutions Technical Committee and highlights how systems-level coordination is helping transform immersion cooling from isolated technologies into deployable infrastructure solutions. Attendees will hear how the committee coordinates multiple immersion-focused workstreams, supports OCP Marketplace product acceptance, and enables consistency across specifications, requirements, and guidance documents. The discussion will focus on practical deployment considerations, ecosystem collaboration, and how community-developed outputs are helping operators, manufacturers, and integrators accelerate immersion adoption at scale.

### Deploying Immersion Native ITE in AI NeoCloud: An OCP Collaboration Story Continued
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks), Austin Hipes (UNICOM Engineering)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9447/
This session highlights a production-scale collaboration between Denvr Dataworks and UNICOM Engineering to design and validate fully warrantied, immersion-native 3OU H100 and H200 SXM servers for sovereign AI deployments. Optimized for high-density GPU workloads in modular data centers, the platform delivers up to 800W per-server power savings and as much as 7x rack-level GPU density improvement. The presentation shares insights from 2.5 years of immersion operations, informing next-generation Blackwell deployments, including B300. Attendees will gain data-driven insights from validation through scaled public sector deployment, including thermal performance and signal integrity results for Blackwell B200/B300 HGX systems. The session also demonstrates how OCP guidance—spanning immersion requirements, Open Rack design, materials compatibility, and safe handling—was applied to reduce risk and accelerate time-to-production while strengthening ecosystem standards.

## Data Center Development Best Practices

### The Gigawatt AI Data Center Era
Speakers: Andy Bechtolsheim (Arista)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10197/
The design of next generation AI Data centers that are pushing to 1 Gigawatt and beyond is a major challenge from a power, cooling and time to market perspective. This talk discusses best practices to build such datacenters in an efficient fashion that minimizes time, resources, and the impact on the environment.

## Executive Sessions

### 224G AECs for AI Data Centers: Extending Reach, Enhancing Telemetry, and Redefining Copper Connectivity presented by Molex & Broadcom
Speakers: Clay Miller (Molex), Rajesh Annapillai (Broadcom Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10073/
This joint presentation from Broadcom and Molex will explore how next generation 224G per lane AEC solutions are enabling a new class of high-performance electrical interconnects for AI and cloud infrastructure. We will discuss the technical innovations required to support longer cable lengths, including advanced DSP architectures, optimized cable and connector design, signal integrity challenges, and co-development methodologies that enable reliable operation at 224G PAM4 data rates. The session will also highlight how embedded telemetry capabilities within modern AEC architecture provide valuable operational insights throughout deployment and production life cycles. Telemetry data can be leveraged for cable identification, health monitoring, diagnostics, power and thermal visibility, predictive maintenance, and fleet management, helping operators increase reliability while reducing operational complexity.

### AI Infrastructure validation at scale presented by Keysight
Speakers: Ram Periakaruppan (Keysight Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10104/
The explosive demand for AI introduces unprecedented challenges in infrastructure quality, interoperability, and performance. While component-level validation remains critical, true interoperability demands strict system-level conformance to industry standards. Furthermore, critical architectural bottlenecks for performance often remain hidden until infrastructure is pushed to its absolute limits under heavy, real-world application workloads. Adding to this complexity is the rapid pace of AI adoption, which compresses development cycles and forces engineering teams to manage multiple generations of accelerator projects in parallel from design to deployment. In this presentation, Keysight explores how it enables leading hyperscalers and OEMs to orchestrate large-scale system validation across the entire lifecycle, bridging the gap between Design, Pre-silicon and Post-silicon validation.

### From LPO to NPO​ to CPO​: The open optical roadmap for AI infrastructure presented by Micas Networks
Speakers: Tim Zhou (Micas Networks)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10172/
As AI cluster bandwidth scales past 51.2T, the power and latency cost of moving signals from the switch ASIC to front-panel optics has become a first-order constraint. Bringing the optics closer to silicon — from linear pluggable to near-packaged to co-packaged optics — reduces that cost, but it also changes the failure boundary, shifting serviceability, thermal design, yield, and fiber management from the module to the system. This presentation examines the architecture options along that path and the engineering and operational practices required to move from validation to repeatable deployment. Drawing on Micas system integration work and measured switch-level testing, we will explore how standard alignment, full-system qualification, software readiness, and staged adoption by risk can reduce integration risk and accelerate the delivery of power-efficient AI capacity.

### Next-Generation Power  & Cooling Solutions presented by TCT
Speakers: Royal Liu (Thermal Control Technology(Shenzhen) Co.- Ltd), Arthur Wang (XFusion)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10169/
As Al computing power density rapidly escalates, system power delivery and thermal dissipation face unprecedented challenges. Single-rack power is projected to exceed 300kW and scale toward the MW level, rendering traditional 54V distribution obsolete and driving the inevitable shift to 800V HVDC- fundamentally redefining the computing power architecture. On the thermal side, liquid cooling has become a prerequisite. Advanced liquid cooling technologies achieve over 1500W single-chip dissipation, combined with diamond-copper and diamond-ceramic cold plates, dual-chip dissipation can surpass 4000W.

### Scaling Al Factory for the Rubin Era: Advanced Liquid Cooling and End-to-End L13 Deployment presented by Pegatron
Speakers: Christopher Liang (Pegatron)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10089/
With Al architecture entering the Vera-Rubin era, single-rack power usage now exceeds an unprecedented 230kW+. This evolution transforms datacenter scaling into a challenge of gigawatt-scale Al Factory integration. This session details PGA's engineering solutions for managing these extreme densities. We will demonstrate how our end-to-end L10-L13 model aligns factory manufacturing precision with complex on-site facility standards. Participants will gain actionable insights to solve next-gen rack integration challenges and minimize time-to-market.

### Standardization that Enables Innovation: Building Deployment Confidence for Scalable Liquid Cooling presented by Danfoss
Speakers: Sébastien LAFOND (Danfoss)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10093/
Liquid cooling technology has demonstrated its ability to support the next generation of AI infrastructure. The challenge no longer cooling performance, but deployment scalability. As adoption expands across OEMs, integrators, facility operators, contractors, and technology suppliers, organizations need confidence that liquid cooling solutions can be deployed consistently and reliably at scale. This session explores how standards can help move the industry from technical feasibility to scalable deployment. Drawing on lessons learned from liquid cooling implementations and the evolving OCP ecosystem, we will examine how specification-driven interoperability, standardized practices, and product lifecycle management can reduce deployment risk and accelerate adoption.

### The Path to Infinite Inference presented by d-Matrix
Speakers: Sid Sheth (D-Matrix)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10096/
The world faces insatiable demand for AI inference as compute becomes constrained by cost, latency, and energy consumption. Today’s infrastructure was not designed to deliver intelligence at global scale. Sid Sheth, founder and CEO of d-Matrix, will lay out the path to infinite inference: AI infrastructure that is fast, efficient, and scalable. He will explain why the memory-compute boundary is a core constraint and how purpose-built architectures can bring compute directly into memory. Sid will share how heterogeneous deployments combining purpose-built inference accelerators and GPUs can achieve 10x+ speedups on disaggregated workloads, and what the industry must build across silicon, software, and open standards to make infinite inference a reality.

### AI Your Way: Compute Agnostic, Rack-Scale Architecture for AI Factories presented by Astera Labs
Speakers: Chris Petersen (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10036/
As AI expands across applications and workloads continue to evolve, hyperscalers, AI labs, and infrastructure providers face new demands. AI systems must keep scaling while supporting specialized accelerators optimized for increasingly diverse inference workloads, creating more heterogeneous deployments across multiple XPUs. A compute-agnostic, rack-scale architecture enables customers to adopt new XPUs without redesigning their entire infrastructure. By bringing an XPU into a previously developed rack infrastructure, customers can accelerate time to market and respond more quickly as workload and performance requirements change. At the same time, the shift to inference makes token economics a central measure of success. Efficient infrastructure must maximize rack utilization, maintain predictable performance, and remain reliable under real-world workloads. Every underutilized accelerator, stalled connection, or service interruption directly impacts the cost and throughput of delivered tokens. This Executive Session explores the architecture required to build flexible, efficient, and resilient AI factories—ones that support diverse compute options, keep infrastructure fully utilized, accelerate time to market, and deliver reliable performance as AI inference continues to evolve.

### Building Open Heterogeneous Infrastructure for Agentic AI presented by Intel
Speakers: Anil Nanduri (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10191/
Agentic AI is turning inference into a systems problem. Different stages of an agentic workflow require different levels of intelligence, performance, and infrastructure. Join Intel and ecosystem partners as we explore how open architectures and heterogeneous compute can match each workload to the right infrastructure, improving efficiency, scalability, and cost from silicon to rack.

### Building the AI Factory: Overcoming the Infrastructure Challenges of AI at Scale presented by Wiwynn
Speakers: Tony Wen (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10105/
As AI scales, infrastructure has become the foundation of performance and efficiency. Wiwynn will explore how innovations in power, cooling, connectivity, and rack-scale system design are addressing the challenges of building AI factories and accelerating large-scale AI deployment. Key Takeaways Infrastructure is becoming the foundation of AI performance at scale Power, cooling, and connectivity must be optimized as a unified system Optical and liquid cooling innovations are critical for future AI growth Open collaboration and ecosystem partnerships accelerate industry advancement

### From Switches to Systems presented by Jabil
Speakers: Abhinav Sharma (Arista), Matt McGuff (Jabil)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10037/
This strategic overview outlines the Arista and Jabil partnership to transform AI network infrastructure by treating the factory as an integral part of the AI architecture. It details design principles that unify switch architecture, thermal management, power distribution, and fiber routing into a single repeatable rack-level system. Operational priorities highlight automated manufacturing, liquid cooling integration, and rigorous testing strategies to ensure supply chain readiness. Ultimately, the framework emphasizes industry standardization and joint accountability to eliminate handoffs and rapidly scale high-density AI clusters.

### Scaling AI Infrastructure: The Pivotal Role of Silicon Photonics in Full-Stack Optical Interconnects presented by Genuine Optics
Speakers: Haijiang Yu (Genuine Optics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10054/
With AI training compute scaling ~100× biennially, the industry faces an urgent need for higher-speed, lower-power connectivity. Genuine Optics delivers a clear optical roadmap from 800G to 1.6T, 3.2T, and beyond, leveraging silicon photonics (SiPh) as the core scalable platform for LPO, NPO, CPO, and OIO architectures. Backed by full in-house capabilities, Genuine Optics has shipped over one million SiPh modules with field-proven FIT rates <0.2. Scaling lane rates from 100G to 400G enables 1.6T/3.2T pluggables (including 12.8T XPO) alongside next-gen 3.2T/6.4T NPO/CPO prototypes. Key innovations feature 200G/lane SiPh MZM transmitters, 400G/lane TFLN modulators with live 420–450 Gbps PAM-4 eyes, compact MUX designs, and MRM-based CPO engines for OCI-MSA and DWDM external laser sources. Genuine Optics is positioned as a reliable, high-volume partner for scaling both scale-out and scale-up AI architectures.

### Scaling Innovation for the AI Era: Keep Your Compute Running at Full Throttle with Highly Scalable Memory presented by Samsung
Speakers: Hojun Shim (Samsung Electronics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10060/
In the agentic era, it is the context, not the model, that dominates memory traffic. Bytes read per generated token have grown roughly six times in two years, while the KV cache created every month has grown sixty times, outrunning every compression technique available today. Capacity grew at every tier over the same period. None of it grew enough. This talk presents how Samsung closes that gap across three architectural pillars: Speed , moving compute to memory with custom HBM, zHBM and LPDDR-PIM Memory expansion , breaking the capacity wall with CMM-D and CXL memory pooling Persistence , adding storage to the memory hierarchy with high-bandwidth PCIe Gen6 and high-density QLC SSDs made durable by FDP Each pillar is backed by measurements on real hardware, and all three advances follow a single roadmap — the co-design path for scaling innovation in the AI era.

### The New Tokenomics: How Data Infrastructure Increasingly Sets AI ROI presented by Solidigm
Speakers: Ace Stryker (Solidigm), Steen Graham (Metrum AI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10077/
A short prompt compiles into a far larger working context, and where that data lives shapes cost-per-token, tokens-per-second, GPU utilization, and answer quality. This talk follows the ROI across the pipeline. In training, accelerators idled by data and checkpoint waits are a major cost drag. In inference, reasoning and agents broke the GPU→DRAM→SSD hierarchy; an emerging NVMe context-memory tier cuts cost (reusing context, not recomputing) and lifts value (deeper recall, more persistent agents). With power the binding constraint, every watt spent on data trades against compute. The takeaway: data placement is a day-zero ROI decision.

### Breaking the Walls: Open Scale-Across for the AI Era presented by Cisco
Speakers: Rakesh Chopra (Cisco), Tom Wiliams (Cisco), Vipul Deokar (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10082/
Generative AI has broken the assumptions of prior networks. Frontier models now demand clusters approaching 100 MW—forcing GPUs to span multiple data centers tens of kilometers apart, performing as if in the same room. This is scale-across, and it's not just faster DCI: it carries loss-intolerant, synchronous flows where one dropped packet can restart an entire AI job. In this Cisco executive session at OCP, we'll make the technical case for why this demands deep-buffered routers, proactive and reactive congestion control, and coherent optics that extend reach while freeing power for compute. We'll show why silicon, systems and optics must be co-optimized—and why open standards like OIF, OpenROADM, and OpenZR+ make this deployable at scale. Grounded in real momentum from Cisco, you'll also hear directly from customers building distributed AI infrastructure in production.

### Forging an Open Control Plane for the AI Era presented by Lattice
Speakers: Mamta Gupta (Lattice Semiconductor), Kelly Bryant (AMI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10021/
AI infrastructure is reaching a scale and complexity that traditional management models were never designed to handle. As clusters grow, power densities rise, and heterogeneous silicon becomes the norm, the challenge is no longer simply building faster systems, it is operating them securely, reliably, and efficiently at scale. The next generation of AI infrastructure will be enabled by an open control plane: the software, firmware, management frameworks, and industry standards that allow thousands of components to function as one resilient system. Lattice and AMI will share a vision for an open, interoperable control plane that advances OCP's mission of openness, efficiency, and community-driven innovation. We will explore how open standards, open source, and ecosystem partnerships can accelerate the management, security, and resilience of AI factories while reducing operational complexity.

### From grids to bits presented by BizLink
Speakers: Hendrik Coldenstrodt (BizLink Technology, Inc.), Terry WU (BizLink International Corp.,), Helmut Jungsthoefel (BizLink Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10065/
Current and future AI datacentres are pushing data rates and power consumption into uncharted territory. With 1+ MW IT racks gaining adoption, scale-up and scale-out extending data rates rapidly, liquid cooling becomes the new normal. As data transmission becomes tightly connected to the power that drives compute, the challenges to interconnect technologies are just as varied. In this session, we will explore how BizLink turns these challenges into custom solutions by combining our full spectrum of technologies. Ranging from advanced power transmission, sensing, and next-generation high-speed cables to extremely low-loss optical connections. Join us to discover how we combine highly efficient and power-dense HVDC with increased operational reliability and stability. We will also show the latest advancements in the highest data rate transmission with our solutions for NPO, CPC, and CPO.

### Inside AMD Helios™: Engineering AI at Rack Scale presented by AMD
Speakers: Andrew Dieckmann (AMD), Steve Scott (AMD), Alan Smith (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10090/
As AI workloads grow in complexity and scale, the rack becomes a critical frontier for system innovation. Join Andrew Dieckmann and AMD engineering leaders Steve Scott and Alan Smith for a fireside conversation on the thinking behind AMD Helios and how open collaboration shapes the architecture of AI infrastructure. Go inside the engineering decisions and tradeoffs that bring the rack together as one accelerated system, guided by customer requirements. The discussion will connect AMD’s approach to rack-scale design with the performance, flexibility and operational resilience customers need to advance AI at scale.

### Open Rack, Liquid Cooling, Now What?   Operationalizing the Next Gen Datacenter presented by HPE
Speakers: Joseph "JBK" Knight (Hewlett Packard Enterprise)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10062/
Power and cooling demands on infrastructure are increasing exponentially. Datacenters MUST evolve to accommodate AI and core densification deployments. Of course, the purpose of OCP projects such as "Rack and Power" and "Cooling Environments" is to create industry solutions to tackle those demands. And industry segments like CSPs and Enterprises are now embracing these solutions-- "Open Rack" and "Direct Liquid Cooling" are not just for T1 core anymore. But are operations and manageability frameworks evolving at the same pace for companies to consume these solutions? Join us for HPE insights into these lifecycle considerations and how our infrastructure products and solutions are being engineered through this lens.

### Spearheading a New Security Standard for Data Centers presented by Marvell
Speakers: Cary Ussery (Marvell), Bryan Kelly (Microsoft), Mohan Kumar (Oracle Cloud Infrastructure)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10195/
Today, most cloud and enterprise servers rely on network-attached hardware security modules (HSMs) or software-only key management. This requires cryptographic keys to traverse the network or reside in unprotected, non-FIPS 140-3 Level 3 memory, exposing sensitive keys to potential security attacks. In this session, Marvell, Microsoft and Oracle will discuss a collaborative effort to address this issue with the formation of a new OCP Working Group, which aims to define open specifications for embedding an integrated hardware security module (iHSM), secure key caching and attestation cryptographic key lifecycle management at the server level. With this new approach, keys will never leave a certified hardware boundary, strengthening the security posture of cloud compute workloads while reducing latency and improving scalability.

That evolution is bringing optics closer to compute and deeper into the rack, reshaping AI-fabric architecture. But architectural proximity alone is not enough: the next challenge is turning this vision into economically viable, operational infrastructure at scale—from silicon and systems to manufacturability, serviceability, and deployment. In this executive session, leaders from Celestica, Broadcom, and OpenAI, will examine how AI workload requirements translate into infrastructure choices spanning AI applications, systems, networking silicon, and optics. The panel breaks down what separates organizations that stand up capacity from those that convert it into value — and makes the case that the same principles driving gigawatt-scale execution are directly transferable to enterprise-scale AI deployments.

### From Dirt to Tokens: Building AI Infrastructure as an Integrated System presented by Flex
Speakers: Rob Campbell (Flex (Flextronics)), Chris Butler (Flex (Flextronics))

The leaders in AI infrastructure will be those who shorten the journey from dirt to tokens while extracting more compute from every watt. Achieving both requires a fundamentally different approach to how infrastructure is designed, integrated, and manufactured. As 800 VDC gains traction and rack power densities continue to climb, the walls between grey space and white space are coming down. Power and cooling are becoming more compact, efficient, and disaggregated from the IT rack. Success depends on: Tighter integration across compute, power and cooling design Standardized architectures that optimize system performance and efficiency Modularization and prefabrication to accelerate deployment Cross-functional collaboration across the ecosystem This session explores how a systems-level approach to designing, integrating, and manufacturing AI infrastructure is transforming one-off projects into repeatable, deployable products, enabling faster deployment and greater efficiency.

### Rethinking CPU Performance for the Agentic AI Era presented by Arm
Speakers: Ravi Malhotra (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10117/
Agentic AI is changing the role of the CPU in AI infrastructure. Agents continuously reason, invoke tools, execute code, access data and coordinate with other agents, creating very different CPU demands across applications. This session explores how to measure infrastructure performance for agentic AI. We will examine how reasoning, tool use, planning and multi-agent orchestration stress compute, memory, storage and I/O differently, and how existing agent benchmarks can be translated into repeatable infrastructure workloads. Using real-world agentic use cases, we will show why both per-core performance and rack-level throughput matter, how concurrency shifts system bottlenecks, and why traditional CPU benchmarks alone are not enough to characterize infrastructure for the agentic era.

### The Silicon Heartbeat of the AI Economy: the Efficiency Paradox in Next-Gen Data Center Silicon presented by MediaTek
Speakers: Bhushan Chitlur (MediaTek)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10029/
Generative AI has made the data center the computational heart of the global economy — and conventional silicon is hitting its limits. Scaling throughput for increasingly complex models now demands a fundamental rethink: compute, memory, interconnect, and packaging co-optimized as a unified system rather than individual components. This panel examines how XPU architectures, advanced memory, and packaging innovations from 2.5D to wafer-scale are tackling the core challenge — the "Efficiency Paradox" — where performance per watt and per TCO matter as much as raw compute. Attendees will leave with a forward-looking view of the system design strategies needed to sustain the next decade of AI infrastructure growth.

### Betting on the Network: Meta's Case for Full-Stack, Open AI Networking presented by Meta
Speakers: Omar Baldonado (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10192/
Infrastructure is increasingly the differentiator in what companies can achieve with AI. As AI models, rack-scale AI systems, and AI workload designs rapidly evolve, networking must innovate and stay ahead to enable high performance across all the different network domains (scale-up, scale-out, and scale-across). Omar Baldonado, from Meta's Network Infrastructure team, makes the case that this is a co-design problem no single company can solve alone and that open initiatives move the entire OCP ecosystem forward faster. He'll cover where Meta is placing its bets with partners across the whole networking stack, including open platforms (100T liquid-cooled, rack-scale network switches), platform guidelines (OCP OSPA), Ethernet for Scale-Up Networking (OCP ESUN), multi-planar scale-out topologies, MetaRoCE transport, and scale-across deployments.

### OSFP 2×8 Liquid Cooling I/O Solution – Enabling High-Performance Thermal Management for 1600G AI Data Center Interconnects presented by Luxshare-Tech
Speakers: Robbie Xu (Luxshare-Tech)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10080/
With the rapid advancement of AI, large-scale model training, and high-performance computing (HPC), data centers face ever-increasing demands for network bandwidth and computing density. 1600G high-speed optical modules are emerging as the mainstream solution for next-generation switch and server interconnects, which presents new thermal management challenges. As technology evolves toward 224G SerDes and beyond, the power consumption of optical modules rises; traditional air-cooling methods are approaching their technical limits, becoming a significant bottleneck for system performance improvements. Against this backdrop, liquid cooling technology is expanding from chip-level thermal management to the realm of network interconnects. The OSFP 2×8 Liquid Cooling I/O solution addresses these challenges to provide efficient, reliable liquid-cooled interconnect platforms for next-generation high-bandwidth switches and AI clusters.

### Characterization of Hose Material Effects on Coolant Chemistry During Accelerated Aging presented by Gates
Speakers: Matthew Schantz (Gates Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9024/
Material-fluid compatibility is a critical reliability factor in liquid-cooled data centers. This work evaluates chemical interactions between coolant formulations and peroxide-cured EPDM hose materials under accelerated aging conditions. Using a modified ASTM D471 methodology, hose samples were aged in coolant at 80°C for 1,344 hours to simulate long-term operation. Pre- and post-test mass measurements quantified permeation and evaporative losses, while mass spectrometry identified leached and adsorbed species relative to control samples. Results showed contamination indicators including coolant discoloration and suspended particulates, suggesting additive migration and material degradation over time. The findings help identify contamination pathways, support coolant health-monitoring strategies, and inform hose material and design improvements to enhance long-term cooling-system reliability.

### Cooling the AI Revolution: Scaling Performance Through Advanced Liquid Cooling Technologies presented by Auras
Speakers: Patrick Liu (Auras Technology Co., Ltd.), Ali Heydari (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10071/
As AI models continue to grow in scale and high-performance computing (HPC) workloads rapidly increase, the thermal design power (TDP) of GPU servers and rack power density are reaching unprecedented levels, placing greater demands on data center thermal management. This session focuses on the integrated application of advanced liquid cooling technologies, exploring how different liquid cooling solutions can work together to optimize cooling architectures, improve heat dissipation efficiency, enhance thermal management performance and system reliability, and support high-density AI computing deployments. By enabling more efficient and scalable cooling infrastructures, these technologies help build the next generation of AI data centers that deliver high performance, lower energy consumption, and sustainable scalability.

### From Chip to Grid: Scaling Holistic AI Data Center Co-Design Through Open Standards presented by Google
Speakers: Amber Huffman (Google), Tom Garvens (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10087/
AI is reshaping not just how we build servers, but how data centers interact with the grid itself. With AI architectures evolving every two years and utility transmission upgrades taking five to seven, infrastructure can no longer be a passive power consumer — it must become an active, stabilizing grid partner. We'll share how Google bridges this gap through chip-to-utility co-design, and how the open ecosystem is turning that blueprint into shared standards. We'll walk through a three-tiered architecture: silicon and software load shaping, in-rack energy storage that smooths sub-second oscillations, and facility microgrids that cap multi-megawatt ramp rates — making high-density AI data centers good grid citizens. We'll then highlight the latest OCP milestones, from 800 VDC distribution and Solid-State Transformers to modular DC power sidecars and liquid cooling, showing how open co-design turns one company's breakthroughs into the industry's path to scale.

### Innovation on Diagnosis and Maintenance of AI Datacenters presented by Aivres
Speakers: James Zou (Aivres)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10039/
As data center complexity scales exponentially, rapid and accurate hardware diagnostics are vital for maintaining high operational efficiency and minimizing downtime. This session dives into the critical challenges of addressing modern data center failures. Leveraging a detailed case study of an intermittent system-hang scenario in a large compute cluster, we analyze the structural limitations of current logging and replication methodologies. Furthermore, this talk pushes beyond reactive troubleshooting toward predictive maintenance. We propose a concrete vision for next-generation AI hardware architecture, outlining the required features—such as dual-path out-of-band register access—needed to eliminate diagnostic "black boxes." Attendees will gain actionable insights into current best practices and discover how to participate in shaping open industry standards for more resilient data center infrastructure.

### Introduction to Memory-centric AI Rack Systems (MARS) presented by SK hynix
Speakers: Hoshik Kim (SK hynix)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10045/
The explosive growth of generative AI and hyperscale data centers has pushed traditional CPU/GPU-centric architectures to severe bottlenecks, commonly known as the 'Memory Wall,' alongside soaring power consumption. This talk introduces the 'Memory-centric AI Rack System,' a revolutionary hardware paradigm designed to overcome these critical limitations. It uses Compute Express Link (CXL) technologies to connect CPUs and GPUs, enabling rack level memory pooling and dynamic, zero copy data sharing. This architectural shift drastically accelerates data movement for demanding workloads. Specifically, it eliminates data synchronization lag in collaborative Agentic AI frameworks, seamlessly serves massive context windows required by multimodal Omni-models, and resolves irregular memory access challenges in large-scale GNN training. The presentation outlines a transformative vision for future data center infrastructures.

### Powering the AI Revolution: Navigating the 800V-to-Chip Frontier presented by LITEON
Speakers: Teng Long (LITEON)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10084/
The rapid growth of AI is pushing data center power infrastructure beyond traditional limits. Modern computing chips demand thousands of amperes at ultra-low voltages (<1V), while AI racks now operate at megawatt scale. To support future demand, the industry is transitioning from 48V architectures to 800 VDC power distribution. However, efficiently converting an 800 VDC rack input to a 0.5V processor core remains a critical challenge. This keynote will explore next-generation AI power delivery architectures under evaluation by the Open Compute Project (OCP), from multi-stage approaches leveraging existing 48V/12V/6V ecosystems to highly integrated 800 VDC grid-to-chip designs. The session will highlight key enabling technologies, including advanced power topologies, wide-bandgap semiconductors, high-frequency magnetics, and compact power modules. It will also discuss architectural trade-offs and standardization efforts required to power the future of AI computing.

### XPO (eXtra Dense Pluggable Optics): A High-Density, Liquid-Cooled Optical Interconnect Architecture for Next-Generation AI Networks presented by FIT
Speakers: Terry Little (Foxconn Interconnect Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10019/
The XPO (eXtra Dense Pluggable Optics) Multi-Source Agreement (MSA) was established to create a next-generation pluggable optical interconnect architecture that combines the density advantages of co-packaged optics with the flexibility and serviceability of traditional pluggable solutions. The initiative has been strongly championed by Andy Bechtolsheim, founder of Arista Networks , whose vision for scalable AI infrastructure has been a major driving force behind the development and promotion of the XPO ecosystem. This presentation reviews the latest XPO connector, cage, cable assembly, power delivery, and thermal development activities and includes signal integrity results from FIT's live OFC 2026 demonstration. Acknowledgement: The author would like to acknowledge the leadership and vision of Andy Bechtolsheim and the contributions of the XPO MSA member companies in advancing the next generation of high-density optical interconnect technology.

### Co-Designing High-Speed Copper and Optical Interconnects: A Passive Interconnect Approach for Next-Gen AI Data Centers presented by Amphenol
Speakers: Brian Kirk (Amphenol)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10102/
Scaling AI networks beyond 102.4 Tb/s is limited not only by switch silicon, but increasingly by the interconnect fabric around it, including front I/O density, thermal load, and end-to-end latency. Retimed optical transceivers rely on DSPs that add latency and heat at high-radix switch faces. This presentation highlights passive interconnect design as a key enabler of lower-latency architectures. It surveys options from near-chip co-packaged copper (CPC) channels and passive linear-drive optical cable assemblies (LPO) to backplane interconnect strategies for copper and optical channels, showing how passive design principles apply at board-to-board and chassis levels. Using measured insertion loss, latency, and thermal/mechanical data, the presentation outlines a practical passive interconnect approach for low-latency, power-efficient multi-terabit AI infrastructure.

### Eliminate Every Obstacle Between Your Data and Your GPUs presented by Dell Technologies
Speakers: Peter Corbett (Dell Technologies), Kirankumar Bhusanurmath (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10173/
Too many AI deployments stall before they ever start — GPUs sit idle waiting for data, storage choices lock you in, and stitching together compute, networking, and storage from scratch burns months before a single model trains. Dell Technologies is changing that. The Dell AI Data Platform is modular by design, starting with a Storage Engines layer that matches the data path to the workload rather than forcing one architecture onto all of them — file, object, block, or parallel, with the throughput and low latency that keep GPUs fed and inference fast. And when you're ready to deploy at scale, Dell PowerRack brings it all together: compute, networking, and storage arriving as a single, pre-validated, factory-integrated blueprint — racked, stacked, and ready to run. See how Dell is eliminating every obstacle between your data and your GPUs.

### From Chips to Compute: Delivering AI Infra at GW Speed presented by Ingrasys
Speakers: ZongChang Liu (Foxconn)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10046/
As AI infrastructure scales rapidly, manufacturing has become a key driver of AI Factory success. In this session, Ingrasys will showcase its fully automated production line for AI servers. By combining intelligent automation, digital manufacturing, and standardized processes, Ingrasys can quickly replicate the same production model across its global factories. This enables faster deployment, scalable manufacturing, and reliable delivery of next-generation AI infrastructure. Join us to learn how automation optimization is helping Ingrasys build smarter, faster, and globally scalable AI Factories for the AI era.

### Open, Modular Rack-Scale AI Infrastructure for the Agentic Era presented by HYVE
Speakers: Dr. Dimitrious Ziakas (HYVE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9643/
The AI era is forcing the industry to rethink a fundamental assumption: what is a computer? Historically, the answer was simple: a server containing processors, memory, storage, and networking. Today, AI workloads are driving a transition toward rack-scale architectures where compute resources operate as a single integrated system. As AI continues to evolve, infrastructure architects must balance requirements across CPUs, GPUs, memory, storage, and networking, not just on component performance, but on integrating resources into flexible, rack-level architectures. This session examines how open standards and interoperable infrastructure enable the transition from server-centric to rack-scale AI systems, exploring composable infrastructure, modular compute, open networking, and disaggregated resources. The future of AI infrastructure is not defined by any single processor, but by the ability to orchestrate heterogeneous compute as a cohesive system. The rack is becoming the computer.

### Powering the AI Era: Building Sustainable and efficient Infrastructure at Cloud Scale presented by Microsoft
Speakers: Brijesh Warrier (Microsoft), Nithish Mahalingam (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10051/
The explosive growth of generative AI is driving an unprecedented increase in demand for computing infrastructure. As AI workloads scale from training to widespread inference, every layer of the cloud stack is being challenged, from power grids and datacenters to servers, silicon, and software systems. Meeting this demand sustainably requires rethinking how computing infrastructure is designed, operated, and optimized. In this talk, we will explore the emerging challenges in powering and scaling the next generation of AI infrastructure, including constraints around energy availability, power delivery, efficiency, and sustainability. We will share real-world examples of how Microsoft is addressing these challenges through innovations that span datacenter design, power-aware infrastructure, hardware and system optimization, and intelligent software-driven resource management.

### Scaling AI Inference Infrastructure from Silicon to System presented by ASRock Rack
Speakers: Charlotte Li (ASRock Rack), Jeff Chan (ASRock Rack Inc.), Sammy Huang (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10058/
As AI infrastructure evolves from large-scale training to distributed inference, organizations are looking for open, flexible platforms that accelerate deployment while improving operational efficiency. In this executive session, Arm and ASRock Rack will discuss how Arm-based AI infrastructure, together with open firmware initiatives such as OpenBMC and OpenBIOS, is enabling a more interoperable and manageable data center ecosystem. The session will explore how open platform design, modular system architecture, and ecosystem collaboration help simplify integration. Attendees will gain insights into how embracing open standards and flexible Arm-based platforms can accelerate innovation, increase infrastructure efficiency, and prepare data centers for the next generation of AI workloads.

### Validation Challenges for Next-Generation AI Power Racks: From 100kW to MW Scale presented by Chroma
Speakers: Larry Sharp (Chroma)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10057/
As AI rack power levels continue to increase, validating next-generation power infrastructure has become a major challenge for system developers, power architects, and data center operators. Building MW-scale validation environments requires more than increasing power capacity. Engineers must address thermal management, facility power constraints, high-current distribution, transient workload emulation, and automated validation. This session presents practical methodologies for AI power validation, including rack level power characterization, dynamic power excursion testing, high current interconnect design, and scalable validation architectures. It also explores Server Load Emulation to reproduce realistic AI workloads at the tray, rack, and system levels. Compared with static load testing, it provides more accurate evaluation of power delivery networks, transient response, system stability, and deployment readiness.

### Beyond Power Density: Building a Safe 800V DC Ecosystem presented by Eaton
Speakers: Joe Skorjanec (Eaton)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10097/
As AI infrastructure drives unprecedented rack densities, the industry is accelerating toward 800VDC power architectures to improve efficiency, simplify power distribution, and support future scaling requirements. While the benefits are clear, deploying higher voltage DC directly within the white space introduces new considerations for safety, operations, and serviceability. This session explores key safety considerations for 800VDC Side Power Rack and compute shelf architectures, with a focus on protecting personnel while enabling reliable operation at scale. Topics include grounding and bonding strategies, touch-safe design principles, maintenance and service procedures, arc-flash and energy isolation considerations, and a comparison of circuit protection approaches for managing DC faults and stored energy. Attendees will gain insight into the design practices and safety frameworks needed to support the deployment of 800VDC infrastructure in next-generation AI data centers.

### Dispersion Managed Transceiver for Next-Gen AI Fabrics: Cost-Effective 800G (20km) & 1.6T (10km) Interconnects  for Scale-Out, Scale-Across, and OCS SuperPOD Architectures presented by Linktel
Speakers: Grace Zhang (Linktel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10048/
Driven by exponential scaling of large AI training clusters, hyperscale data centers adopt three critical optical fabric architectures: rack-level Scale Out expansion, multi-building campus Scale Across GPU pooling, and OCS-based all-optical SuperPOD switching fabrics. Conventional IMDD CWDM transceivers at 800G and 1.6T face fundamental chromatic dispersion bottlenecks at high baud rates. Standard 1.6T CWDM modules only support transmission under 3 km, while mainstream 800G CWDM variants cap at 10 km reach. This work introduces a family of dispersion-managed pluggable CWDM transceivers fully aligned with universal commercial CWDM wavelength grids, realizing two key product configurations: 1.6T 2FR4-10km and 800G 2LR4+ 20km. We elaborate the core working mechanism of the dispersion management scheme and demonstrate comprehensive lab characterization results covering Tx eye,RX BER waterfall, and switch level FEC BIN.

### Every Megawatt Counts: The Path to Building AI Factories with DSX MaxLPS presented by NVIDIA
Speakers: Ajay Kamalvanshi (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10193/
Power, not silicon, now sets the ceiling on AI factory revenue. Yet measurements across mixed production workloads — inference, training, and post-training RL — show that a large share of provisioned datacenter power is never drawn. Racks are nonetheless provisioned against worst-case per-GPU settings, so operators energize, cool, and pay for capacity that is always underutilized. MaxLPS recovers that stranded capacity through closed-loop dynamic allocation and iso-performance perf/watt optimization, letting the same megawatt support significantly more racks. The perf/watt techniques applied include per-workload and per-silicon power settings, runtime deep-learning profiling, GPU-to-CPU and GPU-to-NVSwitch power steering, and inference-framework-aware steering across prefill, attention, and decode stages. We will present measured results for inference, the thermal and facility co-design that makes higher inlet temperatures practical, and provisioning guidance expressed in the unit operators actually plan against: GPUs per megawatt. We will also cover the MaxLPS interfaces that are aligned with OCP specifications.

### Joint Session: Inventec Autonomous AI Factory Agents powered by NVIDIA, presented by Inventec
Speakers: JK Jung (Inventec Corporation), Alvin Clark (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10110/
Inventec Corporation and NVIDIA showcase how the NVIDIA Factory Operations Blueprint (FOX) turns raw electronics manufacturing data into real-time operational decisions. Inventec demonstrates production-ready FOX applications for complex server and notebook assembly, followed by the NVIDIA Defect Image Generation skill, which creates synthetic data for rare defects. In laptop cosmetic-defect inspection, more than 10,000 synthetic images helped reduce data collection and manual labeling effort by about 30%. Inventec also presents its Observation Agent, an edge vision AI solution running on NVIDIA Jetson Orin™ and powered by NVIDIA Cosmos™. Demonstrations include sensor-less cycle-time tracking, layout anomaly alerts, SOP motion-compliance verification, and automated root-cause analysis without manual video tagging.

### Multi-Dimensional Scalability for AI infrastructures: From Model Creation to Global AI Service Delivery presented by Nokia
Speakers: Clayton Wagar (Nokia)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10076/
AI does not end at the training cluster. Delivering AI at scale requires a new network architecture that spans model creation, service delivery, and distributed inferencing. This presentation explores how AI infrastructures are evolving from isolated GPU clusters into interconnected AI Grids that function as a unified system. We will discuss the role of high-performance Ethernet fabrics, advanced optical networking, AI-aware routing, and distributed edge architectures in enabling AI services to move seamlessly from centralized training environments to users around the world. This presentation introduces a framework for multi-dimensional scalability in AI networks , encompassing scale-up , scale-out , scale-across , and the emerging network that needs to support the emerging distributed architecture. This network is required to extend AI services across a distributed hierarchy of edge locations, creating AI Grids that place intelligence closer to users, devices, and data sources.

### Solving Power Density and Bandwidth Challenges in AI Infrastructure presented by TE Connectivity
Speakers: Gaurav Jain (TE Connectivity), Ram Kasiraman (TE Connectivity)

### Advancing Open AI Infrastructure Management through Community and Ecosystem Collaboration - From Platform Firmware to Open Rack and POD Management presented by MiTAC
Speakers: Hancock Chang (MiTAC), James Shie (MiTAC)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10047/
As AI infrastructure scales from standalone servers to rack- and POD-level deployments, managing compute, networking, power, and liquid cooling through open, interoperable technologies becomes increasingly critical. This session explores how the OCP community and ecosystem are advancing AI infrastructure management through open standards and collaboration. The session highlights how Open Platform Firmware (OPF), including openSFI and UPCI, provides a common firmware foundation, while OpenBMC and Redfish enable standardized and secure platform management. It also discusses how open rack management supports POD-scale operations. Attendees will gain practical insights into how these open building blocks simplify deployment, telemetry, firmware lifecycle management, and infrastructure operations, improving interoperability, accelerating ecosystem adoption, and enabling the next generation of open AI infrastructure management.

### Scaling Direct Current in Data Centers:  From Proven Industrial Concepts to Open Standardization presented by Phoenix Contact
Speakers: Martin Wetter (Phoenix Contact)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10075/
This session emphasizes that the key barrier to scaling DC in data centers is not technology readiness, but the lack of globally aligned concepts and standards. Establishing common frameworks for voltage levels, protection schemes, and system architectures is essential to ensure safety, interoperability, and scalability across the ecosystem. To demonstrate the practical feasibility of DC technology, a detailed industrial application will be presented. Real-world implementation experiences highlight operational performance, key technological challenges, and proven solutions. Special focus is given to protection concepts, fault management, and system integration, providing attendees with valuable insights into the safe, efficient, and scalable deployment of DC systems.

### The AMI Vision for Open: Building an Open Control Plane for the AI Data Center Era presented by AMI
Speakers: Munir Ahmad (Lattice Semiconductor), B Parthiban (AMI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10106/
As AI infrastructure scales, operators face a growing challenge to maintain control across increasingly complex, heterogeneous and rapidly evolving data center environments. Success in the AI era will depend on establishing a trusted, scalable and open control plane for the entire infrastructure lifecycle. AMI believes that open-source collaboration among silicon providers, CSPs, ODMs, and the OCP community is essential to managing AI infrastructure at scale. With deep expertise spanning firmware, platform management, and lifecycle operations, AMI is uniquely positioned to help address the industry's growing control plane challenge. This session will outline how AMI's vision for one Open Control Plane can provide an open, standards-based foundation that unifies management across diverse platforms and vendors, delivering consistent visibility, security, lifecycle governance, and operational control for modern AI data centers.

### Wider. Slower. Better: Optics That Can Beat Copper presented by Credo
Speakers: Bill Brennan (Credo)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10170/
Copper has earned its place in AI infrastructure by being reliable, power-efficient, and cost-effective, and for years that combination has been hard to beat. But as clusters scale to hundreds of thousands of accelerators, copper's reach limits and the growing power and complexity of today's optical alternatives are forcing hard tradeoffs on everyone building at scale. In this executive session, Credo CEO Bill Brennan will share a fundamentally different approach to optical connectivity, one that rethinks the conventional wisdom on how to move data between AI systems. He will examine how wide-and-slow optical solutions will answer the call for high-density connectivity over the next 10 years in AI infrastructure.
