This session explores how modular microgrids accelerate time to power, lower carbon intensity, and unlock new value for operators by combining onsite generation, renewables, energy storage, and advanced controls in a single system. Modular microgrids are becoming increasingly important as AI workloads drive unprecedented power challenges and relying on the grid alone brings new limitations.

### From Silicon to Rack: Arm and Meta Scaling AI Infrastructure Through OCP presented by Arm
Speakers: Prasanna Jayaraman (Arm), Damien Chong (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10116/
AI infrastructure is evolving from individual components to tightly engineered systems, making open standards critical to bringing new compute platforms to deployment at scale. In this session, Arm and Meta will share how they are collaborating through OCP to enable the next generation of AI infrastructure, from silicon to rack. Arm will discuss its contribution of the Arm AGI CPU reference server design to OCP, establishing an open foundation for systems built on Arm’s first production data center CPU. Meta will share how that foundation extends into Yosemite V5, its next-generation rack architecture incorporating AGI CPU. Together, the companies will explore how open specifications, reference designs and system-level contributions can reduce integration complexity, accelerate ecosystem development and create a repeatable path from new silicon to rack-scale deployment.

### MetaRoCE: Rethinking RDMA for the AI presented by Meta
Speakers: Arvind Srinivasan (Meta), Sandeep Nagaraja (Meta), Balakrishnan Raman (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10079/
The network is now in the critical path of every AI GPU connectivity—and the RDMA we've trusted for a decade was never built for it. As GPU clusters scaled from hundreds to tens of thousands of accelerators, RoCEv2 began to crack on a lossless fabric and tail latency directly wastes a multi-billion-dollar compute fleet. Meta's answer was not another round of tuning, but a clean-sheet transport. MetaRoCE is a multipath, out-of-order, receiver-driven RDMA protocol that treats Ethernet as lossy by design and pushes all intelligence into the NIC. Meta is now opening the MetaRoCE specification to the broader industry. Come see how RDMA was reinvented to keep tens of thousands of GPUs running as one.

### Powering AI Infrastructure: Innovations in Compute, Power, Connectivity and Cooling presented by Wiwynn
Speakers: Lentis Pai (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10109/
Building AI at scale requires more than powerful accelerators. As AI clusters grow in size and density, power, cooling, and connectivity have become critical to performance, efficiency, and deployment. In this session, Wiwynn will showcase innovations across the AI infrastructure stack, from advanced AI platforms and liquid cooling technologies to next-generation optical interconnect solutions. Attendees will gain insights into how a system-level approach across compute, power, cooling, and connectivity is helping enable scalable and efficient AI infrastructure for the AI Factory era, and learn more about Wiwynn's latest whitepaper and technical workshops driving industry collaboration and innovation.

### LITEON 1MW Power Rack Solution for AI Data Center presented by LITEON
Speakers: Terry Zhang (LITEON)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10083/
This session introduces LITEON's >1MW 800 VDC Power Rack Design, which will be used as the next generation GPU Power solution with high-density and efficient distribution. The Sub-Modules include PDU/Power Shelf/BBU/CBU as well as the DC-DC conversion (HVDC to 50V/12V) on IT rack will be introduced, along with the key performance of each Module.

### Tiering the Agent’s Memory: From CXL Pooling to SSD-Backed KV Cache Offloading presented by Samsung
Speakers: Youngha Choi (Samsung), Jinin So (Samsung Electronics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10103/
The rise of agentic AI and long‑context, fleet‑scale workloads is inflating KV‑cache requirements beyond the capacity of traditional DRAM and HBM resources. Consequently, the ability to scale inference now hinges more on an intelligently designed memory‑storage hierarchy than on raw compute power. Samsung’s unified strategy, “KV‑Cache Offloading to CXL & Storage,” combines a CXL‑based heterogeneous memory pool that aggregates capacity across the entire agent fleet with high‑density SSDs (e.g., PM1763, E3.S QLC BM1773) that serve as an SSD‑backed KV cache. By moving computation to the memory controller and relaxing durability guarantees for KV entries, this approach delivers higher IOPS, lower latency, and improved DWPD, eliminating the stalls that fixed per‑node designs suffer. Integrating CXL 3.0 memory, PCIe Gen6 TLC SSDs, and high‑capacity QLC SSDs in OCP‑aligned form factors yields a seamless hierarchy that extends an agent’s working memory far beyond a single node.

### From Factory Floor to AI Factory: Deploying Intel® Xeon® 6 AI Infrastructure at Scale presented by Lenovo
Speakers: James McFadden (Lenovo), David Hanley (Lenovo), David Lee (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10099/
As AI infrastructure deployments grow in scale and complexity, getting systems into production quickly and reliably is more important than ever. Join Lenovo and Intel to explore how AI infrastructure powered by Intel® Xeon® 6 processors is built, integrated, and validated at the rack level before it arrives at the data center. Through real-world liquid-cooled and air-cooled deployment examples, learn how factory-based integration, testing, and quality processes help reduce deployment risk, accelerate time to production, and ensure systems are ready to roll directly into service upon arrival.

### Going Wide: Scalable and Reliable Optical Interconnects for AI Clusters presented by Credo
Speakers: LK Bhupathi (Credo)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10178/
Can we make optical interconnects as reliable as copper and meet the needs of AI infrastructure as cluster-sizes grow over the next decade? This talk makes the case for going wide: many parallel lanes at optimal speeds, delivering the increasing throughput and radix with simpler electronics and optics, lower energy per bit, and greater robustness. We consider how going wide shifts the reliability equation by reducing the equalization burden that high-rate links demand while allowing for spare lanes. Drawing on Credo's ZeroFlap technology, we discuss how distributed telemetry and intelligent optics deliver energy efficiency and scalability alongside a 100x improvement in MTBI (Mean Time Between Interruptions).

### Agentic AI: The Agentic Rack, Powered by Intel Xeon Processors presented by Intel
Speakers: Jatin Upadhyay (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10179/
Agentic AI workloads are changing the hardware requirements of AI infrastructure. While model inference remains important, orchestration, tool execution, retrieval, data movement, and state management increasingly determine overall performance, placing new demands on CPU architecture. This session explores how Intel is approaching agentic AI infrastructure with Intel Xeon processors, built-in AI acceleration, memory expansion technologies, and integrated data accelerators that help improve throughput, efficiency, and scalability for real-world agentic deployments. Using emerging rack-scale designs as examples, the presentation will examine how balanced compute, memory, connectivity, and acceleration can enable the next generation of open AI infrastructure and highlight areas where industry collaboration can further advance interoperable agentic AI systems.

### From Fleet to Firmware: Bringing AI-Native Security to Open Hardware with Project Equilibrium presented by Google
Speakers: Andres Lagar-Cavilla (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10088/
Just as AI is transforming how we design infrastructure, it is redefining how we must defend it. Open compute systems rest on millions of lines of critical low-level code across OpenBMC, Caliptra, CXL, and NVMe — and as frontier AI accelerates vulnerability discovery, periodic audits and noisy static scans can no longer keep up. Here, we show intelligence turned on itself: how we use AI to continuously secure the very systems that run AI. We'll share Google's experience with Project Equilibrium, an AI-native security architecture that pairs live, repo-colocated threat models with autonomous pre-submit scanning and automated patching — catching deep bugs and slashing false positives before code ever merges. On that foundation, we'll explore how to extend this secure-by-default workflow from hyperscale fleets to bare-metal firmware, using open security cookbooks that turn internal defense breakthroughs into a shared baseline for the entire open hardware ecosystem.

### Hyperscale Storage Solutions for NeoClouds presented by Everpure
Speakers: Prathamesh Nalawade (Everpure, Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10059/
Neoclouds racing to put scarce GPUs to work need storage that keeps pace as they grow. At scale, every percent of efficiency gained becomes real dollars. Hyperscalers capture that efficiency by running their own scale-out storage software on commodity data nodes, and Neoclouds can now follow the same path with OCP-aligned hardware. Everpure's hyperscale architecture builds on our DirectFlash foundation by moving flash management from the drive into host software. This approach enables deployment optimizations traditional in-drive designs can't offer. With flash intelligence at the host, efficiency becomes a property of the whole system, not of each individual drive. In this session you will learn how Neoclouds can reach hyperscale-grade storage efficiency and flexibility without hyperscale-sized engineering teams.

### Scaling AI Compute Fabrics: The Pivotal Role of NPO in Full-Stack Optical Interconnects
Speakers: Haijiang Yu (Genuine Optics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10181/
With AI training compute scaling ~100× biennially, the industry faces an urgent need for higher-speed, lower-power connectivity. Genuine Optics delivers a clear optical roadmap from 800G to 1.6T, 3.2T, and beyond, leveraging silicon photonics (SiPh) as the core scalable platform for LPO, NPO, CPO, and OIO architectures. Backed by full in-house capabilities, Genuine Optics has shipped over one million SiPh modules with field-proven FIT rates <0.2. Scaling lane rates from 100G to 400G enables 1.6T/3.2T pluggables (including 12.8T XPO) alongside next-gen 3.2T/6.4T NPO/CPO prototypes. Key innovations feature 200G/lane SiPh MZM transmitters, 400G/lane TFLN modulators with live 420–450 Gbps PAM-4 eyes, compact MUX designs, and MRM-based CPO engines for OCI-MSA and DWDM external laser sources. Genuine Optics is positioned as a reliable, high-volume partner for scaling both scale-out and scale-up AI architectures.

### How can high-speed interconnect solutions drive next generation AI data centres at 448G and beyond? presented by BizLink
Speakers: Hendrik Coldenstrodt (BizLink Technology, Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10066/
While next-generation AI data centres are pushing the limits of every key component, increasing channel bandwidth becomes nearly exponentially more challenging. In this talk, we will discuss various options for increasing data rates to 448G, using copper and optical solutions for plugables, as well as co- and near-packaged solutions. We will cover, for example, bandwidth extension, changed modulation, and the implications on cable flexibility and characterization. We will also show that high-performance optical patch cords can improve channel performance in plugables and CPO systems alike.

### Scaling the Future of Open Infrastructure with Advanced Cooling presented by MiTAC
Speakers: Raymond Huang (MiTAC)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10072/
Driven by the rapid rise of Agentic AI, the next era of computing demands unprecedented scale and thermal management. Join MiTAC Computing alongside industry pioneers to explore how we are scaling the future of open infrastructure to support the next-generation rack-scale platform. This session delves into the hyperscale evolution of high-density AI systems, showcasing next-level liquid cooling enhancements that maximize thermal efficiency to unlock ultimate compute performance .

### Enabling an Open Near-Package Ecosystem for AI Scale-Up presented by Molex
Speakers: Bill Wilson (Molex)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10064/
AI scale-up architectures are entering a transition zone where copper remains critical at short reach while optics becomes increasingly necessary inside the box. Near-Package Optics (NPO) is emerging as a practical approach to improve efficiency, density, and scalability while preserving serviceability and ecosystem flexibility. Building on work underway within the OCP Short Reach Optical Interconnect (SROI) community, this session explores how open interface definitions and interoperable integration approaches can reduce ecosystem fragmentation and enable greater architectural choice. Attendees will see how Molex technologies including Mirror Mezz Enhanced, Gemini Mezz™, CuOperate™, and Si-Fly HD CPX support the evolution toward open, scalable, near-package interconnect ecosystems for next-generation AI infrastructure.

### Engineering the Transition to 800 VDC presented by MEGMEET
Speakers: Ted Shi (Megmeet)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10115/
As AI rack power continues to scale toward 100 kW and beyond, conventional AC-based power architectures face growing challenges in efficiency, power density, scalability, and system integration. This session explores the transition to 800 VDC power architectures for next-generation AI infrastructure, covering rack-level sidecar solutions, air- and liquid-cooled power shelves, cluster-level power centers, and emerging solid-state transformer approaches. The presentation will compare different deployment options, discuss their engineering tradeoffs and application scenarios, and share practical considerations for building scalable, high-density power systems for future AI data centers.

### Open, Trusted, and Secure SONiC at Scale presented by Cisco
Speakers: Will Eatherton (Cisco), Dina Papagiannaki (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10085/
As SONiC expands beyond hyperscale data centers into AI infrastructure and enterprise environments, trust and security are essential to adoption and operational success. Trust begins with FIPS-ready hardware and a platform root of trust that validates hardware, firmware, and software integrity across the system lifecycle. This extends to confidential networking, protecting sensitive data and network state, and runtime security that monitors and safeguards live systems. SONiC's end-to-end security framework combines supply-chain integrity, verified software authenticity, secure build pipelines, and Day-2 monitoring. Integrated CVE intelligence and vulnerability databases provide risk visibility, while eBPF-based runtime protections enable rapid mitigation and live patching without reboots or service disruption: protect immediately, apply permanent patches during a planned window, and revalidate. Join us to discover how SONiC remains open, trusted, resilient, and secure at scale.

### One Abstraction, Many Transports: Push-Mode Communication from Chiplet to Rack presented by d-Matrix
Speakers: Sai Rahul Chalamalasetti (d-Matrix)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10095/
SRAM-based accelerators are turning low-latency inference into a practical reality, and 3D- stacked DRAM extends that model to far larger memory capacity and bandwidth. As models outgrow any single accelerator, the system-level question of how to partition and connect them now dominates inference performance. Interfaces and protocols built for yesterday's workloads don't scale to modern inference; wrapping a highly capable accelerator in legacy interconnect quickly becomes the bottleneck. At d-Matrix, we've built a communication model that stays consistent to software even as the underlying transport changes, whether a message crosses a chiplet, a package, a node, or a rack, and even as the accelerator changes underneath it. This talk shows how push-mode streaming communication over Ethernet, between homogeneous or heterogeneous nodes, underpins the disaggregation techniques shaping modern inference, including heterogeneous AFD with GPUs. We cover how JetStream, our network interface for low-latency inference clusters built around Corsair, enables this today, and how the same model carries forward to Raptor, our 3D-DRAM- based accelerator, through NVL-based scale-up and RDMA scale-out.

### Evolving the Infrastructure Stack: Rack Scale Designs, Open Firmware, and Intelligent Sustainability presented by AMI
Speakers: Zachary Bobroff (AMI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10107/
The data center industry is at an inflection point, and open platform firmware is at the center of the transformation. As infrastructure demands grow in complexity and scale, open-source firmware is emerging as a powerful catalyst to accelerate development cycles, enhance security visibility and sustainability efforts, and foster deep collaboration across the ecosystem. This session explores how open firmware initiatives are reshaping the foundation of modern data center platforms, from boot and manageability to security and telemetry. Attendees will gain the unique perspective from AMI as an operator across the data center stack and understand how it is contributing to and building on open firmware frameworks to unlock greater agility, drive interoperability, reduce environmental impacts and fuel the kind of community-led innovation that is pushing the boundaries of what modern infrastructure can do.

### Direct Medium-Voltage-to-800VDC Conversion for AI Data Centers presented by Luxshare-Tech
Speakers: Jingkui Shi (Luxshare‑Tech and Zinsight Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10081/
As AI data centers move toward higher rack power and 800VDC distribution, facility-level power conversion is being re-evaluated. This session explores direct medium-voltage-to-800VDC conversion, drawing on development experience from 13.8-kV and 35-kV solid-state transformer platforms. The discussion will focus on practical design considerations, including medium-voltage insulation, modular scaling, thermal management, fault containment, and serviceability. It will conclude with hardware development lessons and considerations for integrating SSTs into future 800VDC data center power architectures.

### Water or Glycol for D2C? A Quantitative Tradeoff Analysis Across Thermal, Operational, and Programmatic Domains presented by Ecolab
Speakers: Dolly Batra (Ecolab)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10050/
Selecting a coolant for direct-to-chip (D2C) liquid cooling is no longer a routine specification; it’s a strategic lever. As AI and HPC deployments drive higher rack densities and chip power, coolant choice can significantly impact system performance, reliability, and operating costs. This session will compare treated water and PG25 coolants in single-phase D2C loops using real-world engineering criteria, including heat transfer, pumping requirements, microbial risk, regulatory exposure, and operating cost drivers. We will present a practical decision framework that helps operators, designers, and engineers determine which fluid is best suited for their D2C deployment based on long-term operational goals. This framework can help reduce risk while optimizing cooling performance at scale and where the industry's default assumptions are starting to break down.

### The Changing Course of Storage in an AI World presented by FADU
Speakers: Young Paik (FADU)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10111/
The pattern of SSD storage was well established in 2024. For years new generations of SSDs had been methodically planned, designed, and released with a steady-predictable pattern. Each generation of NAND was planned in strict accordance with limits set with the appropriate PCIe lanes. This pattern was easy to understand: double PCIe bandwidth, double SSD performance. Double SSD performance, double SSD capacity. The systems based on top of the SSDs scaled in a similarly predictable way. The advent of AI has thrown a monkey wrench into all that predictability. Suddenly there are new compute units (mainly GPUs) with wildly different SSD access needs. Servers and racks need to be totally redesigned. This talk will go over the the different workloads needed by AI systems. We will cover the characteristics of these systems and offer suggestions for what to look for in terms of specs.

### Extending Copper for Efficient Next-Generation Scale-Up Networks presented by Ciena
Speakers: Matt Bolig (Ciena)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10028/
For decades, copper has been the preferred interconnect medium for short-reach connectivity, offering broad availability, low cost, simple infrastructure, and minimal supply-chain risk. But as data rates continue to increase, the practical reach of passive copper is shrinking—challenging its use in next-generation scale-up networks. This session explores how active copper cables (ACCs) can extend the reach of copper while preserving many of its inherent advantages. Unlike active electrical cables based on integrated retimers, ACCs use linear amplification to compensate for channel loss with a fraction of the power, zero added latency and reduced implementation complexity. We will examine the design trade-offs behind ACCs and how they can enable high-bandwidth, power-efficient connectivity for next-generation scale-up architectures.

### A Pathway to 224/448 Gbps Copper and Optical Connectivity in AI Infrastructure presented by Samtec
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10043/
AI Scale-up and Scale-out infrastructures demand both reduced power consumption, cost, and latency and increased bandwidth density, capacity, reliability and scalability. Interchangeability between copper and optical interconnects simplifies system architectures and offers flexibility in scale-up/scale-out implementations. Field replaceable CPO solutions are a must for AI scale-up/scale-out as well. In this Expo Hall Session presentation, technical experts from Samtec will detail the Si-Fly® HD 224 Gbps PAM4 CPX Systems supporting electrically pluggable CPC/CPO solutions. In additional, presenters will detail the benefits of an interoperable ecosystem of optical engines enabling various AI scale-up/scale-out use cases.

### Any Rack, Everywhere: Future-Readiness for the 800 VDC Transition presented by Vertiv
Speakers: Scott Armul (Vertiv)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10108/
The shift to 800 VDC is settled in direction but not in endpoint or path, and most operators don't know what IT loads they will need to serve. Future-ready means moving to ultra-high-density racks and some mix of 800 VDC without tearing apart the building or stranding resources. This session presents a framework built on two types of design decisions: hard-to-change infrastructure sized for future loads on day one, and flexible interfaces that let operators change what connects without rip-and-replace. It covers: 1) Setting a credible planning envelope for density and power 2) Four real-world transition scenarios, from a few DC racks in an AC pod to full DC conversion 3) Possibilities at the edge of what we know The session concludes with a simple formula: future-ready equals hard-to-change infrastructure sized for future loads on day one, plus flexible interfaces that simplify the transition and provide options. Minimize stranded capacity. Minimize wasted capital. Minimize disruption.

### Fanless Servers Are Coming: The Peripheral Coldplate Roadmap Behind 100% Heat Capture
Speakers: Brad Zakaib (CoolIT Systems)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10092/
Fanless servers are arriving and will reshape how operators design for density, efficiency and uptime. Achieving this requires liquid cooling to extend beyond CPUs and AI accelerators to every major heat-generating component in the server. In this session, CoolIT outlines the roadmap to 100% heat-capture architectures from coldplate designs for networking optics, storage and memory to integrated server-level cooling. The session provides system architects and component designers with a view of how 100% heat-capture-to-liquid server designs will unlock in the next era of AI infrastructure.

### Burning Cash: The Hidden Cost of Ignoring Thermal in Your AI Strategy presented by Frore
Speakers: Seshu Madhavapeddy (Frore Systems)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10091/
Every AI roadmap accounts for chips, power, and capital, but few account for the line item quietly burning through all three: HEAT. As GPU power draw climbs past 3000 watts and AI Factories scale into gigawatt power territory, the gap between the data center’s true capability and what it actually delivers under thermal constraints has become a material concern, not an engineering footnote. This session traces that gap from chips to the boardroom, showing what becomes possible when thermal is treated as a fundamental design criteria instead of an afterthought: higher GPU AI Token throughput, optimal data center power efficiency and improved AI Factory return-on-Investment (RoI). Attendees will leave with a practical framework for evaluating thermal design choices in their own infrastructure decisions, and a sharper sense of why the next competitive edge in AI comes down to who can keep their chips cool enough to win the AI race.

### Powering Progress: Murata’s Role in Advancing OCP Innovation presented by Murata
Speakers: Kevin Gero (Murata), Miguel Millan Montanes (Murata)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10094/
As Open Compute Project (OCP) architectures evolve, so does the demand for power solutions that are more efficient, scalable, and future-ready. In this session, Murata will explore its contributions to OCP innovation – highlighting key developments across ORV2, ORV3, and the High-Power Rack (HPR) initiatives. We’ll outline the progression from 12V and 50V power delivery in ORV2 to the standardized 50V architecture in ORV3 and examine how Murata is engineering solutions that align with OCP’s aggressive performance and efficiency standards. The session will also touch on power delivery in immersion-cooled environments, including foundational insights into how OCP is shaping power strategies for high-density compute applications. Join us to learn how Murata continues to support and accelerate the future of open hardware innovation .

### Multi-Core Fiber - A solution for scale up, out, and across presented by Sumitomo Electric
Speakers: Dwayne Holst (Sumitomo Electric Lightwave)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10061/
Presentation will be focused around the latest on multi-core fiber (MCF) development. Intro: What is multi-core fiber and how does it work What problems does MCF fiber solve (e.g. rack level fiber congestion issues) Solution: Illustrate latest on MCF products, and illustrations reduction in congestion/space saving within racks and pathways. Timing: When it will be required for scale up/scale out segments of the data center due to congestions within rack.

### Liquid-cooling Approaches for E1.S SSDs presented by KIOXIA
Speakers: Paul Wu (KIOXIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10185/
As AI and hyperscale systems are looking to increase SSD capabilities in terms of performance and density, thermal management for E1.S storage is becoming more critical. This session will discuss liquid-cooling approaches for E1.S SSDs and how it can help to maintain stable SSD performance under heavy workloads. The presentation will cover KIOXIA’s industry collaboration through OCP activities and alignment with SNIA® SFF-TA-1006 rev 2.0 specifications to support thermal cold plate solution in Nvidia® reference design platforms. We will also demonstrate a liquid-cooling SSD design to show device level improvements compliance with this standard.

### FUJITSU-MONAKA: Optimized for High Performance and Energy Efficiency for AI, presented by Fujitsu
Speakers: Masato Fukumori (Fujitsu)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10074/
Fujitsu has decades of experience developing processors and computing systems across mainframes, UNIX servers, and high-performance computing. FUJITSU-MONAKA builds on this engineering experience to address the growing demands of AI and cloud infrastructure. Optimized for high performance and energy efficiency, FUJITSU-MONAKA combines a many-core Arm architecture with Fujitsu technologies including 3D chiplet design and Ultra-Low Voltage technology. This session will introduce the technologies behind FUJITSU-MONAKA, its approach to security and open data-center integration, and AI inference performance demonstrated on the FUJITSU-MONAKA reference platform.

### Driving Efficiency Through Innovation Across the Operating Range and Power Chain, Aligning Power Architecture With the Needs of AI presented by Omnion Power
Speakers: Philip Zuk (OmniOn Power)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10186/
AI workloads are driving higher rack power, power density, and thermal demands. Peak efficiency matters, but AI loads move across dynamic profiles, so thermal performance depends on efficiency across the operating range, not a single best-case point. This presentation examines why small differences in weighted efficiency matter across the power chain, from single- or three-phase AC/DC input through DC/DC conversion to near-GPU voltage. It considers how Si, SiC, and GaN devices reduce conduction, switching, gate-drive, and crossover losses, and how device selection can yield more than one high-performance solution. It then focuses on high-density 48 V-to-12 V onboard power delivery. Using competitive benchmarking and OmniOn's Osprey DC/DC portfolio, it shows how higher operating-range efficiency can reduce heat load, improve cooling headroom and reliability, and strengthen AI data center economics.

### Changing workloads demand Active Compute Fabric presented by Cornelis Networks
Speakers: Jayraj Nair (Cornelis Networks)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10187/
AI infrastructure no longer serves one dominant workload. Disaggregated inference, mixture-of-experts models and agentic pipelines now run alongside large-scale training, and each one stresses the network differently. They bring bursty all-to-all traffic, latency-sensitive collectives, and constant KV-cache movement between prefill and decode. When the fabric is just a passive pipe, GPUs sit idle and every token costs more. This session introduces Active Compute Fabric, which moves communication offload and collective computation into the network itself. Using real scenarios such as KV-cache transfer, MoE token dispatch and collective-heavy training, we'll show where an active fabric cuts TTFT latency, raises accelerator utilization and keeps clusters adaptable as workloads continue to change.

### Fungible Power Architectures for the 800V DC Transition presented by Edged Infrastructure
Speakers: James Feasey (Edged Infrastructure), Wes Short (Edged Infrastructure)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10188/
The transition to 800VDC at the AI rack creates a design problem: power infrastructure must be committed today while wider compute system and facility distribution requirements remain uncertain. Legacy fixed-function power electronics force operators to lock electrical topologies and equipment ahead of definitive distribution requirements. As native 800VDC arrives in stages — vendor by vendor, system by system — those early commitments carry costs: conversion losses, stranded equipment, redesigned one-lines, construction delays, and lost time-to-compute. This session presents an alternative: fungible power architectures that late-bind to power requirements as they evolve. Edged Infrastructure will describe a power fabric built on multi-port energy routers, solid-state transformers, and on-site energy systems — a stack that dynamically reconfigures AC and DC distribution across the rack, data hall, and facility without redesign or extensive rip-and-replace of one-line components. Attendees will learn the design principles behind late-binding power topologies, how a single architecture accommodates mixed AC/DC deployments during the transition, and a practical migration path to native 800VDC.

### Building AI Infrastructure Faster: Prefabricated Modular Liquid-Cooled Data Centers presented by BEEHE
Speakers: Kent Pang (BEEHE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10190/
As AI infrastructure grows in scale and power density, traditional data center deployment faces increasing challenges in construction timelines, system integration, and scalability. This session will explore how prefabricated modular liquid-cooled data centers can accelerate deployment by integrating power, cooling, controls, and IT infrastructure into factory-built and pre-tested modules. The presentation will also introduce flexible architectures ranging from compact all-in-one systems to standard and custom multi-module solutions for large-scale AI and HPC deployments, highlighting improvements in deployment speed, scalability, reliability, and overall project execution.

## FTI Workshop: Data Centric Computing

### Deep dive into what allows 1 million GPUs talk to thousands of smart devices
Speakers: Pankaj Mehra (The Ohio State University), Galen Shipman (Los Alamos National Laboratory), Anand Jayarajan (NVIDIA), Evangelos Georganas (Intel), Atharva Chougule (Stanford University)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10273/

### Beyond the Monolith: Open Middleware for Heterogeneous AI Infrastructure at Hyperscale
Speakers: Gaurav Agarwal (Marvell), Chris Gottbrath (Gottbrath Technologies LLC), Mayank Saxena (Samsung), Harry Kim (XCENA), Youngpyo Joo (SK hynix)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10278/
AI infrastructure is rapidly diversifying, with domain-specific devices such as near-memory accelerators, computational storage, and advanced DPUs promising performance and efficiency gains. This diversity creates a fundamental software challenge: how can hyperscale systems adopt heterogeneous hardware without fragmenting applications, burdening developers, or creating vendor lock-in? This panel explores open, standardized middleware—spanning frameworks, runtimes, and programming models—between high-level ML frameworks (PyTorch, TensorFlow, JAX) and pluggable hardware backends. Such middleware could decouple application and hardware innovation while enabling portable performance. Key capabilities include unified compiler interfaces, pluggable backend optimization, and automated cost-aware compute/data placement to minimize data movement and latency. Hardware vendors, system architects, framework developers, and researchers will discuss current gaps and how efforts across PyTorch, OpenXLA, UXL, oneAPI, SNIA, and standards bodies can converge toward interoperable solutions.

## FTI Workshop: Short Reach Optical Interconnects

### Rack-Ready High-Power ELSFPs: A Co-Development to Support CPO Deployment in AI Scale-Up and Scale-Out Datacenters
Speakers: Nandita Aggarwal (Ayar Labs), Willie Chen (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10299/
Silicon photonics and co-packaged optics (CPO) demand high-power, thermally stable laser sources for AI datacenter interconnects. Ayar Labs, in collaboration with Wiwynn, presents the co-development and statistical characterization of a water-cooled ELSFP delivering 23 dBm per channel across eight channels. Wiwynn designed the cold plate with a parallel flow manifold for multiple ELSFP modules, and a flexible TIM, enabling repeated module insertions under a 45°C coolant inlet boundary condition. Ayar Labs engineered module internals with active TEC management, and through joint CFD and transient simulation, the teams reduced surface temperature non-uniformity from <2.9°C to ≤0.8°C. Built on a multi-vendor laser die ecosystem and validated across >100 units, the ELSFP achieves sub-MHz linewidth, <−150 dBc/Hz RIN, and >18 dB PER, confirming rack-ready production readiness for CPO deployment in AI scale-up and scale-out infrastructure.

### Architecting Near-Packaged Optics for AI Datacenters: Key Technology Choices and Trade-offs
Speakers: Armaghan Eshaghi (Nokia)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10300/
As AI cluster scale pushes interconnect bandwidth and power budgets to their limits, near-packaged optics (NPO) is emerging as a pragmatic path beyond traditional pluggables. Yet the device and packaging choices behind NPO remain open. This session examines key trade-offs in modulator technologies, integrated versus external lasers, and packaging and assembly choices, and their impact on the performance, thermal management, and serviceability.

### High-Density Optical I/O Using Micro-VCSEL Arrays and Multicore Fiber
Speakers: Weipeng Zhang (LightXcelerate Inc), Manchen Hu (LightXcelerate)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10302/
Short-reach scale-up networks require rapidly increasing bandwidth density and energy efficiency for processor-to-processor, processor-to-memory, and processor-to-switch connectivity. We present a scalable optical I/O architecture combining two-dimensional backside-emitting micro-VCSEL arrays with 19-core multicore fiber. A single micro-VCSEL demonstrates approximately 17 GHz small-signal bandwidth and successful 32 Gb/s NRZ transmission. Mapping one optical lane to each core provides 608 Gb/s per fiber, providing beachfront density >6 Tb/s/mm². Larger arrays and higher per-lane speeds provide a path toward multi-terabit optical connectivity for next-generation AI and HPC scale-up systems.

### µLED Interconnects: Addressing the Future of Scale-In and Scale-Up Connectivity
Speakers: Nigel Alvares (Avicena)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10303/
As AI systems scale from advanced packages to large accelerator clusters, copper interconnects are increasingly constrained by bandwidth density, power efficiency, routing complexity, and reach. µLED-based optical interconnects provide a promising route to high-density, low-power optical connectivity with a simple and scalable architecture. This presentation discusses the key drivers, technology advances, and opportunities for µLED interconnects in future scale-in and scale-up AI systems.

### Optical Scale-Up AI architecture with Co-Packaged Optics with Open Rack-Scale Infrastructure
Speakers: Kinny Chen (Wiwynn Corporation), Vishal Chandrasekar (Ayar Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10304/
Optical interconnects enable scale-up beyond a single rack. This presentation introduces a co-packaged optics (CPO) fabric architecture that extends a high-bandwidth scale-up domain across multiple racks to support up to 1,024 ASICs, overcoming the ~1-meter reach limitation of passive copper at 224 Gbps PAM4. The proposed deployable four-layer architecture aligns with Open Rack V3 and encompasses compute, optical fabric, tray-to-rack, rack-to-POD integration, and management layers. Feasibility is demonstrated through a working physical prototype, with successive generations showcased at OFC and Computex 2026. This work contributes to OCP’s SROI workstream and advances the OCP Community’s Open Systems for AI strategic initiative toward an open and interoperable optical scale-up infrastructure standard.

### Predictable Performance, Link Stability and density: Advancing field ready lens connectivity for next generation ai networks
Speakers: Rebecca Lovely (Corning)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10305/
A discussion of lens-based optical interface architectures for next-generation AI and hyperscale data center interconnects. This presentation explores how lens-based expanded-beam optical interfaces implemented in compact VSFF form factors can address challenges associated with density scaling, contamination tolerance, and performance variability in front-plane and back-plane applications. Results from optical stability, environmental, contamination, and deployment studies will be discussed, with particular emphasis on how predictable connector performance can reduce high-loss outliers, simplify deployment, and improve operational confidence as AI optical infrastructure scales.

### Scale-up optical reliability and GPU/system resiliency to optical link flaps
Speakers: Eduard Roytman (Microsoft), Lenin Patra (Marvell), Greg Link (Microsoft), Jeffrey Hutchins (Ranovus)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10307/

## FTS: Keynotes & Awards

### FTS Chair Keynote
Speakers: Jon Summers (Research Institutes of Sweden)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10158/

### Inference Data Center 2030: Memory Pod Fabric with Rightsized Compute presented by Samsung
Speakers: Mayank Saxena (Samsung)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10159/
By 2030, inference data centers will be defined less by raw GPU count and more by optimally architected fabric of memory pods with rightsized compute. Efficient data centers are quickly transcending into multi-tier, photonic-linked exabyte “data oceans” of memory pods, disaggregated via silicon photonics interposers and 3D DRAM, which are emerging as primary substrates for AI context and state. This shift enables fluid, workload-defined cooling and inference that can pull zettabytes of data on demand without hitting latency walls. Al model advances such as hybrid linear attention shrink persistent state by up to 100x, requiring HBM5, SOCAMM, and glass-substrate memory tiers to act as a self-orchestrating continuum powering agentic AI workloads across GW-scale data centers. This keynote will translate those technology vectors into deployment tradeoffs and decisions you can make today, in 2026. Outlining a path from current GPU-centric inference clusters to future-ready inference fabrics in data centers built on memory pods, e.g., shift to modular hardware sockets, co-packaged optics, fabric-level multi-tier memory latency handling and interconnected system topology, to name a few.

## Keynotes

### Welcome to the 2026 OCP Global Summit
Speakers: George Tchaparian (Open Compute Project Foundation (OCP))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10139/
Hear from our CEO as we kickoff the 2026 OCP Global Summit!

### Building the Next Era of AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Dr. Lisa Su (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10134/

### Concrete to Code: Engineering AI Infrastructure at Gigawatt Scale
Speakers: Santosh Janardhan (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10135/
AI has changed the shape of infrastructure. Santosh Janardhan, Head of Infrastructure at Meta, will share how Meta is designing, building, and operating at a scale that required rethinking the network, the facility, and the systems that hold it all together.

### The Agentic Decade Starts with Open Infrastructure
Speakers: Mohamed Awad (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10136/
Agentic AI is changing the unit of scale in the data center. AI is evolving from models responding to prompts into agents that reason, use tools, access data and act across increasingly complex systems. As these workflows scale, the challenge moves beyond any single processor, accelerator or server. Performance, efficiency and scalability become infrastructure-level problems. In this keynote, Arm will explore what this shift means for the architecture of next-generation AI data centers, why the industry may need to rethink how infrastructure is designed and measured, and the role collaboration can play in scaling the agentic era. Drawing on Arm’s work across the AI infrastructure ecosystem, the session will examine how the industry can move beyond optimizing individual components toward building more efficient, interoperable and scalable systems for the next decade of AI.

### Scaling AI Infrastructure Through Open Innovation
Speakers: Asad Khamisy (Broadcom)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10137/
AI infrastructure is scaling faster than ever as both training and inference drive unprecedented demand for compute, memory, and networking. Training requires tightly coupled clusters with massive bandwidth, while inference demands highly efficient, low-latency fabrics that can operate at global scale. Meeting these demands requires a unified networking architecture that can scale up within and across racks, scale out across data centers, and scale across geographies to support million-plus-XPU AI infrastructures. This session highlights how the OCP community is advancing open AI networking through initiatives such as Scale-Up Ethernet Transport (SUE-T), Ethernet Scale-Up Networking (ESUN), Open Cluster Designs for AI, and Multipath Reliable Connection (MRC). MRC extends Ethernet fabrics with intelligent path diversity, reliability, and load balancing to improve utilization and resiliency at scale. The session will also explore emerging interconnect technologies and Optical Compute Interconnect (OCI), which increase bandwidth density, improve power efficiency, and enable larger scale-up domains. Together, these innovations demonstrate how open, Ethernet-based AI fabrics are democratizing next-generation AI infrastructure.

### AI Factories at Scale: Advancing Open Infrastructure for Agentic AI
Speakers: Ian Buck (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10138/
Useful AI has arrived. Agentic AI is redefining computing, moving from simple question-and-answer interactions to prompts that deliver productive work. This new computing model requires infrastructure to move beyond individual racks to datacenter-scale systems where compute, networking, storage, power, cooling, and software operate as one. The NVIDIA Vera Rubin platform with the OCP MGX rack architecture brings seven breakthrough chips and five rack-scale systems together for end-to-end agentic AI. Learn how NVIDIA and its partners are advancing the open ecosystem through OCP and shaping the path forward for AI factories at scale.

### OCP Welcome Address
Speakers: George Tchaparian (Open Compute Project Foundation (OCP))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10140/
Hear from our CEO as we kickoff Day 2 of the OCP Global Summit

### Intelligence Infrastructure: Scaling AI for the Frontier Era
Speakers: Reynold D'Sa (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10141/
AI’s next frontier depends on infrastructure engineered as one coordinated system: from silicon, models, and software to networking, power, cooling, hardware security, and datacenter design and operations. This talk advances the vision of the Frontier Firm, where human knowledge, judgment, and creativity combine with AI capability in a continuous learning loop and highlights how the infrastructure beneath it can make that model accessible to every organization. Microsoft brings these layers together through full-stack co-design, turning advances across the stack into useful intelligence that is scalable, reliable, efficient, secure, and cost effective. In this keynote, Microsoft will share how it is advancing frontier infrastructure from purpose-built silicon and high-performance networking fabrics to power efficiency, liquid cooling, hardware-rooted security, and self-managing fleet reliability. The session will show how lessons from operating AI infrastructure at global scale shape platforms built for real enterprise workloads, customer choice, and measurable outcomes. The keynote will also explore how open specifications, reference architectures, and collaboration through OCP can accelerate interoperability and sustainable growth: including cleaner backup power, lower-carbon construction, closed-loop cooling, lifecycle carbon standards, and circularity. Attendees will gain a clear view of where the industry must co-design across organizational boundaries to make advanced AI infrastructure more accessible, resilient, and sustainable.

### Scaling AI Infrastructure Responsibly Through Open Standards
Speakers: Pradeep Vincent (Oracle)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10214/

### Women in OCP (WOCP) - Community First, AI Forward
Speakers: Sung A Kim (bp Castrol), Anu Ramamurthy (Microchip), Allison Boen (Alcatex and Shell Fluid)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10143/
Hear from the WOCP leaders and find out more about this growing community group!

### Secure Control and Management Plane (SCMP): From AI Racks to Autonomous Infrastructure
Speakers: Ford Tamer (Lattice Semiconductor), Sanjoy Maity (AMI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10144/
The future of infrastructure will require systems that can operate with less human intervention, from dark data centers on Earth to systems in space. That will not be possible if compute, power, cooling, firmware, security, and telemetry continue to be managed separately. AI racks are where this challenge is showing up first, because performance, availability, recovery, and efficiency now depend on the rack working as one system. Ford Tamer, CEO of Lattice Semiconductor, joined by Sanjoy Maity, Senior Vice President and Leader of AMI, Lattice Semiconductor’s platform firmware and infrastructure manageability business, will discuss how the OCP community can connect the open technologies already in place into a common management and control model for compute infrastructure. They will also outline the role OCP can play in defining the interfaces, specifications, and reference designs needed to make that model practical.

### Scalable and Open AI Solutions: From Cloud to Sovereign to Edge
Speakers: Trish Damkroger (HPE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10145/
As AI inference moves closer to users, infrastructure must support an AI Compute Continuum, spanning cloud, sovereign and edge. OCP is driving this shift through interoperable solutions across compute, networking, storage, power, cooling, and management. HPE, in collaboration with OCP contributors, is advancing open AI systems through modular hardware, next-generation OCP NICs, open Ethernet, and SONiC-based fabrics, and by shipping the industry-first DLC ORv3, compliant 1.6T switch. HPE and partner-led initiatives, such as AMD Helios and GX5000-based systems, integrate OCP technologies across compute, power, and cooling. As AI shifts toward inference-driven workflows, sustained collaboration, and adoption of open standards such as UEC and UALink will be critical to delivering scalable, energy-efficient, AI infrastructure. HPE remains committed to advancing this open architecture vision with the OCP ecosystem.

### Put Every Prompt in Its Place
Speakers: Nic Dubé (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10146/
The future of AI isn't about sending every request to the largest model. It's about matching each prompt to the right intelligence, the right infrastructure, at the right cost. As open models and agents reshape computing, AI has become a systems problem. This session explores how open ecosystems, heterogeneous infrastructure, and right-sized intelligence will define the next era of AI, from compute and inference infrastructure to next-generation interconnects and silicon photonics.

### Shifting to DC Power Distribution in Data Centers: A Collaborative Journey
Speakers: Paolo Catapane (ABB), Peter De Bock (Eaton)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10147/
More compute. More power. Less space. Rack power density has increased by an order of magnitude, pushing conventional power architectures to their limits. The solution lies in shifting low voltage power distribution to Direct Current. But achieving it demands industry-wide collaboration on safety, interoperability, and standards. Learn how ABB and Eaton, working at OCP alongside 180+ organizations, are defining the common ground for safe, interoperable 800 VDC power infrastructure.

### Intelligent Infrastructure for the Intelligence Era
Speakers: Partha Ranganathan (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10148/
We are in an extraordinary era of computing, where AI is reshaping how we design the data center itself. Last year, we called on the industry to build agile, fungible infrastructure for the AI era — and this year, that vision is taking shape. Across the community, the open ecosystem is coming together to complete the Open Data Center specification, giving us standards-based, interoperable infrastructure from silicon to cooling, and a shared answer to AI's energy demands through grid mitigation and demand response that make data centers good grid citizens. On that foundation, we'll share Google's newest innovations and what they unlock for capacity at velocity. And we'll show intelligence turned on itself — how we're using AI to design the very systems that run AI. As always, the throughline is co-design: the open collaboration that turns one company's ideas into the whole industry's foundation.

## Manageability Workshop

### Redfish Model Enhancements; Policy Model, Redfish for Personal Devices, Redfish for Backup Power and Energy Storage Systems
Speakers: Divyanand Malavalli (AMD), Jeff Autor (Vertiv), Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10243/
This session will cover new modeling enhancement topics worked over the last year in DMTF. The Redfish 2026.2 release included a new “policy service” to represent reaction policies, such as actions taken when a sensor is too hot, and allow users to define their own policies. New work is being discussed to address aging concerns with DASH 1.0 by applying Redfish to personal (client) devices. A work-in-progress publication to model backup power and energy storage devices is available for review.

### Redfish Native Fleet-Scale GPU Observability Assistant for AI Cluster Operational Efficiency
Speakers: Cyril Jose (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10245/
Hyperscale AI infrastructure runs on hundreds of thousands of accelerators, yet the debug-data pipeline behind that hardware is still stitched together from proprietary agents, vendor-specific tooling, and ad-hoc log collection. When a customer files an RMA or a debug ticket, the engineering team often receives partial telemetry, stale snapshots, or formats that no two organizations can agree on — significantly increasing Time-to-Hypothesis (TTH) and Time-to-Root-Cause (TTR). This session walks through the empirical Redfish based observability assistant (https://github.com/amd/Gyanam-GPU-Observability-Assistant) , the standards choices that make it vendor-neutral debug use cases drawn from real engagements: Fleet-wide thermal outlier triage — heatmap + per-system drill-down + evidence export in under five minutes. VR/HSC/IBC power-rail anomaly investigation — board-level voltage-rail visibility for hardware-debug engineers. Sporadic GPU fault correlation — SSE alert → on-demand diagnostic bundle → reproducible CSV → RMA evidence package.

### Redfish Aliasing Support and Redfish Streaming Telemetry Enhancements
Speakers: Bill Scherer (HPE), Hari Ramachandran (Microsoft Corporation), Jeff Autor (Vertiv)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10244/
Redfish is a hypermedia API, which means resource identifiers that form URI segments must be discovered before they can be accessed. While this flexibility lets vendors follow their own naming conventions, it creates significant discovery overhead in multi-vendor and hyperscale environments. Resource Aliases, introduced in Redfish release 2026.2, address this challenge by providing operator-defined, vendor-independent identifiers for Redfish resources. Management applications can utilize these predictable identifiers to directly access known resources without relying on vendor-specific runtime discovery, reducing complexity and accelerating automation. This session also explores proposed enhancements to Redfish telemetry support, focused on vendor-independent metric naming, alias-based instance labeling, and an efficient time-series output format. These improvements enable telemetry producers and consumers to exchange data using consistent semantic identities, reducing the need for vendor-specific translation logic and simplifying analysis at scale. Together, these capabilities make both resource identity and telemetry identity more predictable, enabling more portable management software, improving interoperability, and simplifying Redfish deployment across multi-vendor environments.

### Remote Attestation of Devices with Redfish and SPDM
Speakers: Jeff Hilland (Intel), Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10246/
Coupling SPDM and Redfish together enables remote attestation of devices in the data center. DMTF's SPDM (Security Protocol and Data Model) provides methods for a BMC to secure communications with hardware components and verify their authenticity. Redfish defines modeling patterns that allow a BMC to report the security status and health of these devices. Redfish-based BMCs provide actions for a remote user to collect signed measurement transcripts for external processing. This presentation explains how remote attestation of remote devices can be guaranteed when these two standards are used together in a server's management processor.

### SPDM Update: Hybrid, Authorization Policy and Standard Signatures (FRU)
Speakers: Jeff Hilland (Intel), Brett Henning (Broadcom Inc.), Bill Scherer (HPE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10247/
The DMTF Security Protocol and Data Model (SPDM) Working Group continues to advance the ecosystem for robust, interoperable platform security. This session provides a comprehensive update on the group’s latest technical progress, emerging specifications, and deployment guidance across modern infrastructure environments. We will highlight key developments including: · A flyover of the 274 v1.5 updates, focusing on notable enhancements and alignment with industry requirements. · Evolving Authentication Policy (Auth Policy) mechanisms to support flexible, policy-driven trust decisions across heterogeneous systems. · Applying the SPDM Signing specification to Field Replaceable Unit (FRU) signing to strengthen component authenticity and supply chain integrity, Attendees will gain insight into the working group’s publication roadmap, including expectations for upcoming errata releases and the stabilization of prior SPDM releases. This session is intended for architects, firmware developers, and standards contributors seeking to align with the latest SPDM direction and implementation best practices.

### Crypto for SPDM: From Legacy to Quantum-Resistance and More
Speakers: Wilson Young (Solidigm Technology), Xiaoyu Ruan (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10248/
Since its introduction in 2019, the Security Protocol and Data Model (SPDM) has emerged as the predominant security protocol standard for devices and embedded systems. In this presentation, we revisit the foundation of SPDM – cryptography – and provide a comprehensive historical walkthrough of SPDM's cryptographic algorithm and scheme selections and integration into the specification – spanning from the initial SPDM 1.0 release in 2019 through the latest SPDM 1.4 revision published in 2025. Over the course of this seven-year development trajectory, the SPDM specification has evolved in direct response to significant advances in quantum computing and the corresponding cryptographic landscape shifts. This presentation examines the decision-making framework employed by the SPDM Working Group in selecting cryptographic algorithms and schemes, including the reference standards and external inputs consulted, the evaluation criteria applied, and the processes governing design finalization. The presentation further extends to prospective enhancements currently under consideration, encompassing the post-quantum cryptography (PQC) hybrid support in SPDM 1.5 and longer-term directions for cryptography of the specification.

### Using SPDM for PLDM Firmware Updates: Authorization Policy and Measurement Manifest Capabilities
Speakers: Patrick Caporale (Lenovo), Brett Henning (Broadcom Inc.), Scott Phuong (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10249/
PLDM Firmware Updates is widely used to perform out of band updates to a variety of device types using a standardized protocol without needing custom/specific code to transfer the image. With the new DMTF Authorization Policy Specification the ability to define a policy definition with command level privileges that aligns to the PLDM FW Update flow. This provide a new level of security provisioning within a managed platform in addition to the existing capability of updating firmware images using secured messages and sessions. In addition, the latest PLDM Firmware Update Header can contain data that aligns with the SPDM Standards Body and Vendor Header Registry Specification to provide the management controller with a reference manifest associated to the specific image used for updating the device. This data structure is aligned with industry recognized formats and can be a benefit to a managed platform intending to obtain SPDM Measurements for the firmware running on the device. This session is intended for implementers of managed platforms looking to learn more about how to implement SPDM security methods when performing PLDM Firmware Updates.

### OCP RAS API PLDM Modeling
Speakers: Justin King (AMD), Samer El-Haj-Mahmoud (Arm), Janusz Jurski (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10250/
This presentation explores how the OCP-defined RAS API abstraction can be mapped to PLDM message semantics for use within a managed system. We will focus on identifying how RAS API concepts, objects, and flows can be represented using PLDM so that management controllers can interact with silicon-provided RAS endpoints in a consistent and interoperable way. Rather than redefining the RAS API, this effort is intended to describe how its abstractions can be expressed over PLDM, including fault reporting, analysis coordination, and action or remediation guidance exchanged across the host-to-BMC management boundary. A primary benefit of this mapping is enabling platform-agnostic BMC implementations - silicon vendors can implement the host-side RAS API endpoint, while BMC firmware can consume a consistent PLDM representation independent of vendor or platform-specific integration details. Establishing this interoperability reduces duplicated integration work, lowers implementation cost, and shortens time to market for systems that require robust and consistent RAS behavior across heterogeneous platforms.

### PLDM Modeling for Common Crash Dump, Bulk Telemetry and Watchdog Timer Access
Speakers: Lukasz Lanecki (Intel), Patrick Schoeller (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10251/
The industry, and specifically OCP, has adopted DMTF manageability standards. Many OEMs are integrating PMCI WG standards such as PLDM and NC-SI into their products to allow seamless integration into PMCI WG compliant platforms. This presentation will introduce the DSP2091 PLDM Bulk Telemetry Modeling, DSP2092 PLDM Crash Dump Modeling and DSP2093 PLDM Watchdog Timer Modeling PMCI WG Technology papers, providing an overview of the Data Models and solicit community feedback. Key Features of the PLDM Bulk Telemetry Modeling, Crash Dump Modeling, and Watchdog Timer Modeling: •Leverage existing PLDM standards such as PLDM for Monitor and Control and PLDM for File Transfer •Provides guidance on the Data Model such that the capabilities are self-describing to the requester such as the BMC •Provide guidance of configurable device supported timers, providing a uniform interface operating on a self-describing data model

page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10151/
Welcome & Overview: Adoption, Ecosystem Growth, and the Path to Participation

### From Silicon to Services
Speakers: Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10252/
Dylan Patel, CEO of SemiAnalysis, will present the How of Optimizing Every Layer of the AI Infrastructure for Tokenomics at Scale.

### Modular by Design: Building AI Clusters from Factory-Built Units
Speakers: Alex Akesson (Crusoe Energy Systems), Bill Stube (Crusoe Energy Systems)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9799/ | materials: https://www.crusoe.ai/resources/newsroom/crusoe-unveils-crusoe-edge-zones
Modular Data Center Sub-Project As AI infrastructure demands accelerate, the industry needs deployment models that are faster, more flexible, and easier to standardize. This presentation proposes a modular approach to AI cluster design: factory-built units, each pre-integrated with cooling, power, and networking, that can be assembled into large-scale clusters. We will present a reference architecture for modular AI cluster construction, covering unit-level specifications, inter-unit networking topology, and power/ cooling integration. Key design principles include backbone-first sequencing for incremental energization, single-fabric connectivity constrained by active-optics reach, and logical cluster partitioning that supports multi-tenant workloads without physical recabling. The goal is to bring this architecture to the OCP community: a repeatable, open blueprint that gives the industry a common foundation for building large AI clusters from modular units.

### Designing AI Infrastructure for the Inference Era
Speakers: Ty Schmitt (Dell), Mohan Kumar (Oracle Cloud Infrastructure), Curtis Aubry (Vantage Data Centers)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10253/
As AI infrastructure evolves from large-scale training toward inference and agentic workloads, the requirements for compute, memory, networking, power and cooling are changing rapidly. At the same time, AI deployments are becoming more heterogeneous, spanning different accelerators, rack architectures, cloud environments and increasingly distributed and edge locations. This panel brings together leaders from Dell, Vantage Data Centers and Oracle to discuss how the industry is designing infrastructure for this next phase of AI. The conversation will explore how to balance performance and utilization with flexibility, accommodate rapidly changing hardware within much longer infrastructure life cycles, and determine where standardization can improve interoperability without constraining innovation.

### From Infrastructure to Impact: Implementing Sovereign AI at Denvr Dataworks
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9796/
AI innovation is accelerating, but turning promising technology into meaningful outcomes requires more than access to computing power. Organizations must navigate challenges involving data sovereignty, technical complexity, and infrastructure scalability. Through three Canadian customer examples, the City of Calgary, Precision AI, and NESI, Denvr Dataworks explores how different industries are overcoming these barriers. From enabling municipal AI experimentation and protecting proprietary engineering knowledge to powering computer vision for precision agriculture, these stories demonstrate the diverse requirements shaping AI adoption. The session connects these customer outcomes to the infrastructure and cloud technologies that enable them, including sovereign computing, efficient high-density data centers, and accessible AI services. Attendees will learn why successful AI adoption requires infrastructure that adapts to the organization, not the other way around.

### From Projects to Platforms: Reducing Risk in AI Infrastructure Through Standardization and Ecosystem Integration
Speakers: Troy Goldschmidt, PE (Kele Incorporated)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9593/ | materials: https://q.com/
As AI infrastructure scales from individual facilities to gigawatt-scale campuses, the challenge is no longer simply deploying new technology. The challenge is reducing financial, operational, technical, and schedule risk while delivering capacity at unprecedented speed. Operators, engineers, contractors, OEMs, and technology providers are rethinking how infrastructure is designed, integrated, commissioned, and deployed. Traditional project-based approaches are giving way to platform architectures, modular delivery, off-site commissioning, and deeper ecosystem collaboration. This session explores lessons learned from deploying AI-ready infrastructure at scale, including platform standardization, design-level coordination, supply chain resiliency, controls integration, and strategies for balancing innovation with operational reliability. Attendees will gain practical insights into accelerating deployment while reducing risk across the infrastructure lifecycle.

### A unified power management solution for  AI data centers and legacy data centers
Speakers: shuming Zhu (AMI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9564/ | materials: https://www.ami.com/resources/real-time-power-usage-effectiveness-pue-solution-for-data-centers-and-racks/
As more and more enterprises begin adopting AI servers, tens of thousands of data centers are being upgraded to support new hardware, new infrastructure, and new management requirements. This transition period will take years. The presentation will focus on the challenges of power management that arise as traditional data centers evolve into AI data centers, such as new infrastructure support, communication protocols, metrics, and sampling intervals, automatic emergency handling, and more. It will also cover available solutions to address these challenges. Ultimately, the goal of this presentation is to simplify power management and improve data center energy efficiency during the evolution of data centers.

### Standardizing AI Data Centers to Unlock Faster Access to Power
Speakers: Anuja Ratnayake (EPRI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10285/
Standardizing AI Data Centers to Unlock Faster Access to Power

### Intelligent Power Smoothing at the Vera-Rubin Rack
Speakers: Gabriele Gorla (NVIDIA), Rouslan Dimitrov (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10314/
AI workloads can create large, synchronized swings in data center power demand, challenging utility grids designed for steadier loads. This talk introduces Intelligent Power Smoothing (IPS), which controls the rack power ramp rates and fluctuations to meet grid requirements without dedicated battery systems for power smoothing. IPS also eliminates most stranded power, putting the provisioned capacity to useful work. The talk will present rack AC whip power measurements across a range of workloads and analyze their compliance with grid requirements.

Speakers: Keegan Yaroch (DOW Chemical), Paul Artman (AMD), Jacob Paugh (ChemTreat), Cam Turner (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10152/
PG25 at Scale: What We've Learned and Where We're Going

### The Missing Layer: Why Compute Hardware Needs a BOM Interchange Standard
Speakers: Jacob Wright (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8862/ | materials: https://cyclonedx.org/capabilities/hbom/
OCP standardizes rack architectures, power, cooling, and networking but manufacturing BOM data still moves between vendors and customers via unstructured spreadsheets. No open standard exists for structured BOM interchange across organizations. This session presents results from validating a version-controlled BOM exchange protocol against real production manufacturing BOMs from multiple ODMs. By applying git-like semantics (immutable snapshots, diffs, and attribution) to manufacturer-customer BOM exchange, we eliminate 30-day manual reconciliation cycles and resolve part number fragmentation where identical components carry 5+ identifiers across the supply chain. The proposed format extends CycloneDX HBOM (ECMA-424) and aligns with IPC-2591, delivering machine-readable hierarchical BOM data consumable by any PLM, analytics, or supply chain system. We invite collaboration from ODMs, hyperscalers, and software vendors to develop this as an OCP supply chain interoperability workstream.

### From Compute Demand to Capacity Delivered: Unlocking the Data Center Value Chain for the AI Build-Out Through OCP-Aligned Design and Supply Chain Innovation
Speakers: Denise Lee (McKinsey & Company), Elia Berteletti (McKinsey & Company)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8984/ | materials: https://www.mckinsey.com/industries/technology-media-and-telecommunications/our-insights/the-cost-of-compute-a-7-trillion-dollar-race-to-scale-data-centers
AI is turning compute into a defining infrastructure constraint of this decade. Global data center demand is expected to rise sharply through 2035, requiring $7T in investment across land, power, cooling, electrical infrastructure, semiconductors, networking, construction, and operations. Yet deployment is constrained by power availability, interconnection queues, equipment lead times, thermal readiness, labor shortages, and the need to align infrastructure with AI silicon. This session will examine where demand is headed, how investment will flow across the value chain, and where bottlenecks could slow conversion of capital into live capacity. We will connect these dynamics to OCP-recognized hardware, open designs, and standard components. Attendees will leave with a practical view of how OCP-aligned specs, modular architectures, reference designs, and validation can reduce fragmentation, improve interoperability, speed supplier readiness, and help scale AI infrastructure.

### More Tokens, Same Megawatts: How Agentic AI Rewrites AI Factory Economics
Speakers: Imran Latif (JCI), Mohamed Eissa (Johnson Controls)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9887/
Hyperscaler CapEx is racing toward $1.1 trillion by 2027, and AI data center power is set to climb from 60 GW to 340 GW by 2030. When power is this scarce, the winners won't lock down the most megawatts. They'll pull the most tokens out of the watts they already have. Agentic AI connects two worlds that rarely talk: the compute side (GPU temperatures, workloads) and the facility side (chillers, coolant, ambient conditions). Read in one loop, they let a 500 MW site shift roughly 100 MW from overhead into compute. PUE drops from 1.54 to 1.18, and the site produces 31 percent more tokens and revenue per watt. Same power envelope. Same grid connection. This panel digs into the hard parts: who owns the IT and OT boundary, whether autonomous control earns trust in regulated environments, and whether AI tuning AI becomes the flywheel that decides who keeps scaling.

### Upgrading OCP S.A.F.E. and S.O.L.I.D. for the AI age
Speakers: Zahra Lak (Microsoft), Jeff Andersen (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10282/
S.A.F.E. defines how data center products are reviewed for security. S.O.L.I.D. defines security requirements for them. With the recent advances in AI security reviews, these programs needed to be updated to continue ensuring products are secure. In this talk we present our experience with AI security reviews, how S.A.F.E. and S.O.L.I.D. have been updated and what benefits this brings to the industry.

### Overcoming OCP Adoption Barriers in European Data Centers
Speakers: Christoph Streit (ScaleUp Technologies), Jeroen Burks (OCP)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9584/
While the Open Compute Project (OCP) is a big player in hyperscale infrastructure worldwide, it's not being used that much by European companies and mid-market providers. Legacy 19-inch ways of thinking, worries about local vendor support, and a general lack of awareness make it hard to see the huge sustainability and cost benefits that open hardware can deliver. In this session, ScaleUp talks about what it's like to run a sustainable IaaS public cloud on OCP hardware and help German businesses set up their own on-premises OCP deployments. We'll go over the main problems European IT teams are dealing with, like getting into existing tech setups and managing the whole lifecycle. We'll talk about how the European tech ecosystem can bridge this gap.

### Recent Open-Networking Deployment: Feedback and Trends
Speakers: Sujay Gupta (Edgecore)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10283/
In this session, Edgecore will cover recent notable deployment architectures and customer needs. We will also discuss the challenges encountered and how open networking ecosystem solutions were used to address them.

### OCP in Action: How RackRenew’s Hardware Enables Brigade’s Open, Self-Service Platform
Speakers: Matty Bakkeren (RackRenew)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10268/
In this 15-minute breakout session, we will explore how RackRenew’s OCP-certified, remanufactured hardware serves as the backbone for Brigade’s open, self-service orchestration platform, delivering a sovereign, sustainable, and scalable solution. This session is designed for a technical audience passionate about open-source hardware and software, as well as organizations seeking to break free from vendor lock-in while reducing environmental impact.
