## Networking

### DPU Sled Design Standard in a Modular SmartSwitch by Cisco and AMD
Speakers: Murali Venkat (Cisco), Pirooz Tooyserkani (Cisco Systems, Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9239/ | materials: https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi26-wu-shaofeng.pdf
This talk details hardware architecture standard for a DPU Sled , a modular form factor for a SmartSwitch . The DPU Sled introduces an OIR-able design supporting select DPU ASICs based on specific mechanical, thermal, signal integrity, and power constraints. We focus on key architectural elements, component functions, and Sled electrical high-speed interfaces and management signals with a focus on system behavior and constraints—such as size, memory utilization, and power—rather than specific manufacturing methods. We also cover key insights for next-generation designs. Additionally we discuss NOS agnostic software aspects i.e. system integration, lifecycle management and monitoring. This design is endorsed by a leading Hyperscaler. Our call to action includes open sourcing the sled design through OCP to enable vendor agnostic hw-design and broaden the eco-system. We are looking for DPU/OEM/ODM vendors in the community to collaborate and accelerate adoption of switch-based DPU solutions.

### Meta 102.4T Ethernet Switch
Speakers: Rongchun Zhou (Meta), Reza Niazmand (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9150/
Meta IceCube is an advanced 102.4T non-blocking Ethernet switch built on the Broadcom TH6C switch ASIC and running FBOSS. In a 2OU form factor with 64× OSFP-RHS user ports and liquid cooling, IceCube is purpose-built for AI and high-performance networking applications. IecPack is an ORv3-HPRv1 rack that integrates eight IceCube blades with servicing equipment, delivering versatile switch roles across Meta data centers. The IceCube/IcePack platform is engineered for reliability, resiliency, operationally efficiency, power efficiency, and NPI efficiency.

### Switch Platform Architecture: A Unified Network Switch Platform Reference Design
Speakers: Tao Ren (Meta), Ying Xie (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9232/
The proliferation of switch platforms across the industry has led to fragmented and redundant platform software development efforts. This specification addresses the challenge by defining a reference architecture for the switch platform layer, with the goal of simplifying and accelerating the platform onboarding process. This architecture sets design guidelines specifically for the platform layer, covering Power Sequencing, Hardware Access, Hardware Inventory Management, Environment Monitoring and Control, Firmware Maintenance, and Security. Data and Control planes, including routing protocols and forwarding management, are excluded. By codifying these guidelines, new switch platforms can onboard more efficiently using reusable hardware designs and existing software stacks. While primarily software-focused, this architecture incorporates essential hardware context to support seamless development and integration.

### More Signal, Less Noise: Improving AI Cluster Returns Through Standardized Transceiver Telemetry in the OCP Optics Reliability Workstream
Speakers: Chris Collins (Credo)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9079/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Networking/OpticsReliability
As AI fabrics scale to hundreds of thousands of GPU nodes, optical links become a key reliability bottleneck. Failures, link flaps, and limited visibility, especially at bare-metal endpoints, impact network stability and training efficiency. This presentation introduces the OCP Enhanced Telemetry Optics Software Specification, a standardized approach to improving transceiver observability. The specification defines six core capabilities: continuous link monitoring, a Link Health Score, in-band communication, persistent event logging, hitless firmware updates, and a host-side telemetry SDK. These provide end-to-end visibility and faster fault isolation, even in breakout configurations. The framework includes standardized telemetry commands, remote diagnostics, and a SONiC-compatible SDK for multi-vendor interoperability. A production trial of Credo transceivers demonstrates improved reliability, fault detection, and operational visibility in large-scale AI deployments.

### Operating Linear Pluggable Optics at Hyperscale: From Link Management to Production Reliability
Speakers: Amin Yekani (Meta), Qing Wang (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9673/
Can optical links remain reliable at hyperscale without a DSP safety net? LPO eliminates the in-module retimer, shifting equalization to the host ASIC. We present experience operating LPO across three pillars, supported by both controlled lab and live production field data. LPO Link Management: Without a DSP that autonomously adapts, LPO requires host and module-side signal conditioning maintained across the fleet. We discuss the operational framework and lessons learned. Link Reliability Monitoring: We describe the multi-layer monitoring approach to validate LPO link health covering signal quality, error metrics, and fleet-wide coverage including early detection of lane-level degradation. Production Results: A large-scale deployment carrying live traffic has accumulated tens of millions of link-hours. We present failure rate, signal quality, power, and thermal measurements alongside the methodology used to evaluate LPO at scale. Findings apply to any DSP-free optical architecture.

### SONiC for OTN: Expanding SONiC and OCP SAI into Optical Transport Network
Speakers: xuemei shi (Alibaba), Eddie Ruan (Alibaba)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8899/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/sai
Optical transport networks (OTN) for data center interconnects are shifting to open and disaggregated systems, yet equipment still relies on vendor-specific Network Operating Systems (NOS), increasing cost at scale. The SONiC-OTN workgroup—with contributions from Alibaba, Microsoft, Molex, Nokia, Accelink, and Cisco—is extending SONiC to support white-box OTN equipment. Building on OCP SAI (Switch Abstraction Interface) experimental extension framework, we defined OTN extensions within SAI as a vendor-neutral abstraction for optical components including optical amplifiers and variable optical attenuators. SONiC-OTN reuses SONiC's containerized architecture and adopts a Multi-ASIC design for managing multiple optical linecards. This session shares our collaborative journey: challenges in abstracting optical hardware, architectural decisions made together, and engineering results achieved. We will also discuss next steps and how attendees can get involved.

### Closing the Observability Loop in SAI: From Performance Telemetry to Platform Constraint Discovery
Speakers: Ravindranath Coimbatore Kanakarajan (Marvell), Jai Kumar (Broadcom)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9252/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI/pull/2287
As AI workloads impose ever-tighter SLAs on hyperscale infrastructure, operators face heterogeneous platforms with varying capabilities and distinct hardware limits. Without a principled way to discover those boundaries or measure execution performance, the networking stack stays opaque — impossible for AI-driven controllers to reason over or safely program at scale. This session presents two complementary SAI advancements that close the gap. The first introduces a performance monitoring framework enabling NOS applications to track real-time dataplane execution — critical for latency-sensitive AI fabric management. The second empowers the control plane to introspect platform-enforced value boundaries for any attribute, eliminating misconfigurations when assumptions diverge from hardware reality. Together, they transform SAI into a self-describing software-silicon contract, enabling observable, constraint-aware AI management across diverse switching platforms.

### NVIDIA SDK + FBOSS: Building Production-Ready AI Network Fabrics
Speakers: Harish Krishnamoorthy (NVIDIA), Parvez Shaikh (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9277/ | materials: https://urm.nvidia.com/artifactory/sw-nbu-fboss-generic/
Modern AI infrastructure depends on networks that can scale to massive GPU clusters while delivering high bandwidth, low latency, fast failure recovery, and predictable operations. Meta’s FBOSS, an open-source networking operating system, provides a programmable, testable, and production-proven platform for operating these large-scale fabrics. This work describes the joint onboarding of NVIDIA Spectrum switch ASICs into Meta FBOSS through NVIDIA’s SDK and SAI APIs. The integration covered both infrastructure changes, including ISSU support, SDK-as-a-library packaging, firmware upgrade flows, and SDK usability, and feature development across ACLs, telemetry, cable-length estimation, and FBOSS platform adaptations. A test-driven and agile collaboration model, backed by CI/CD test execution in NVIDIA environments, enabled rapid issue discovery and resolution. The teams achieved 100% FBOSS agent and test pass rates and successfully moved through EVT, DVT, PVT, and production in a compressed timeline. Open-source collaboration through the FBOSS GitHub workflow and early validation using NVIDIA SimX simulation helped make next-generation ASIC onboarding faster, smoother, and repeatable.

### Bridging the Gap: Accelerating Open-Source Agility on Cisco Silicon One Platforms
Speakers: Masiuddin Mohammed (Cisco), Wei-Jen Huang (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9022/
Cisco Silicon One unifies G-series (routing) and P-series (switching) ASICs, providing a consistent foundation for disaggregated networking. We enable open-source NOS like SONiC and FBOSS via a common BSP and robust SAI integration. This session shares our journey onboarding new ASICs and SDKs using OCP-standard SAI test suites. We will discuss key learnings in achieving feature parity and performance validation within open-source frameworks, highlighting how to overcome common integration hurdles. By sharing these experiences, we aim to provide a blueprint for hardware-software decoupling. We conclude with a call to the community to adopt these practices, contribute to upstream SAI improvements, and expand collaborative test coverage. Join us to bridge the gap between high-performance silicon and open-source agility, driving the next generation of scalable, disaggregated data center infrastructure.

### Open, AI-Ready FBOSS: From Platform Bring-up to Solution Qualification for the OCP Ecosystem
Speakers: Benoit Sigoure (Nexthop AI), Srikrishna Gopu (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9331/ | materials: https://github.com/facebook/fboss
Onboarding new network hardware onto FBOSS, from platform bring-up through fabric-level solution testing, has traditionally taken months and deep institutional knowledge. In this talk, we show how upstream architectural contributions to OSS FBOSS, a schema-validated, data-driven platform configuration model, a standalone FBOSS distribution, and a configuration CLI, created a structured foundation that AI agents can operate on effectively. With tedious manual config edits replaced by typed, validated contracts, AI can now generate platform configs, build tooling, and test scaffolding with minimal human intervention. Using newer platforms as a case study, Wedge800 and 100T air-cooled switches, we show how this AI-ready architecture compressed the NPI timeline. For the OCP community: vendors can now use AI on open-source FBOSS to independently develop, validate, and ship on their hardware, without large teams or Meta-internal tooling.

### Optically-Accelerated AI Clusters Enabled by Interoperable Multi-Vendor OCSs: Use Cases and Opportunities
Speakers: Iwan Kartawira (iPronics), Kazuaki Ueda (KDDI Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9034/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-ocs-white-paper-april-2026-final-pdf | pdf: https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16907
Large-scale AI clusters require scalable, energy-efficient, and operationally sustainable interconnect architecture. While optical circuit switches (OCSs) have emerged as a promising building block for AI clusters, practical deployment remains challenging due to vendor-specific implementations, limited interoperability, and the lack of operational use cases beyond dynamic connectivity. This session discusses how interoperable multi-vendor OCSs can enable open optically-accelerated AI clusters and presents several emerging use cases, including dynamic optical scale-up and scale-out interconnection, shared spare-resource pooling for failure recovery, and programmable optical infrastructure management. The session also examines ongoing industry efforts toward open optical AI-cluster architectures, including OCP-compatible architectures and multi-vendor operational models.

### Standardizing OCS Interfaces for Seamless Integration
Speakers: Richard Yu (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9624/
Optical Circuit Switches (OCS) are experiencing rapid industry adoption, particularly within massive AI/ML clusters and next-generation data centers. This momentum is driven by their ability to deliver the ultra-low latency, reduced power consumption, and dynamic network fungibility required to scale distributed AI training workloads. However, as the vendor ecosystem expands with diverse technologies (e.g., MEMS, robotics, liquid crystal), operators face significant challenges with device onboarding, lifecycle management, and standardized monitoring. To address these hurdles, the OCP OCS Working Group has developed a standard set of interfaces for configuration, control, monitoring, and operations, anchored around OpenConfig, gNMI, and gNOI. Establishing these common interfaces drastically reduces multi-vendor integration friction and enables unified test frameworks. This talk will explore these new standards and how they unlock scalable, interoperable OCS fabrics for the AI era.

### Zero-Touch OCS Fabrics for AI Scale-Out Infrastructure at Hyperscale
Speakers: Michael DeMerchant (Lumentum Operations LLC), Kannan Raj (Oracle)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9164/
As AI infrastructure scales rapidly, Optical Circuit Switches (OCS) are becoming a key building block for agile, resilient data center fabrics. However, successful integration of OCS into AI scale-out networks requires a shift from legacy, CLI-driven operations to a fully automated, API-first model. In alignment with the direction of the OCP OCS Subproject, this presentation describes a production-ready, gRPC/YANG-based operational framework for software-defined Layer 0 fabrics. We cover Zero-Touch Provisioning (ZTP), automated topology discovery and validation, seamless software upgrades, automated fabric stripe reconfiguration, and gNMI streaming telemetry for reliability monitoring and control. We also highlight hardware and manufacturing advances delivering low, predictable insertion loss, fast switching performance, and interoperability through open optical interfaces.

### ESUN: Open Ethernet for the AI Scale-Up Era
Speakers: Pratik Marolia (Microsoft), Manoj Wadekar (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9092/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-esun-network-operator-requirements-base-specification-rev-1-0-final-pdf
As AI models grow, so does the need for faster networking inside AI server racks. Today's clusters connect thousands of accelerators that must exchange data with minimal overhead — but standard Ethernet wastes significant bandwidth on headers designed for general internet traffic, not AI. Meanwhile, proprietary interconnects lock operators into single-vendor solutions. In October 2025, Meta and Microsoft launched ESUN (Ethernet for Scale-Up Networks) at OCP with AMD, Arista, Broadcom, Cisco, Marvell, NVIDIA, OpenAI, and Oracle. Within months, 170+ contributors from 20+ organizations produced the ESUN 1.0 specification, ratified by OCP in March 2026. ESUN is now working on making Ethernet even better through future versions. This session covers why the industry needs ESUN, what the specification delivers, how a cross-industry coalition delivered it in record time, and what comes next for open AI networking.

### Standardizing Ethernet Scale-Up Validation: Test Strategy and Current Progress on LLR/CBFC
Speakers: Haiyang Zheng (Alibaba Cloud), Yuanwen (Daisy) Sun (Keysight)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9137/ | materials: https://lists.sonicfoundation.dev/g/SONiC-Scale-Up-WG/wiki/39581
As AI/HPC clusters scale to exaflops , Ethernet Scale-Up architectures demand lossless, low-latency communication for tightly coupled workloads. Technologies such as Link-Layer Retry (LLR) and Credit-Based Flow Control (CBFC) are pivotal to achieve fast loss recovery, congestion resilience, and predictable performance. This presentation introduces the Scale-Up Test Standard , a vendor-neutral framework bridging UEC specifications with hardware-level SAI verification to enable interoperable, multi-vendor silicon. We define a comprehensive test matrix exercising key LLR and CBFC behaviors including loss detection, replay, credit management, dynamic backpressure, and Incast scenarios. By correlating traffic injection with silicon-level hardware counters, we verify deterministic behavior under boundary conditions and mixed traffic profiles. This work delivers actionable test metrics and performance baselines, empowering operators to deploy robust, interoperable AI fabrics with confidence.

### The UALink Switch API - A Common Abstraction for Scale-Up AI Switches
Speakers: Ayyappa Nuthalapati (Marvell), Ravindranath Coimbatore Kanakarajan (Marvell), Justin King (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9195/ | materials: https://ualinkconsortium.org/
Ultra Accelerator Link is an open standard for low-latency, high-bandwidth accelerator-to-accelerator communication within scale-up AI pods. As clusters grow, vendor-neutral management of the UALink fabric becomes critical. Presented on behalf of the UALink Management Working Group, this talk introduces UALink-Switch API: an open C API between a Switch Management Agent, the software managing UALink switches, and the Physical Switch silicon. The UALink manageability architecture defines interfaces involving the Pod Controller and Node Management Agent, which manages accelerator nodes. This session focuses on UALink-Switch API and how it enables hardware-independent switch management. Defined early in the UALink switch ecosystem, UALink-Switch API establishes a common abstraction from the start. The talk covers the API proposal, based on the UALink Manageability Spec 1.0, including the CRUD API model, attributes, telemetry, event notifications, and switch-management workflows.

### Srv6 powered backbone networks
Speakers: Jasmeet Bagga (Facebook), Andrey Golovanov (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8904/
Backbone networks play a crucial role in connecting Meta’s disparate regions. Meta’s existing express backbone network has done this job with aplomb. However, as the number of regions grows, providing this connectivity within a latency envelope becomes much harder. This means we end up replicating core-services infra (which need to provide latency guarantees) in each DC region. This is prohibitive at our scale. Enter RBB (Regional Back Bone) networks. This is a newly designed inter-region connectivity network running in parallel to our EBB network. Similar to EBB, it's a traffic engineered network. In this session we will go over The problems RBB solves. Novel use of shallow-buffer switches for long-haul backbone ne Why we choose SRv6 as a vehicle for TE in RBB How SRv6 is used in this network. Finally we talk about the SAI support for SRv6 and our experience with it on FBOSS OCP switches using SAI

### Scaling AI Beyond the Cloud Boundary: High-Capacity Interconnects Between Hyperscalers and GPUaaS NeoClouds
Speakers: Krithika Moorthy (Cisco), Anish Narsian (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8988/ | materials: https://github.com/sonic-net/sonic-swss
As AI training and inference demand grows, hyperscale cloud providers are extending beyond traditional data center boundaries into GPUaaS and NeoCloud environments. The challenge is no longer GPU capacity or bandwidth alone; it is building a secure, programmable, multi-tenant interconnect edge that preserves high-throughput access to cloud storage, networking, and platform services while maintaining tenant isolation, route scale, tunnel scale, and operational control across independent infrastructure domains. In this session, Microsoft and Cisco present a high-capacity cloud-to-NeoCloud DCI architecture for AI traffic. We will discuss a programmable edge gateway model that bridges cloud services with external GPU environments while avoiding physical network exposure. The architecture uses VXLAN/VRF separation, SDN-driven provisioning, high-density ECMP, L3 VXLAN inner field rewrites, and consistent hashing to enable secure service reachability and replace legacy DCI designs.

### Scaling AI Infrastructure Across DC and Beyond: Meta's Journey with BAG
Speakers: Jalpa Patel (Meta), Hany Morsy (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8981/ | materials: https://engineering.fb.com/2026/02/09/data-center-engineering/building-prometheus-how-backend-aggregation-enables-gigawatt-scale-ai-clusters/
As AI models grow exponentially in size and complexity, network fabric connecting GPU clusters must evolve. In this session, we will share how Meta designed and deployed Backend Aggregation (BAG) — purpose-built network architecture optimized for large-scale AI training — and how running it in production within our Prometheus clusters. We will walk through how BAG is enabling us to break through the physical boundaries of a single building and data center, allowing AI clusters to scale-across multiple structures while maintaining scalability and high-bandwidth connectivity that large training jobs demand. Achieving this scale has not come without significant challenges — from optical reach limitations and cable density constraints to congestion management and fault tolerance across extended fabrics. We will cover unique solutions we developed to overcome these obstacles, sharing practical lessons learned and design trade-offs that may benefit broader OCP community.

### Deep Buffers: The Essential Foundation for Scalable AI Training and Inference Infrastructure
Speakers: Golan Schuzkin (Broadcom), Hugh Holbrook (Arista Networks)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9373/ | materials: https://www.broadcom.com/topics/what-is-scale-across-networking-for-ai-clusters
As AI training clusters transition to multi-campus "Scale-Across" regions, the industry faces a critical "Latency Tax." Traditional shallow-buffer architectures, while effective locally, suffer from throughput collapse and packet loss when confronted with the RTT of regional distances. This session introduces a "Hierarchical, Hybrid Buffer Architecture" for regional AI networking. We demonstrate how deep-buffered edge switches act as proactive "Lossless Anchors," absorbing synchronized AI bursts and providing the headroom required for long-haul links to prevent "GPU bubbles" that stall Job Completion Times. We explore the synergy between deep-buffered silicon and intelligent NIC agents, showcasing hardware mechanisms like BTS trimming and Remote Congestion Control to modulate traffic without jitter penalties. Additionally, we highlight why deep buffers are crucial for optimizing AI inference performance, especially for heavy TCP traffic and intensive storage access.

### Bridging On-prem and Cloud — GPU Networking at Meta Scale
Speakers: Carmen Au (Meta), Adi Gangidi (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9603/
Meta's AI infrastructure now spans both on-prem data centers and multiple public clouds, and the RDMA network in both environments has to work seamlessly in each cluster but on occasion across RDMA clusters. This talk covers why hybrid is inevitable, what makes cloud networking fundamentally different from owning the rack, and how we're building toward a single converged operational model that delivers consistent performance, reliability, and tooling at Meta scale, regardless of where the GPUs live. One of the things we’ll dive deep into is what it takes to productionize multi-plane networking in different clouds vs meta-prod. Another thing we’ll dive into is how we expose individual cloud, on-prem network topologies to scheduler so that end-user constraints are seamlessly honored. We’ll also delve into our long distance network that connects some of the Cloud / On-prem RDMA network to unblock a single large training job.

### SRv6 Deployments in Scale-Out and Scale-Across Networks
Speakers: Clarence Filsfils (Cisco), Guohan Lu (OpenAI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8801/
SRv6 empowers applications to control their path in the network by encoding the path directly into the packet header. In this presentation, we review two hyperscale SRv6 deployments in Scale-Out and Scale-Across networks. For Scale-Out, we review the traffic characteristics and discuss how legacy ECMP fails to meet these requirements. We then share the deployment experience from one of the largest SRv6 Scale-Out deployments on how packet spraying over SRv6 disjoint paths enables optimized load-balancing, stateless and reliable fabric, fast reaction to congestion, and deterministic path visibility. For Scale-Across, we review the key architecture characteristics and explain why traffic engineering is a must. We then share the deployment experience from one of the hyperscale SRv6 Scale-Across deployments on how SRv6 addresses the specific traffic engineering requirements for Scale-Across.

### Traffic Engineering in AI DCI/WAN for AI Traffic
Speakers: Yuanchao SU (Alibaba Cloud), Clarence Filsfils (Cisco)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8875/
AI training workloads have exceeded the power and space capacities of individual data centers. Hyperscalers are building AI training infrastructure that span multiple, geographically distributed data centers. AI DCI/WAN is the critical infrastructure layer required to connect these geographically distributed data centers. In this session we review the problem statement and traffic engineering requirements for AI DCI/WAN. We explain how the problem was solved for legacy non-AI traffic and then review the solution for AI traffic. We also explain the collection of necessary data and telemetry for the traffic engineering in AI DCI/WAN. We will review two AI DC/WAN real deployments use-cases.

### MetaRoCE in Software
Speakers: Ashkan Nikravesh (Meta), Yiorgos Nikolaidis (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10127/
AI training clusters are scaling to tens of thousands of accelerators interconnected by RDMA fabrics that must deliver maximum bandwidth with minimal tail latency. Meta's MetaRoCE transport protocol was designed from the ground up for this domain: it provides multipath load balancing, in-band fault detection, per-path congestion control, and out-of-order delivery with in-order completions, all embedded in the NIC transport layer rather than delegated to collective library software or switch-level inspection of transport headers. As MetaRoCE moves toward multi-vendor adoption on programmable hardware (SmartNICs, DPUs, and custom ASICs) the need for an open, portable reference implementation becomes critical. Hardware implementers require a proven, readable codebase to validate their designs against, protocol researchers need a platform to experiment with new features (delayed ACKs, advanced congestion control, multi-plane signaling), and the broader community benefits from a shared artifact that disambiguates the specification and accelerates interoperability testing. We present software-metaroce: a clean-slate, open-source software implementation of the MetaRoCE protocol. It gives hardware implementers a proven, readable codebase to validate their designs against, gives protocol researchers a platform to experiment with new features such as delayed ACKs, advanced congestion control, and multi-plane signaling, and provides the community a shared artifact that disambiguates the specification and accelerates interoperability testing.

### MRC: A Purpose-Built RDMA Transport for AI Training Networks
Speakers: Abdul Kabbani (Microsoft), Wael Noureddine (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9763/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-mrc-1-0-pdf | pdf: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04333
The RDMA MRC (Multipath Reliable Connection) protocol specification was published through OCP in March 2026 and has been implemented in the scale-out back-end Ethernet networks of several leading-edge AI training supercomputers. MRC combines key innovations from several existing data center networking solutions while focusing on the subset of RDMA RC functionality tailored for AI training workloads. This focus was motivated by the need to design a practical solution that could be deployed quickly. This talk introduces the protocol's origins, design rationale, and the workload characteristics that shaped it, explaining why a focused RDMA transport has gained rapid traction in production AI infrastructure.

### Clustermapper: Deterministic path diagnosis for AI backend networks using SRv6
Speakers: Ryder Lewis (OpenAI), Guohan Lu (OpenAI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9467/ | pdf: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04333 | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-mrc-1-0-pdf
AI training jobs are sensitive to tail latency and network failures, but conventional telemetry stops short of answering the operator's key question: which dataplane path is failing? We present Clustermapper, a path diagnosis system for MRC/SRv6 networks that provides deterministic path measurement from every node. Clustermapper runs a lightweight agent on each node that sends SRv6 source-routed self-probes through T0 and T1 switch paths. Deterministic SRv6 source routing transforms diagnosis from statistical inference into ground-truth measurement for a path. This avoids ECMP ambiguity and routing-convergence noise, and lets the system localize faults to NIC-T0 links, T0-T1 links, or switch forwarding issues, even between training jobs. In production MRC/SRv6 environments, Clustermapper builds the link-health map used to localize faults, guide repairs, and inform path-avoidance policy, helping small teams turn many link failures from urgent incidents into scheduled maintenance.

### Beyond Traditional BGP: Decoupling Route Distribution for Large-Scale AI Clusters at Alibaba
Speakers: Eddie Ruan (Alibaba), Dennis Cai (Alibaba)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8868/
At OCP 2025, Microsoft introduced the Fairwater AI data center for large-scale AI clusters. By enabling SRv6 at the host, they limited the routing scale to under 1,000 routes with 256 ECMP paths. However, other cloud providers cannot easily mandate such host-side changes for diverse customers. In this talk, we want to present a new way to decouple service route distribution and infrastructure route distribution architecturally. For infrastructure routes, we still use regular BGP to distribute them within spine and leaf. For service routes, we use multiple RRs to redistribute them among leaf switches. These service routes are recursive over the loopback address of the leaf switch they are anchored to. Since we don't want service routes to be impacted by RR cluster outages, we introduce a Group RR concept， where multiple RRs operate as a coordinated group to enhance Graceful Restart capability and ensure high availability for critical service prefixes.

### Networking In Silo is Not Enough - Building End-to-End Automation for AI Factories - the IREN Use Case
Speakers: Amir Elbaz (BE Networks), Kim Kwa (IREN)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9711/ | materials: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7470468020813930496/
Large scale AI Factories are pushing the boundaries in many areas, however, they call for tighter and cross-functional collaborations. From physical layer infrastructure like power and cooling to compute and networking, these mega systems are expansive and complicated, but they call for nano-seconds reaction time for better performance and utilization of the billion dollars investments. Neoclouds either build their own stack to manage this varied infrastructure or collaborate with leading providers in the field. BE Networks partnered with IREN to help it build the end-to-end observability platform and network orchestration for its mega deployments of tens of thousands of GPUs. In this session IREN and BE will share lessons learned and the needs and wants from these software platforms that are needed to efficiently manage AI Factories.

## OCP Special Focus: Photonics

### Integrated Optics at Scale for AI Infrastructure: From 51.2T CPO Validation to Meta’s 102.4T Integrated Optics Switch
Speakers: Granthana Rangaswamy (Meta), Siamak Amiralizadeh (Meta), Xu Wang (Meta Platforms, Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10196/
As AI workloads drive unprecedented bandwidth demand, pluggable optics face power, density, and cost limits at 200G/lane. Integrated optics provide the benefit of lower power and good reliability by placing the optical engines directly onto the ASIC substrate (CPO) or near the ASIC substrate (NPO), eliminating pluggables. The Meta platform is a 1.5OU, 100% liquid-cooled, fanless blade with 64 optical ports via 16 four-port MMC interfaces and 16 ELSFP modules supplying external lasers — decoupling the laser lifecycle from ASIC. Key advantages: ~60% optics power reduction, ~30% system power reduction, 500m reach, and cost savings vs. pluggables. Fanless liquid cooling reduces fiber contamination risk. The 102.4T integrated optics development is built on previous generation design of 51.2T designs. Talk covers architecture, signal/power integrity of the high layer count PCB with optical design, thermal management.

### Optical Scale-Up AI Systems: Architectures, Standards, and Resiliency Strategies
Speakers: Fotini Karinou (Microsoft), Adrian Motamedi (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10157/
AI scale-up systems are driving unprecedented bandwidth and density requirements, making optical interconnect a foundational technology for next-generation infrastructure. Sustaining this scaling requires a full-stack approach — co-designing optical engines, interconnect architectures, form factors, and system integration — with open standards and OCP collaboration ensuring ecosystem alignment, interoperability across generations, and accelerated innovation. We share Microsoft's perspective on scale-up optical interconnects through that full-stack lens, then turn to a challenge it brings into focus: as optical integration deepens, reliability becomes an architectural problem. Unlike copper links, optical engines often share common laser sources and photonic components, creating larger failure domains where a single laser or PIC failure can affect multiple channels simultaneously. At the same time, system constraints increasingly favor dense single-connector interfaces, limiting opportunities to physically isolate redundant paths. We outline the design objectives this implies reducing failure blast radius, enabling graceful degradation rather than hard outages, preserving a single high-density connector interface, and maintaining compatibility with current DR4 optics, future optical engines, and electrical or optical fallback paths — and where coordinated industry work is needed to get there.

### Lessons learned from GW-scale AI deployments
Speakers: Mark Filer (Oracle)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9686/
When you’re training at the scale of 10,000+ GPUs, the "laws of large numbers" become your primary adversary. Single link flaps or other network instabilities can stall a multi-million dollar training job at huge expense to the AI cloud operator. We'll share some of the hard-won operational learnings from OCI ’s large-scale deployments supporting its top AI customers, including some production statistics on one of the world's largest deployments of 800G 2xDR4 LPO transceivers and how that translates to deployments at 1.6T. We'll additionally explore some forward-looking paths toward achieving high uptime and resilience in massive-scale distributed systems.

### Revolutionizing Networking for Gigawatt AI Factories
Speakers: Gilad Shainer (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8853/ | materials: https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/networking/products/silicon-photonics/
NVIDIA Co-packaged optics (CPO) switches are the world’s most advanced networking solution for the era of agentic AI. They integrate optics innovations, including the first 1.6T CPO chip based on new micro-ring modulators, 3-D-Stacked silicon photonics engines, high-power lasers, and detachable fiber connectors. The CPO switches deliver 3.5x more power efficiency and 5x better AI uptime compared with traditional methods. NVIDIA co-invented switch photonics with an ecosystem of partners and designed it to fit into the OCP infrastructure managed by the Sonic operating system. Contributions to Sonic are being made to adjust it to the new CPO model. NVIDIA CPO-based networks simplify manageability and design, and enable greater compute capacity. These benefits are critical to delivering the scale needed to enter the future of million-GPU AI Factories. With NVIDIA CPO switches moving into mass production, the session will also review its manufacturing process in more detail.

### The Interconnect Inflection Point: Evolving Intra- and Multi-Rack Scale-Up AI Networks
Speakers: Akarsh Bhatnagar (Celestica)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9319/
The hyper-scaling of AI/ML clusters has made scale-up networking the primary architectural bottleneck in modern data centers. At 200Gbps/lane and beyond, traditional copper (DAC/AEC) suffers from severe signal attenuation, while legacy air-cooled optics hit absolute density and thermal limits at 1.6T/3.2T. To mitigate unsustainable power overhead on next-gen 102.4T/204.8T switching silicon, this paper explores the industry's pivot toward system-level frameworks using open standards like XPO, Open CPX, and OCI. We analyze XPO as a versatile, modular form factor bridging the gap between front-panel pluggables and on-board architectures (CPO/NPO) and how its footprint can flexibly host DAC, AEC, LPO/LRO, FRO, or coherent ZR engines tailored to cluster tiers. Additionally, we evaluate emerging technologies like MicroLED optical engines and Active Radio Cables (ARCs) within next-gen fabric topologies to provide a definitive roadmap balancing density, serviceability, and thermal efficiency

### System-Level Evaluation of XPO for High-Density Optical Interconnects in AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Daniel Mohaghegh (Microsoft), Sunil Priyadarshi (Arista Networks)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9736/
The XPO (eXtra-dense Pluggable Optics) form factor is an emerging approach to enabling high-density optical interconnects for next-generation AI systems. Developed through collaboration across system vendors and optical module ecosystems, XPO targets improvements in integration density, power efficiency, and system flexibility. This work presents a joint evaluation of XPO based on system-level testing across multiple SerDes implementations and optical module types, including LPO, LRO, and FRO. Using a controlled test matrix, we analyze key metrics such as signal integrity, bit error rate (BER), and sensitivity to channel conditions, providing insight into interoperability across devices and link configurations. This work emphasizes measured system behavior and engineering observations, positioning XPO as an exploratory platform for evaluating next-generation optical architectures and identifying key areas for continued ecosystem collaboration.

### Open NPO Standards: Building the Optical Interconnect Ecosystem for SuperPOD
Speakers: Weiqiang Xu (Huawei), Qirui Fan (Huawei)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9402/
This presentation addresses high-speed electrical interconnect bottlenecks in AI computing centers, and how our company advances the NPO near-packet optical interconnect project to support 6.4T cluster upgrades. Our NPO solution under the super-node architecture covers key technical specifications and the construction of a single-channel 200 Gb/s system. Key technologies include silicon photonics integration, linear direct drive, near-packet substrates, and built-in light sources. Test results and challenges encountered during NPO implementation in super node products are discussed. Prototype verification and interoperability demonstrations confirm a feasible engineering solution. Future work includes refining the 6.4T specification, performing multi-vendor interoperability and reliability tests, and advancing commercial deployment from 2026 to 2027 to build an open and compatible super-node optical interconnect ecosystem.

### Light on the Horizon: Architecting Scale Up Fabrics with NPO & CPO for the Next Gen Data Center
Speakers: Chris Blackburn (Astera Labs), Jignesh Shah (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9758/
AI system architectures continue to scale up across the row and copper architectures, which have anchored switch fabrics for decades, are facing severe limitations in reach and power efficiency, threatening the scaling of massive compute clusters to next generation speeds. This presentation outlines the inevitable migration toward optical scale-up fabrics, focusing on the deployment readiness of Near-Packaged Optics (NPO) and Co-Packaged Optics (CPO). We will analyze how system architectures are evolving to accommodate these optical technologies, contrasting the structural changes against traditional copper-based layouts. The session will cover the impact on switch radix, row-level latency, and overall TCO. Additionally, we will discuss the open standards and interoperability frameworks necessary to transition NPO and CPO from bleeding-edge innovations into mainstream, serviceable OCP-compliant data center deployments.

### 400G/lane Optics Module Connectors
Speakers: Sam Kocsis (Amphenol), Terry Little (Foxconn Interconnect Technology), Robbie Xu (Luxshare-Tech), Matthew Ho (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10129/
400G/lane Optics Module Connectors

### Open Optical Connectivity Solutions for AI Scaling
Speakers: Rang-Chen (Ryan) Yu (TeraHop)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8551/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oGlOTFuARHk&t=177s | materials: https://www.ofcconference.org/program/special-events/dcs-panel-scaling-ai-clusters-challenges-in-scale-up-and-scale-out-for-future-growth/
AI compute infrastructure is growing fast, optical penetrates deeper into compute networking fabrics, especially xPU scale up domain with ~ 10x bandwidth needs vs. scale out. Open optical connectivity solutions are urgently needed to support optimal performance at large scale deployment. We will discuss recent progress in open solutions such as XPO MSA, Open CPX MSA, and SDM4 MCF MSA addressing AI scaling requirements such as high bandwidth capacity, high density, high reliability, serviceability, lower power, lower cost, supply resiliency with high volume manufacturability and availability.

### The Future of Coherent Optics in the AI Era
Speakers: Frank Chang (Ciena)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9652/
AI-driven traffic growth is accelerating demand for higher-capacity, more efficient optical networks, driving the industry toward 1.6T coherent pluggables and beyond. As coherent optics continue to evolve, network operators face important architectural decisions regarding power, density, reach, cooling, and spectral efficiency. This session will explore the diverging paths of coherent optics and examine where pluggables, transponders, and emerging architectures each deliver the greatest value. Topics will include 1.6T and 3.2T coherent innovations, liquid-cooling readiness, XPOs, full-spectrum transponders, and the network requirements shaping future deployments. Attendees will gain practical insight into how optical networks are evolving across multiple parallel vectors to meet the demands of the AI era.

### External Laser Source Architectures for Deployable CPO-Based AI Networks
Speakers: Christian Urricariet (Lumentum)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9550/
Co-packaged optics (CPO) is often introduced as a power-efficiency and bandwidth-density technology for AI data center networks. But as optical engines move closer to high-value switching and accelerator silicon, the deployment question broadens: how should systems be designed for availability, serviceability, thermal margin, and failure containment? This session examines the role of external laser source architectures, including ELS/ELSFP approaches, in that transition. By separating the laser source from the CPO package, external laser architectures can support faceplate serviceability, improved thermal isolation, and independent optimization of the laser platform while preserving CPO's low-power advantages. The presentation frames laser placement as a system architecture decision and discusses deployment metrics including reliability targets, MTTR, sparing, optical power delivery, thermal design, monitoring, qualification, and proven high-power laser-platform heritage.

### High-Speed Open Silicon Photonics Reference Designs
Speakers: Janos Veres (Flex), Bijan Nowroozi (Lightmatter)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9229/ | materials: https://lightmatter.co/press-release/lightmatter-announces-reference-architecture-initiative-with-industry-leaders-in-the-open-compute-project-for-co-packaged-optics/
As AI workloads push bandwidth, power, and integration limits, co-packaged optics (CPO) is emerging as a critical architecture, but ecosystem alignment remains a challenge. Open Silicon Photonics reference designs are critical to enable interoperability and accelerate adoption across the OCP community. In this session, Flex and Lightmatter will present results from a test vehicle built to verify high-speed Open Silicon Architectures for CPO. The test vehicle enables modular architectures with optical engines and laser sources to configure a variety of systems. We will share experimental validation and testing of passive alignment, connector and optical path testing, and system integration. These findings highlight the growing need for similar reference designs dedicated to specific features—like optical, electrical, and mechanical—to improve cross-supply chain collaboration and reduce integration risk.

Speakers: Oleg Shchekin (Lumentum), Ramin Farjadrad (Eliyan), Chris Kocot (Coherent)

Speakers: Rajiv Pancholy (Broadcom)

### CPO solution for AI that checks all the boxes
Speakers: Tom Palkert (Samtec)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9087/ | materials: https://www.ciena.com/insights/blog/2026/how-open-ecosystems-advance-cpo-adoption
Current networking technologies are not meeting demands of AI for power and density. Concurrently, the broad adoption of the most likely solution, Co-packaged Optical (CPO), in AI systems has proven difficult to achieve and has limited deployment to date. A group of AI companies has banded together to break the log jam and deliver networking bandwidth at unprecedented levels by fundamentally re-architecting data center interconnect. This presentation provides an in-depth exploration of the joint work accomplished by Meta, Ciena, Marvell, Terahop, Coherent and Samtec with a vision to enable massive connectivity via a revolutionary CPO solution that meets AI requirements across all vectors - low power, high density, high bandwidth, field replaceability, and copper compatibility.

### Photonics for AI – Aligning the Stack from Components to Cloud
Speakers: Stephen Manley (Oracle)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9586/
As AI workloads surge, photonics are becoming essential to overcome limits in bandwidth, power, and scalability. This panel brings together a hyperscaler, networking vendor, module supplier, and component innovator to examine how each layer is evolving. The discussion will explore AI data center architectures, the shift toward optics-rich fabrics, and trade-offs between pluggable and co-packaged optics. Panelists will address power, thermal, cost, and reliability challenges, along with supply chain readiness and standardization. Spanning the full ecosystem, this session will highlight how cross-layer innovation in photonics is enabling scalable, efficient, and reliable AI infrastructure—and where greater alignment is still needed.

## OIF Workshop

### OIF Workshop – Enabling an Open, Scalable, Sustainable AI Infrastructure: Welcome, Introduction and OIF Overview
Speakers: Nathan Tracy (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10287/
Objectives: - Align on the key demands and challenges of AI infrastructure including exploding bandwidth needs, power and thermal constraints, high speed signal integrity, latency, and the risk of industry fragmentation - Understand the OIF’s role in the development of interconnect standards and how these activities strengthen the industry ecosystem - Hear from industry luminaries on the impact of innovative OIF implementation agreements, interoperability specifications and demonstrations - Engage with industry leaders to enable a scalable AI infrastructure This workshop will address the OIF projects recently completed and currently underway to enable electrical and optical architectures and solutions as well as control interfaces between them. This will include 224Gbps and 448Gbps electrical channel specifications, next-generation optical channel specifications, CPO and NPO based architectures, external laser packaging, next-generation high density electrical interconnects, coherent optics, and common management interface firmware methodology.

### Defining Electrical 448G/Lane: OIF’s Work on Next-Generation Interfaces
Speakers: Nathan Tracy (TE Connectivity), Yi Tang (Cisco), Cathy Liu (Broadcom Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10289/
Driving Development for Next Generation 448G per Lane Electrical Link Applications, Nathan Tracy, OIF President, TE Connectivity - This session will focus on the opportunities and challenges of a next-generation 448G per-lane data rate. CEI-448G Channels: Where Does the Margin Go? , Yi Tang, OIF Board Member and PLL Working Group Electrical Vice Chair, Cisco - This session examines how loss, discontinuities, reflections, crosstalk, and skew affect the usable bandwidth and reach of CEI-448G VSR and LR channels. CEI-448G: Optimizing SerDes Signaling, Signal Processing and FEC Across 448G Reach Topologies, Cathy Liu, OIF Board Member, Broadcom Inc. - This presentation addresses the SerDes architecture, DSP signal processing, and coding strategy across the full spectrum of OIF CEI-448G reach definition - from Extra Short Reach (XSR) to Long Reach (LR) and linear interfaces.

### From Package to DCI: OIF Enablers in Oracle Cloud and AI Networks
Speakers: Mark Filer (Oracle)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10290/
Oracle operates cloud and AI infrastructure at global scale, where attributes like power, bandwidth density, reach, and interoperability increasingly shape network architecture. This talk will cover application of technologies like LPO, LRO (RTLR), NPO/CPO, and ZR in Oracle’s networks, and how OIF implementation agreements and interoperability work help enable these ecosystems. We’ll also provide an operator’s perspective on what's needed to translate emerging interconnect technologies into reliable, multi-vendor deployment at scale.

### Energy Efficient Interfaces: OIF is Enabling the Next Generation of AI
Speakers: Jeffrey Hutchins (Ranovus), Sam Kocsis (Amphenol)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10292/
Foundations of Energy Efficient Interfaces (EEI) for AI: Defining COI and RTLR, Jeff Hutchins, OIF PLL Working Group Energy Efficient Interfaces Vice Chair, Ranovus - This session will explore the development of dense, reliable, and energy-efficient interconnect standards for AI, focusing on the evolving needs of large-scale AI compute systems. Scaling EEI Interconnects: NPO Trends, High-Density Connectors, and the Value of Standardization, Sam Kocsis, OIF Technical Committee Chair, Amphenol - This session uses OIF's High Density Connector Framework as a vehicle to discuss physical form factor constraints, emerging NPO architectures, and related technical tradeoffs. The discussion will highlight why standardization is critical to interoperability, supply chain scalability, and total cost of ownership - offering attendees a clear view of where the industry is heading.

### Coherent Optics and Management for the AI Era: OIF is Enabling Interoperability for Real-World Networks
Speakers: Ian Betty (Ciena), Steven Hand (Nokia of America Corporation), Jeffery Maki (HPE), Tom Issenhuth (Huawei)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10293/
Driving Coherent Optics Solutions for AI Infrastructure, Ian Betty, OIF Board Member, Ciena Simplifying Network Management for Optical Subsystems, Steve Hand, OIF Board Member, Nokia - This session reviews work on the OIF Common Management Interface Specification (CMIS), firmware lifecycle management, security, higher-speed management interfaces, and operational interoperability to address these challenges. Enabling Next Generation Optical Transceiver Operation in Real-World Networks, Jeff Maki, OIF Board Member and Physical Layer User Group Working Group Chair, HPE - We will review how SI parameters are adjusted for host “plug and play” by the choice of host electrical interface ID for refined, half-retimed and linear interfaces. Proving Interoperability: How OIF Validates Technology in the Real World , Tom Issenhuth, OIF Market Awareness & Education Committee Co-Chair, Physical & Link Layer, Huawei - We will highlight how OIF actively validates and tests these capabilities and many other OIF technology Implementation Agreements (IAs) through real-world interoperability demonstrations and plugfest tests.

## Open Platform Firmware (OPF)

### Open Platform Firmware (OPF) - project update
Speakers: Murugasamy Nachimuthu (Oracle), Raj Kapoor (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9640/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/open-system-firmware
As the platform continues to evolve to support modern technologies, continuous improvements in the area of platform firmware are needed to enable the eco-system to deliver these new technologies. The Open Platform Firmware (OPF) project is making significant progress to address these challenges. This session will provide updates on the OPF project accomplishments, milestones and the additional initiatives under consideration to improve firmware development, deployment, security and addressing new technologies. The session also provides OPF tracks overview and guidelines.

### openSFI Update: Advancing Silicon Firmware Interoperability
Speakers: Allison Goodman (TBD), Raj Kapoor (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9863/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/open-system-firmware
Join the openSFI community for a milestone update on the industry’s effort to standardize the interface between silicon and host firmware. This session will cover the latest progress in the openSFI specification, including the evolution of the execution model, initialization stages, and the API framework that enables firmware portability across platforms and silicon vendors. Attendees will gain insight into stage-specific APIs, standardized input and output structures, configuration and handoff mechanisms, and the architectural principles that promote interoperability while preserving vendor innovation. The presentation will also highlight ecosystem collaboration, proof-of-concept activities, and the roadmap toward future milestones. Whether you are a silicon provider, firmware developer, platform manufacturer, or cloud operator, this session offers an opportunity to learn how openSFI is helping build a more open, portable, and scalable firmware ecosystem.

### Advancing Flash-less Boot in OCP Systems: A Streaming Firmware Architecture for Secure and Scalable Infrastructure
Speakers: Munir Ahmad (Lattice Semiconductor), Alex Cervantes (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9791/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-recovery-document-1p1-final-pdf
This session presents a streaming (flash-less) boot architecture aligned with OCP initiatives to address firmware-management complexity across datacenter systems. Today's platforms span firmware across compute, storage, network, and power, programmed at multiple stages, complicating version control and security. Streaming boot replaces per-device flash with centralized delivery, anchoring trust in immutable ROM and the platform Root of Trust (RoT). Firmware is streamed at boot via BMC-managed interfaces and authenticated at each stage, extending the chain of trust aligned with OCP Hardware Secure Boot and recovery specifications. Using PLDM over MCTP for out-of-band delivery improves scalability, fleet visibility, and consistency while removing flash-related attack surfaces and provisioning overhead. We outline the boot sequence, BMC/RoT integration, and impacts on supply chain and lifecycle, enabling software-defined, composable firmware infrastructure for OCP's next-gen platform

### From Code to Data: Advancing the Unified Platform Configuration Interface (UPCI)
Speakers: Felix Polyudov (AMI), Tim Lewis (Insyde Software)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9464/
Build-time. Boot-time. Run-time. Frustratingly, multiple standards, frameworks and vendor-specific tooling speak to how the firmware is configured: how is it created, how it initializes the hardware and how it interacts with local and remote users. What if these basic firmware behaviors could depend on a single source of truth that is expressed in ways that make sense in the environment where it is used? This session offers example-filled updates on how the Unified Platform Configuration Interface(UPCI) workstream addresses common firmware configuration scenarios, describes configurations in both human- and machine-readable formats and uniquely, intercepts other OCP workstreams like openSFI and M-PnP for silicon initialization and inter-firmware communication. Attendees will gain insight into how UPCI simplifies platform customization, reduces ecosystem fragmentation, and lays the foundation for multi-vendor collaboration and richer configuration tooling in the OCP community.

### Decoupling Silicon Dependencies from Control Plane Firmware
Speakers: Sarathy Jayakumar (AMD), Rama Bhimanadhuni (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9059/ | materials: https://github.com/AMDESE/OpenBMC/tree/master/meta-amd/meta-common/recipes-amd/dynamic-platform-abstraction
A growing class of firmware increasingly runs under a general-purpose OS, such as Embedded Linux, and must stay running, such as BMCs, DPUs, switch NOS, storage and accelerator controllers. Yet we still integrate silicon-vendor functionality using a model built around a monolithic firmware image. The consequence is expensive coupling: a change in vendor-specific functionality can force an entire image to be rebuilt, requalified, redeployed across the fleet, and the device rebooted. When the platform runs continuously, why should the entire firmware image remain the unit of change? Using the BMC as a concrete case, we examine how to break this coupling across heterogeneous hardware without sacrificing vendor interoperability. We separate three concerns that are commonly treated as one: semantic contract, binding, and delivery. A common semantic contract, with OpenSFI as one example, defines the interoperability boundary across silicon vendors. Dynamic binding removes the requirement that vendor functionality be permanently composed into the image at build time. Binding and delivery can then evolve independently. The larger question extends beyond the BMC: if firmware has evolved into a service platform, should its software architecture evolve with it?

### Building Bridges for Adoption: OCP, Standards Bodies, and Open‑Source Firmware
Speakers: Michael Kinney (Intel), Dong Wei (ARM), Christian Walter (9elements)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9923/
The OCP workstreams are a great place to collaborate on interoperability specifications to ease integration of silicon products across the ecosystem. But the rate of adoption of these specifications also depends heavily on collaborations with industry standard specifications. openSFI and UCPI are no different. This panel will review the importance of leveraging existing specifications and how Open Platform Firmware work can help improve and strengthen existing standards. They will also discuss how we could improve collaboration with standards bodies and existing open-source FW communities to accelerate changes and additions to specifications in alignment with OCP specifications.

### Scaling Memory Safe Security Across Firmware Stack
Speakers: Giri Mudusuru (Microsoft), Tim Lewis (Insyde Software), Bertrand Achard (Google), Raj Kapoor (AMD), Oleg Ilyasov (American Megatrends (AMI))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9648/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/open-system-firmware
Deploying a secure platform at scale requires security measures at every step of the firmware: from design/development, release, manufacturing, transit to deployment and use. This panel will discuss the importance of memory safe firmware, preferred languages, scaling across the firmware stack, industry standard needs and eco-system readiness. The panel will discuss current industry and OCP community activities related to memory-safe firmware that are in progress. The panel will conclude with possible action items for OCP and industry standard bodies to help bring the memory safe firmware to reality.

### Trusted, AI-Ready Open Firmware: From Auditable Foundations to Agentic Lifecycle Automation
Speakers: Paul Grimes (AMD), Chitrak Gupta (American Megatrends (AMI))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10118/
Firmware complexity is outpacing traditional development, and nearly every engineer now uses AI assistants -- yet on open firmware, ungoverned AI is unreliable and hard to trust. This session connects two halves of one problem. First, making repositories AI-ready: shared, version-controlled context, firmware guardrails, and reusable review and CI-triage playbooks built on the AGENTS.md standard, so AI-assisted changes stay reviewable and reproducible. Second, what that foundation enables -- trusted agentic AI across the secure firmware lifecycle: generation, debugging, validation, security analysis, and SBOM for OpenBMC, Redfish, and UEFI on OCP platforms (DC-MHS, DC-SCM, mPNP), using local, sovereign models. Together, a path to AI that accelerates open firmware without surrendering the transparency, auditability, and security the ecosystem depends on. Examples span production openSIL firmware, with a proposal to contribute the shared foundation to OPF.

### The Next Evolution of Intel's FSP: Aligning to openSFI and UPCI
Speakers: Michael Kinney (Intel), Chad Tsai (intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9636/
Intel has been and continues to evolve their FSP for server platforms to streamline integration of common Intel silicon code with multiple bootloaders. Intel is investing to ensure we stay true to that goal as the silicon continues to grow in complexity and the ecosystem demands timely bootloader integrations and support. This presentation will share some of the latest changes to FSP to align with the goals of Open Platform Firmware development and the ongoing openSFI definition. Intel will introduce bootloader agnostic changes, unification to reuse code between API and Dispatch modes, and alignment with openSFI initialization stages.

## Rack & Power

### Rack and Power ( Opening and update)
Speakers: Steven Moore (Rittal), Harry Soin (Advanced Energy)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9220/
Harry and Steven to open and Provide: - Rack and Power update - Rack and Power Roadmaps - Update on open and ongoing projects - Call for action

### Open Rack Wide (ORW): Progress, Validation, and What's Next
Speakers: Cecilia Galvan (Meta), Richard Banyard (Rittal)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9145/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/open-rack-wide-orw-base-specification-v1-0-0-final-pdf
Open Rack Wide (ORW) v1.0 was formally contributed to OCP in April 2026 as a double-width, large form-factor rack standard for next-generation AI platforms. The contribution included a Base Spec plus a Meta Design Spec. In this joint session, Meta and Rittal pick up where that contribution left off. Meta shares the latest progress on ORW: refinements since the initial spec release, results from mechanical and structural validation, and the practical lessons of bringing a high-payload, double-width rack from spec toward deployment and launch. Rittal brings the manufacturing perspective: standing up a semi-automated production line for ORW, the production challenges encountered along with developed solutions, and the path toward further automation as volume scales. Together we'll cover what changed, what held up, and what it takes to move a wide-rack standard from open spec to a production-ready product.

### ORv3 Risk Assessment of Multi-degree of Freedom Transportation Vibration
Speakers: Ken Leung (Google), Eric Joneson (TransPak)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9859/
In this joint field evaluation between Google and Transpak, the behaviors and risks of OCP standard ORv3 racks are examined using state of the art portable vibration data acquisition and analysis systems, multiple 6 degrees of freedom accelerometers, stereo high speed cameras, GPS, and multiple Insta360 cameras instrumented inside and outside a typical cargo truck. Our working theory is that industry transportation vibration standards such as those published by ISTA and ASTM are insufficient for representing the real-world dynamic behavior and stress conditions experienced by the ORv3 rack, whether populated or unpopulated. The full test setup and procedure will be shared with the OCP community so that others can build up this comprehensive field evaluation method. The findings will also be officially submitted to ISTA standards council to guide future directions for transportation vibration standards and testing requirements for Unitized Loads.

### Scaling Hyperscale Power Delivery: Managing Aggregated and Cascaded Power Shelf Architectures for High-Density GPU Systems
Speakers: Praveen Kumar Velpuri (Microsoft), Yang Hsiung (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9946/
As hyperscale AI systems evolve toward large GPU clusters, rack power delivery is transitioning from single-shelf designs to aggregated multi-shelf architectures. While OCP standards enable modular power shelves with parallel scaling, emerging high-density systems introduce new challenges when multiple shelves are combined or cascaded across a rack. This session explores system-level considerations for managing aggregated and daisy-chain–like power shelf topologies, focusing on power balancing, coordination, and efficiency optimization. It highlights challenges in distribution imbalance, control-plane scaling, fault isolation, and dynamic load behavior across shelves. The discussion also covers opportunities to improve power utilization and reduce energy losses through coordinated shelf management, enabling more efficient and scalable power delivery for next-generation hyperscale GPU deployments.

### Powering the AI Rack of the Future: A Scalable 800 V DC Sidecar Architecture
Speakers: Sven Finke (Bosch), Justin Mark Herrmann (Rittal)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9449/
Rising AI power demands require compact 800 V DC distribution. We present a sidecar PDU aligned with the OCP Mt. Diablo architecture and LVDC power distribution whitepaper that decouples power from compute, helping to preserve valuable GPU space. Based on high-density Power Module on Cooler (PMoC) packaging technology, the system uses compact modules and a scalable topology that supports expansion in power capacity and rack-level deployment. Blind-mate connectors and defined service interfaces support modular replacement with reduced downtime, while isolation strategies and connector design ensure touch-safe operation and servicing. A real-time Linux-based industrial controller dynamically scales power to match fluctuating GPU and rack loads, while providing telemetry and native IoT integration. Simulations demonstrate dynamic performance under rapid load changes, indicating a scalable, serviceable, and safe 800 V DC solution for next-generation AI data centers.

### Enabling 1MW+ IT rack power with an open rack busbar interface
Speakers: Hal Loket (TE Connectivity), Aniket Singh (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9645/
This presentation introduces a high-voltage busbar mating interface designed to enable >1 MW power delivery at the IT rack level. The busbar design is agnostic to both 0–800V and ±400V implementations, enabling flexibility across evolving rack architectures. Developed in collaboration with hyperscalers & chip makers, the busbar optimizes thermal efficiency and increases current density to meet next‑generation rack power demands. The solution defines a touch safe interface, incorporating global design safety standards for high voltage, and includes a roadmap to reuse the OCP rack mounting interface for streamlined integration. The architecture can be scaled for various rack power needs while maintaining the same interface industry-wide.

### High-Power-Density BBU and CBU Design for Evolving AI Data Center Power Architectures
Speakers: Takuya Tsutsumi (Panasonic Corporation), Hank Tsuda (Panasonic Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9926/
As data centers expand and GPU power increases, power architectures are evolving toward higher-voltage distribution, sidecar configurations, and direct integration with high-voltage DC buses. At the same time, load fluctuation absorption is becoming an increasingly important function in power systems. In this session, we present the latest developments of Battery Backup Units (BBU) and Capacitor Bank Units (CBU). We introduce system-driven design approaches that translate performance and power requirements into optimized device and module specifications, where achieving both high power density and safety(UL9540A) is a key challenge. We focus on lithium-ion battery materials (LFP, NCM, and high-nickel) and discuss their characteristics and application strategies. We also highlight high-power-density CBU technologies and outline how material evolution and functional integration can support future data center power architectures.

### Orv3 Power architectures for Higher Power work loads
Speakers: Cihan Aydin (Delta Electronics Inc.), Harry Soin (Advanced Energy)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9219/
In this presentation, we will look at the requirements of next generation higher power ORv3 power supplies and HVDC power shelves for AI workloads, which will help increase rack payload and power density yet again, while supporting key design requirements ranging from hot swap ability to battery backup. Among the topics covered during the session will be an update on key design specifications and design considerations, as well as the most recent ORv3 technologies – including power supplies, power shelves, shelf controllers and battery backup solutions. We will also explore the next Generation Rack and Power roadmaps.

### Mitigating AI Training Power Oscillations: Utility Requirements and In-Rack Power Filtering
Speakers: Bo Wen (Microsoft), Xianyong Feng (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9738/
Large-scale AI training workloads create fast, repetitive power swings as GPUs transition between compute and communication phases. At cluster scale, these synchronized variations can overlap with sensitive utility-grid frequency bands, creating risks such as forced oscillations, voltage flicker, inter-harmonics, impedance-stability concerns, and high ramp rates. This talk connects utility power-transient requirements with practical in-rack mitigation. We will review grid-side constraints, including power-change and oscillation limits, ramp-rate expectations, and synchronized monitoring from utility interconnects to rack level. We will then present PSU + CBU filtering concepts, frequency-sweep test methods, attenuation analysis, and verification results showing how rack-level buffering can reduce upstream power ripple. The goal is to share a practical framework for aligning AI rack power design with utility requirements through measurement, modeling, and scalable in-rack filtering.

### Simplifying High-Density Deployments: Reducing AC Whip Complexity in Multi-Power Shelves Open Rack Architectures
Speakers: Fabricio Alves Borges (Schneider Electric)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9184/
As high-density AI workloads drive ORV3 adoption, powering multiple 33kW/72kW power shelves requires excessive power whips, causing cabling complexity and brownfield AC incompatibilities. This session introduces an open, modular AC PDU supporting multiple independent modules with blade-level short-circuit protection, optimizing the layer between AC facility and the ORV/ORW integrated racks. Accelerating open standards requires solving real-world deployment friction to a wide range of customers. By using this AC PDU for power shelves, a reduction of power whips per rack can be achieved which resolves space constraints and input mismatches without cost-prohibitive facility overhauls. It provides enterprise and hyperscale operators a practical bridge to ORV3 ecosystems, advancing OCP tenets of compatibility, efficiency, and scalability.

### Scaling OCP for Supercomputing: The Cray GX Infrastructure
Speakers: Javier Izquierdo (HPE), Brian Purcell (HPE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9215/
The HPE Cray GX infrastructure extends Open Compute Project principles into large-scale supercomputing. Built on a 28-inch-wide architecture, it can support standard 21-inch OCP Open Rack v3 components for compute, networking, and power, while also providing enhanced power delivery, cooling, serviceability, and management for demanding HPC and AI workloads that exceed ORv3 capability. Its OCP-based bus bar and modular DLC power shelf approach helps address rapidly changing GPU transient power needs. The GX5000 platform is silicon-vendor agnostic, supporting AMD, NVIDIA, Intel, and other processor and accelerator roadmaps without redefining the infrastructure each generation. This flexibility is reflected in deployments such as Discovery at Oak Ridge and Mission at Los Alamos, while innovations in liquid cooling, high-current delivery, and heterogeneous blade support enable next-generation supercomputing environments.

### MGX Power Rack Reference Design
Speakers: Jared Huntington (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9913/
With compute rack power densities continuing to increase power racks are a necessary near term solution for enabling higher power density compute racks. Power racks are also key for enabling N+N redundancy and BBU backup. To solve these problems Nvidia has put together the MGX power rack reference design. This presentation will give an overview of the electrolytic capacitors used for power smoothing, BBU solution, and path forward for 100% liquid cooled compute and power racks.

### 800V direct-to-server and power shelf solutions
Speakers: Harry Soin (Advanced Energy), Andy Brown (AEI - Advanced Energy)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9217/
As Mount Diablo architecture takes hold, a lot of emphasis is being placed on the development and deployment of +/-400V and 800V power solutions. Advanced Energy is at the forefront of developing advanced high voltage solutions that increase overall system efficiency and improve the reliability of compute and storage applications in hyperscale and enterprise data centers. In this presentation, AEI will dig deep into the deployment of both direct-to-server and power shelf solutions that will be needed in an HVDC infrastructure. The presentation will cover various design approaches including the challenges of realizing safety-approved application solutions that are thermally optimized for liquid-cooled next-generation rack-mounted equipment at both rack-mounted sub-rack level and at PCB level when deployed into an HVDC eco-system and will address the concerns of Hot-swap, hold-up time, EMI and possible pollution degree aspects of application design.

### 800V Data Center DC/DC Design Considerations, Topology Comparisons, and Trade-offs
Speakers: David Reusch (Texas Instruments)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9276/ | materials: https://www.ti.com/lit/ta/ssztdb1/ssztdb1.pdf?ts=1760316959192?hqs=multi-tech-data-gridtogate_dce-pr--null-ww_en | video: https://youtu.be/GVfBzr6W7oU?si=AGqBzmvfiDzsqp0m
As data center power demands soar, high-efficiency, high-density power conversion solutions are more critical than ever. This presentation explores DC-DC design considerations for 800V high-voltage DC (HVDC) power architectures. We will highlight core topologies, key trade-offs, enabling technologies, and practical lessons learned from real-world design examples. Mapping the path from an 800V bus to a sub-1V load, we analyze how intermediate bus voltages and the number of conversion stages impact overall system efficiency and density. Finally, we compare key topologies for these stages, highlighting their strengths, weaknesses, and suitability for regulated vs. unregulated architectures and modular vs. chip-down discrete designs.

### Applying OCP ORv3 and ORW Design Principles to Rack-Scale Arm AI Infrastructure with Supermicro
Speakers: Marc Meunier (Arm), Daniel Chen (Supermicro)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8982/
