Agentic AI is pushing infrastructure design up to the rack level, where density, power delivery, cooling, and serviceability matter as much as raw compute. In this session, Arm and Supermicro will show how they are applying OCP ORv3 design principles to Arm AGI CPU-based infrastructure for modern AI data centers. The session will cover practical engineering choices around rack-level density, power and thermal design, modularity, and serviceability, and explain how those choices support scalable AI deployments across both air-cooled and liquid-cooled environments. Attendees will leave with a practical view of how OCP-aligned rack design can improve deployment flexibility and data center economics for next-generation AI infrastructure.

### The Power Distribution sub-project at OCP - Low Voltage DC Distribution in Data Centers
Speakers: Paolo Catapane (ABB), JP Buzzell (Bitdeer)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9386/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/dcf-power-distribution-lvdc-white-paper-version-1-0-final-pdf-1
The data center industry faces growing needs for efficiency and scalability. Launched in October 2025, the OCP Power Distribution sub-project released its “Low Voltage Direct Current (LVDC) Distribution for data centers” white paper on March 31, 2026. This framework outlines how LVDC distribution can reduce conversion stages, boosting efficiency and power density while reducing complexity. Key focus areas include aligning the value chain on interoperability, analyzing grid architectures and earthing options, and addressing safety, protection, and regulatory standards like NEC/IEC. Additionally, the project collaborates with the OCP Rack & Power project to define interfaces with IT racks and sets high-level requirements for main components and control principles. This work provides a foundational resource for LVDC-powered data centers.

## SONiC Workshop

### SONiC Community Year in Review: Powering the Open Network for the AI Era
Speakers: Yanzhao Zhang (Microsoft), Xin Liu (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10257/
Over the past year, the SONiC community has delivered one of the most significant feature expansions in the project's history. This session presents a community-wide update spanning the last twelve months, with contributions from more than fifteen organizations and 80+ tracked features. We will walk through the themes shaping SONiC's roadmap. Attendees will leave understanding where SONiC stands today and how it is becoming the open, hardened foundation for AI-era data centers.

### SONiC BMC for Unified Management and Next-Generation Liquid-Cooled Networks
Speakers: Judy Joseph (Microsoft), Roger Liao (Nexthop AI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10260/
Liquid-cooled network racks are becoming widely adopted to meet the power and thermal density requirements driven by AI and high-performance workloads. SONiC BMC extends the role of SONiC, the OS for network devices, to encompass the baseboard management controller and provide integrated management and monitoring even when the switch host crashes or freezes. Containerized architecture and management interfaces like gNMI provide a unified experience across the switch host and BMC. Leak detection, remote power control, and console monitoring enable the operator to quickly respond to leaks, reduce on-site visits, and debug unresponsive switches. Redfish APIs allow SONiC BMC and the rack manager to coordinate on telemetry and rack-level alerts to control liquid flow and address rack level leaks. SONiC BMC provides the foundation for operators to manage liquid-cooled networking platforms today and is built to evolve to handle the future challenges of rack optimization and fast remediation. Special thanks to contributors Chinmoy Dey, Software Engineer, Nexthop.ai and Ying Xie, Microsoft

### Formal Methods in SONiC: Public Infrastructure and Advances
Speakers: Mengqi Liu (Alibaba), Ali Kheradmand (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10261/
Traditional hand-written unit tests struggle to cover the deep, nested state of SONiC components. The SONiC Formal Methods Working Group takes a different approach: write an abstract executable model as the spec, auto-generate high-coverage test cases via symbolic execution, and execute them against the real C implementation. This session covers our open-sourced infrastructure, produced test cases, and on-going work. We will demonstrate open-sourced infrastructure tackling two levels of tests. For unit tests, we show public modeling libraries in both Python and P4, with custom types and primitives targeting the routing domain. For multi-module interaction, we show a validation framework that leverages P-language models to enumerate schedules and compose atomic actions into diverse sequences. On top of that, three on-going projects are validating various aspects of SONiC artifacts. They cover bgpd’s malformed packet handling, the BGP-LS protocol, and the BGP graceful restart mechanism. They all utilize formal models to generate comprehensive test cases. In addition, we demonstrate SAI models and generated test cases covering the low-level interfaces in the SONiC stack.

### Democratizing DPU High Availability with a SONiC-Driven DASH HA Control Plane
Speakers: Changrong Wu (Microsoft), Sudharsan Dhamal Gopalarathnam (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10262/
High availability is a critical requirement for deploying DPUs in production cloud infrastructure, yet the existing implementations rely on proprietary, DPU-driven control planes. This vendor-specific model limits hardware choice, complicates multi-vendor deployment, and creates a significant barrier for emerging DPU vendors. To address this gap, we developed an open-source, disaggregated DASH High-Availability control plane as part of the SONiC SmartSwitch project. The implementation is included in the SONiC 202605 community release and moves HA orchestration from proprietary DPU software into a common, SONiC-managed control plane daemon. In this session, we will present the architecture of the SONiC-driven DASH HA solution and compare its white-box approach with traditional black-box, DPU-driven HA implementations. We will explain how SONiC coordinates HA state management and failover operations across DPUs, identify the SAI abstractions required by the architecture and discuss the API extensions needed in DPU implementations.

### Building AI Data Center Networks with SONiC​ : Key Insights from a Cisco Customer Pilot Journey
Speakers: Siddesh Nayak (Cisco), Bhavani Parise (Cisco)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9747/
Join Cisco as we share our journey to solve one of the most pressing challenges in AI infrastructure today: building AI data center networks that scale efficiently, perform predictably and remain operationally sustainable. As AI workloads drive unprecedented demands on scale and performance, data center fabrics must evolve—and open networking is central to that path forward. In this engineering-focused session, we present a candid account of designing, deploying, and operating a SONiC-based AI networking fabric. Intended towards anyone evaluating SONiC for AI fabrics, operating infrastructure, or contributing to the open networking ecosystem. We'll walk through the measurable benefits which open networking delivers, architectural decisions, telemetry/observability approaches, and operational concerns that emerged from customer deployment experience along with software lifecycle management and performance validation. We'll close with concrete opportunities for cloud providers, networking vendors, and the open-source community to advance SONiC for AI fabrics together—turning pilot lessons into community-driven specifications and shared best practices.

### Beyond the T2 PIN: Pizza-Box Disaggregation for Next-Gen Regional Hubs
Speakers: Kamini Santhanagopalan (Broadcom), Rita Hui (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9204/
As hyperscale cloud networks scale, traditional monolithic networking architectures introduce deployment bottlenecks and vendor lock-in. SONiC has successfully addressed these challenges at the Tier-2 data center layer by transitioning from modular chassis-based systems to an open, disaggregated "pizza-box" architecture. This session explores how this disaggregated design methodology can be extended to higher-layer networks, specifically the regional gateway routers that provide massive connectivity between data centers within a geographic region. We will discuss how SONiC can power the regional gateway layer in hyperscale networks, enabling simplified operations, improved scalability, flexibility and network resiliency, and significant cost savings across data center operations. Audience will be able to understand advantages of disaggregation, simplified design.

### Architecting Autonomous Operations for OCP Hardware: An Agentic AI Pipeline for SONiC
Speakers: Arun Solleti (Celestica), Robert Covington (Celestica)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9149/
As network complexity increases, the demand for intelligent, autonomous operations on open platforms has never been higher. This session introduces an open-source, agentic AI pipeline designed natively for SONiC and OCP-compliant hardware, bridging the gap between raw telemetry and actionable network intelligence. By integrating real-time gNMI telemetry and configuration snapshots with deterministic fault rules and locally hosted LLMs, our solution delivers precise, automated root-cause analysis and remediation. Prioritizing on-premise data sovereignty, this modular solution reduces reliance on proprietary systems. This approach provides a foundation for robust, AI-ready infrastructures, paving the way for fully autonomous configuration management and maximizing the long-term value of open networking standards.

### 6–12x Route Programming Acceleration in SONiC: A Collaborative Profiling and Optimization Journey
Speakers: Deepak Singhal (Microsoft), Amol Rawal (Nokia)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9833/ | materials: https://github.com/sonic-net/SONiC/pull/2267
SONiC's default route programming pipeline delivers 2K–4K routes/sec — insufficient for datacenter fabrics with 1M+ route scale requirements. Multiple optimization proposals existed in the community but no methodology to determine which matter or how they interact. This session presents a joint Microsoft–Nokia effort where we built a Snappi-based benchmarking framework and profiled each optimization and their combinations on multiple hardware platforms to identify the key contributors. Beyond profiling, we contributed fresh optimizations: replacing Redis-based IPC with direct ZMQ communication for regular routers, offloading synchronous recording from the hot path, and integrating tcmalloc into FRR daemons — together delivering 6–12x acceleration. We drove consensus in the SONiC Routing Working Group, published HLDs, and coordinated implementation across multiple sonic-net repositories — all open-source.

### Plug-and-Play ML Pipelines for SONiC: On-Device Intelligence with Advanced Feature Extraction
Speakers: Venkata Ravichandra Mynidi (Marvell Technology), Abed Mohammad Kamaluddin (Marvell Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10263/
SONiC has become the dominant open NOS for enterprise and datacenter switching — and now needs to safely host ML inference workloads: Traffic Classifiers, Anomaly Scorers, and Telemetry Feature Extractors. This session presents a three-stage SONiC pipeline—ingest, extract, and publish—that transforms live data-plane state into model-ready features for various ML inference workloads. Running in an isolated container and using only standard SONiC interfaces, it ingests signals, computes features on-device, and publishes them on- or off-device. Forwarding-path integrity and NOS lifecycle guarantees are preserved, making feature extraction a first-class SONiC workload for on- and off-device inference. Framework Stages 1. Telemetry Ingestion — Redis COUNTERS_DB, IPFIX and sFlow data behind a common event bus 2. Feature Extraction — Windowing, Normalization, and Transforms produce ML-ready feature vectors from raw device telemetry 3. Feature Publish — Routes vectors to on-device or off-device inference per application policy and latency needs Key Takeaway A framework-agnostic, placement-flexible ML pipeline for SONiC, from Enterprise Edge to Datacenter-Scale Fabrics

### Software-Assisted Congestion Signaling and Sub-Millisecond Telemetry in SONiC
Speakers: Arpitha Raghunandan (Google), Shane Gao (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10264/
As AI workload demands push network utilization to the limit, granular observability is vital but historically constrained by hardware pipelines. This session presents a novel SONiC architecture, proven at Google scale, framed around a core telemetry framework and a powerful in-band signaling scheme built on top of it: 1. High-Speed Switch Telemetry (HST): Delivers scalable fabric observability via lightweight digests and high-resolution traces, enabling microsecond-scale profiling reported per device and upstreamed or polled at regular frequencies. 2. Software-Assisted Congestion Signaling (CSIG): Updates packet signals with real-time switch state, providing end-hosts with high-frequency per-packet telemetry. By leveraging the HST framework for sub-millisecond runtime write-backs of per-port metrics and utilizing unused match-action resources, this architecture provides flexibility to incorporate new custom signal types not inherently supported by hardware implementations. It further enables the adoption of cutting edge telemetry techniques like CSIG in existing networks with older ASICs.

### Extending Community SONiC for Advanced Optics Telemetry Aligned with OCP Standards
Speakers: Rajan Pai (Credo), Prince George (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9812/
As AI networks scale to unprecedented densities, real-time visibility into optical link health is critical for reliability and uptime. Community SONiC's well-defined extension points allow vendors to contribute custom optics features without disrupting existing platform behavior. This session presents a framework for contributing custom optics telemetry to Community SONiC, aligned with the OCP Optics Telemetry Software Specification. Using the Zero Flap Optic's CDB Telemetry Specification (v2.0) as a reference implementation, we demonstrate how custom CDB commands integrate into SONiC to enable real-time module health scoring, FEC statistics, in-module event logging, and remote firmware management. Attendees will gain a practical, replicable model for extending SONiC telemetry in a standards-aligned way — accelerating vendor collaboration toward a more interoperable optical layer for AI fabric networks.

### Unlocking Nanosecond Precision with P2P Transparent Clocks
Speakers: Bhaskar Chinni (Broadcom), Eric Lance (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10265/
Accurate time synchronization is critical for modern networks. This session provides a practical introduction to PTP transparent clocks, explaining the key differences between End-to-End (E2E) and Peer-to-Peer (P2P) approaches and their impact on timing accuracy and scalability. Attendees will learn how P2P transparent clocks can be configured through SAI and deployed in SONiC-based switches. The session will also cover performance measurement techniques, validation methods, and deployment best practices for achieving precise network synchronization.

### FIPS 140-3 Support in SONiC
Speakers: Kenneth Cheung (Arista Networks), Qi Luo (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9072/
This presentation provides an overview of the latest FIPS revision 140-3 as applicable to both software and hardware encryption engines deployed in open-source Network OS such as SONiC. It covers the constraints imposed by the standard on the control and operation of the encryption function and how that translates into the architecture of the corresponding software/firmware modules, and how it is integrated into an OS like SONiC to provide a compliant implementation. We will show how to use OpenSSL with SymCrypt as the provider for core cryptographic operations in SONiC software stack. We will also show at the dataplane level, how a hybrid module — comprising a software MACsec shared library and a hardware System On a Chip with MACsec engines — plugs into the SONiC Network OS to enable FIPS 140-3 certification.

## Security

### An Introduction to Attesting Hitlessly Updatable Systems
Speakers: Matt King (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8585/
Hitless firmware updates let a platform activate new firmware without interrupting a running tenant workload, improving availability while enabling rapid deployment of security fixes. They also complicate attestation: conventional evidence describes the trusted computing base (TCB) at the moment evidence is produced, but an updatable platform can change before the relying party uses it. Trusting such a system therefore requires more than measurements of the firmware that has run so far; a relying party must also assess whether future updates could compromise the security properties it depends on. By combining evidence of the current TCB, the history of prior updates, and the policy that governs future updates, attestation lets relying parties make an informed decision about whether to trust a system that can change while it runs.

### Integrated HSM: Bringing Silicon-Level Key Security to the Server Tray
Speakers: Craig Barner (Marvell), Vishal Soni (Microsoft), Mohan Kumar (Oracle Cloud Infrastructure)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9098/ | materials: https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/azure-integrated-hsm-new-chaptershift-from-centralized-clusters-to-embedded-sili/4462923
As AI and confidential compute workloads scale, the centralized key vault model introduces latency, bottlenecks, and an expanded attack surface. Azure's Integrated HSM initiative shifts trust to a dedicated security chip inside the server - a local hardware anchor co-located with the workload. This session introduces a new OCP standardization effort defining the iHSM - a FIPS 140-3 Level 3 module and interoperable server tray building block. It delivers faster crypto, no network egress, hardware-enforced isolation, and tamper-resistant key protection. For long-term keys, a cloud HSM such as Marvell's LiquidSecurity, serves as the key custodian - provisioning keys to the iHSM, the authorized local holder and user at the workload. The reference architecture covers attestation-based provisioning, lifecycle management, and a trust model extending from the remote vault to the local tray - supporting sovereignty, data residency, and compliance.

### Endorsements for Attestation: A Common Language for the Whole Supply Chain
Speakers: Paul Howard (Arm), Shefali Kamal (Fujitsu)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9131/ | materials: https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-ietf-rats-coserv-06.html
Trust in computing platforms is increasingly based on attestation. Endorsements and Reference Values are essential artefacts for attestation evidence appraisal. They can originate from various sources throughout the supply chain, including silicon manufacturers, hardware integrators, firmware providers, and software providers. Their distribution is influenced by technical, commercial, and even geopolitical factors. The consumers of these artefacts include cloud-hosted verification services, local verifiers bundled with applications, constrained nodes, and endpoint devices. This diversity of producers and consumers creates challenges for software integration and poses fragmentation risks. Aligning on common interfaces will address these challenges and maximise software component reuse for artefact transfer between entities. In this talk, Arm and Fujitsu will share progress on the creation of a standard interface, and show how it is being implemented in the open source community.

### OCP Device Identity Provisioning over SPDM
Speakers: Fabrizio Damato (AMD), Xiaoyu Ruan (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9155/
Data-center operators and their tenants need to provision their own identity certificates to fleet hardware, independent of vendor-issued birth identities. Until now, SPDM lacked first-class primitives to discover device keypairs, request cryptographically-bound CSRs, and install operator or tenant PKI chains — forcing proprietary, per-vendor integrations. The Device Identity Provisioning specification defines an Attested CSR that cryptographically binds a newly generated keypair to the device's attested identity and measured state, together with a companion keypair-discovery flow so enrollment authorities can enumerate the identity keys a device exposes. Today these flows ride on SPDM vendor-defined messages, giving early adopters a path to deploy. In parallel, OCP is driving this capability toward DMTF SPDM, where it is anticipated for addition in SPDM 1.5. Open-source RoTs such as Caliptra are expected to adopt the specification, enabling consistent operator and tenant enrollment.

### Future-proof security in TPM with SPDM and PQC capabilities
Speakers: Antonio Javier Cabrera Gutierrez (Infineon Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9180/
The rapid evolution of computing, particularly the emergence of quantum technologies, poses significant challenges to existing hardware root of trust architectures such as Trusted Platform Modules (TPMs). Traditional cryptographic primitives used in platform attestation, firmware protection, and secure communication are vulnerable to quantum attacks, necessitating a transition to quantum-resistant mechanisms. The Security Protocol and Data Model (SPDM) provide a standardized framework for device authentication, attestation, and secure session establishment, enabling confidentiality and integrity across heterogeneous platforms. This presentation explores how TPMs can leverage SPDM-based secure channels and PQC-enabled operations like attestation mechanisms to provide long-term resilience, mitigate “harvest-now, decrypt-later” risks, and enable crypto-agile platform security architectures in future datacenters architectures.

### OCP L.O.C.K. and FIPS
Speakers: Gwangbae Choi (Samsung Electronics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9341/
As regulatory expectations tighten across hyperscale and enterprise environments, FIPS compliance is becoming a critical requirement for deploying trusted infrastructure. This session explores how OCP L.O.C.K.—a key management architecture within Caliptra with its own stringent security requirements for media encryption keys—can be aligned with FIPS cryptographic module expectations. We focus on key differences from a FIPS validation perspective between Caliptra and OCP L.O.C.K. We discuss how these gaps are, and should be, addressed to meet certification requirements and share insights from ongoing compliance efforts. This talk highlights the importance of bridging open security architectures with formal validation frameworks, enabling certifiable, scalable, and trustworthy deployment of secure storage solutions.

### Advancing Data Center Trust: Open Standards for Attestation, Device Identity, and Post-Quantum Readiness
Speakers: Shiva Dasari (HPE), Jeff Andersen (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9369/ | materials: https://trustedcomputinggroup.org/resource/dice-concise-evidence-binding-for-spdm/
The presentation will explore how Device Identifier Composition Engine (DICE) architectures and TPM‑over‑SPDM integration can improve interoperability and operational efficiency for attestation across varied infrastructure. It will also discuss recent advancements enabling Trusted Platform Modules (TPMs) to support post‑quantum cryptographic algorithms and migration strategies while preserving long‑term platform trust.

### Upgrading OCP S.A.F.E. and S.O.L.I.D. for the AI age
Speakers: Zahra Lak (Microsoft), Jeff Andersen (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9380/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-Security-SAFE
S.A.F.E. defines how data center products are reviewed for security. S.O.L.I.D. defines security requirements for them. With the recent advances in AI security reviews, these programs needed to be updated to continue ensuring products are secure. In this talk we present our experience with AI security reviews, how S.A.F.E. and S.O.L.I.D. have been updated and what benefits this brings to the industry.

### Advancing Caliptra Security Through Open-Source DV Infrastructure
Speakers: Andreas Kurth (lowRISC), Zachary Halvorsen (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9490/ | materials: https://chipsalliance.github.io/caliptra-web/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-bvneQVcBS8
Caliptra is an open-source Root of Trust (RoT) IP block providing hardware security for cloud and edge silicon. To meet rising security demands, robust design verification (DV) infrastructure is vital. This presentation details the DV enhancements for Caliptra 2.2, developed by lowRISC, Google, Microsoft, and the OCP community. We discuss expanded block-level DV coverage for components like AES, KMAC, CSRNG, SPI, I3C, and the VeeR RISC-V core. By leveraging open-source tooling, constrained-random testing, and functional coverage models, we enable scalable verification and strengthen Caliptra's security properties. Finally, we share lessons learned and present a reusable framework for verifying security-critical IP, fostering a robust, community-accessible DV ecosystem for open-source silicon.

### Baremetal Security Architecture
Speakers: Bharat Pillili (Microsoft), Darpana Munjal Loodu (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9530/
Next-generation AI cloud deployments are driving demand for baremetal control alongside increasingly complex, multi-node hardware topologies. Traditional single-controller management models cannot scale to these architectures and introduce security risks due to privileged debug interfaces. This work presents a secure baremetal framework based on hierarchical hardware management, where distributed management controllers aggregate into an isolated control plane. The model enables tenant access to system-level management while maintaining strong isolation from provider control infrastructure through mutually distrusting domains. In parallel, a shift toward flashless “streaming boot” replaces persistent firmware storage with dynamic, attested delivery over standard interfaces, reducing attack surface and improving recovery resilience.

### Out of Band Seamless Updates with Runtime Authorization
Speakers: Anahit Tarkhanyan (Intel), Eli Kupermann (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9537/
Modern hyperscale platforms require continuous trust across the firmware lifecycle, beyond traditional boot-time guarantees. This session presents Intel’s proposal of seamless out-of-band (OOB) microcode (MCU) update flows aligned with the industry-defined OCP Impactless Firmware Update. The solution enables rebootless patching using BMC-driven PLDM mechanisms, allowing rapid, fleet-scale deployment of critical updates with minimal workload disruption - addressing requirements for timely vulnerability mitigation and operational continuity. The model emphasizes BMC-orchestrated update flows with optional host notification, reducing dependency on OS participation while preserving flexibility for OS-aware environments where applicable. By combining OOB delivery, seamless activation, and customer signed firmware enforcement via standards-aligned control flows, this framework demonstrates how silicon-specific implementations can scale across heterogeneous cloud environments.

### Secure Key Distribution Protocol for SoC Hardware Blocks
Speakers: Craig Barner (Marvell), Chandra Shekhar Reddy (Marvell)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9662/ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1ZswLDwY6Ri8oZIMY2DyDKiIAEVByteXa/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=109152408377995252301&rtpof=true&sd=true
This session provides the latest update to the Caliptra secure key distribution workstream, continuing from the update delivered at OCP 2026 EMEA. As SoCs scale to support many accelerators and multi-tenant workloads, ad-hoc key management across hardware blocks (MACSec, PCIe IDE, DRAM, crypto accelerators) creates serious security and compliance risks. We present a hardware-only architecture extending Caliptra HRoT as the sole FIPS-certified DEK source for all SoC hardware blocks. Lightweight KMTx and KMRx blocks encrypt keys in transit using a per-instance Bus Encryption Key (BEK) derived from a boot-time random secret (MSS1) and an RTL-synthesized secret (MSS2), bound to each block's AXI ID and MMIO address — providing forward secrecy, minimal blast radius, and CNSA 2.0 compliance. We will cover specification status, key lifecycle management, and the v1.0 release at the summit, and invite the community to collaborate within the Caliptra as Security Hub Sub Working Group.

### From Securing Links to Securing Pods- Security of Scale-Up Networks
Speakers: Avdhesh Chhodavdia (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9671/
AI scale-up systems have changed the architectural unit of trust. Workloads no longer run on an isolated endpoint attached to a passive interconnect; they run on pods of tightly coupled accelerators, switches, & control software whose behavior emerges from the combined fabric. This session argues that scale-up security should be modeled as a multi-plane trust architecture spanning management, control/configuration, & data planes. We analyze management, control, and data-plane attack surfaces against current attestation and link-encryption mechanisms, then derive a trust-boundary model validated across all pod lifecycle events. The resulting framework defines trust boundaries across 3 trust planes, assigns security responsibility, & reasoning about pod formation, operation, fault handling, and reprovisioning. We propose this model as a basis for open community discussion, extending from single-pod to cluster scale, & provide a checklist practitioners can use in design reviews.

### Package-Aware Integrity: From Source-Agnostic Publication to Full-System Attestation
Speakers: Felipe Zimmerle da Nobrega Costa (KonaSense), Dhananjay Phadke (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9700/ | pdf: https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/9927416 | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-Security-SAFE
BMC firmware ships as a monolithic signed image: bootloaders verify the whole blob, but no package can be updated or measured independently without re-signing everything. Build-time provenance and runtime attestation (Caliptra RIM, SPDM) are linked only at whole-image granularity. The gap is the package: the smallest buildable, auditable, updatable unit of a firmware image. We make the package the unit of trust. On the publishing side, each package becomes a content-addressed entry in a public, append-only transparency log, extending OCP S.A.F.E. SFR manifests and RIM work. On the verification side, per-package measurements compose into a Merkle root bound to the boot signature and extended into a TPM PCR; inclusion proofs against this root enable per-package attestation and rollback protection. We elaborate on Yocto build to demonstrate package-granular attestation from build through boot, and integrate with OCP Security Project artifacts and firmware update ecosystem.

### Confidential Storage
Speakers: Srini Narayanamurthy (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9792/
Securing specialized Artificial Intelligence (AI) training and inference workloads requires protecting sensitive model weights and datasets both in flight and at rest. This talk presents a vendor-neutral blueprint for extending confidential computing trust boundaries from the host to storage devices. On the host, the Trusted Security Manager (TSM) and Trusted Execution Environment (TEE) Device Interface (TDI) isolate workloads and assign them to secure Virtual Functions (VF) using TDI security protocol (TDISP). In transit, data crossing PCIe or CXL interconnects is protected through Data Object Exchange (DOE) and Integrity and Data Encryption (IDE). On the device, Device Security Management (DSM) uses Security Protocol and Data Model (SPDM) for attestation and key exchange. Anchored by a hardware root of trust, the device protects storage partitions using OCP Layered Open-source Cryptographic Key Management (LOCK) and TCG Media Encryption Key–Multi-Party Authorization (MEK -MPA).

### BMC-Mediated Platform Composite Attestation via Redfish
Speakers: Xiling Sun (Microsoft), Roksana Golizadeh Mojarad (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9814/
Today's platform attestation over Redfish is disaggregated. Attesting a server takes N transactions and N signed responses, with no platform-level artifact binding them. There is also no uniform claim: devices exposing only SPDM measurements and devices emitting their own signed EAT share no common structure. We propose a BMC-mediated composite attestation model to close these gaps: the BMC's SPDM daemon collects nonce-bound evidence from all devices and relays it to its platform RoT, which adds its own measurements and signs a composite EAT with the platform attestation key, delivered over Redfish. We pair it with an operator-signed platform CoRIM declaring expected inventory and firmware policy, so the verifier checks what is present against what should be - making completeness and anti-substitution checkable. We invite the community to standardize the Redfish extension and the platform CoRIM as the operator-side complement to vendor CoRIM.

### Caliptra Subsystem 2.2: USB 2.0 and OCP Streaming Boot
Speakers: Caleb Whitehead (Microsoft), Bart Vertenten (NXP Semiconductors)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9829/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/caliptra-ss
Caliptra 2.0/2.1 Subsystem enables secure boot of server components through I3C (streaming boot) as well as vendor specific interfaces through AXI streaming boot. Caliptra 2.2 Subsystem provides a USB 2.0 device as an OCP Streaming Boot interface option implemented in hardware. Caliptra 2.2 Subsystem also adds a capability to transfer the control of USB from Caliptra Subsystem to an SoC specific microcontroller for implementing SoC-specific runtime protocols, including OBMF and MCTP, as well as any other SoC-specific use models. In this talk, we present the architectural overview of USB 2.0 enhancements and discuss the anticipated firmware development model for SoC integrators. We also highlight the updated interfaces between SoC microcontroller and Caliptra Subsystem MCU through submission/completion queues for faster processing.

### Architectural Masking for PQC: Adam’s Bridge 3.0 Leveraging NTT Linearity
Speakers: Mojtaba Bisheh-Niasar (Microsoft), Kiran Upadhyayula (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9835/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/adams-bridge
Post-quantum cryptography (PQC) accelerators face a critical challenge: while algorithms such as ML‑DSA and ML-KEM provide quantum resistance, their implementations remain highly vulnerable to side‑channel attacks (SCAs). Traditional countermeasures rely on operation-level masking, introducing significant area and latency overhead. In this talk, we present an enhanced Adam’s Bridge architecture that leverages algorithmic linearity (NTT/INTT) to shift masking from arithmetic operations to the architectural level. By introducing dual-pipeline NTT flows and selective masking , we eliminate the need for pervasive operation-level masking while maintaining strong SCA resilience. Our results demonstrate substantial efficiency gains, including approximately 50% area reduction compared to classical masking approaches , while maintaining robust protection. This work enables practical deployment of PQC accelerators in silicon root-of-trust (RoT) systems.

### Beyond Static Identity: Enabling Secure Certificate Refresh in Silicon Roots of Trust
Speakers: Emre Karabulut (Microsoft), VISHAL MHATRE (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9847/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/caliptra-ss
Caliptra’s DICE-based certificate chain provides a strong hardware-rooted identity, but is typically treated as immutable after provisioning. In practice, devices benefit from the ability to refresh their identity over time without compromising security or exposing secrets. This talk introduces a hardware-based identity refresh mechanism that enables secure regeneration of certificate chains from chip-internal roots. By deriving identity from secrets and binding it with a wrapper key, the approach allows identity updates without having a large persistent fuse storage. The design ensures that sensitive key material is never exposed outside the device and that each refresh produces a cryptographically isolated identity. This work demonstrates how silicon roots of trust like Caliptra can support dynamic identity lifecycles while preserving strong DICE security guarantees.

## Server

### MHS: Modular Plug-and-Play (M-PNP) Workstream Overview, Updates, and Progress Towards Industry Adoption
Speakers: Todd Rosedahl (Jabil), David Heinrich (HPE), Chitrak Gupta (American Megatrends (AMI))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9213/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EDExp2vPzyc | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Server/MHS/DC-MHS-Specs-and-Designs#Latest_DC-MHS_Specifications
The MHS Plug-and-Play (M-PNP) workstream addresses the challenges of ensuring Secure Control Module (SCM), Host Processor Module (HPM) and peripheral interoperability by specifying the buses, interfaces, protocol layers, APIs, and data models for BMC consumption. The M-PNP workstream is focusing on a set of specifications that build on the MHS base specifications to provide a set of consistent management interfaces and system behaviors that facilitate rapid platform enablement, greater development efficiency, and a reduced need for custom firmware generation. This presentation provides an overview of the M-PNP workstream and the progress we have made on the core M-PNP specifications as well as updates regarding tools and POC development work for the enablement of M-PNP industry adoption. We plan to have a 0.7 release of the specifications package available which will include key deliverables such as updated versions of the M-PNP.FRU_Discovery_Boot and M-PNP.FPGA specifications.

### MHS: Onward Towards Industry Adoption - Demonstrating Modular Plug-and-Play (M-PNP) Tools and POC Work
Speakers: Phillip Leech (HPE), Bill Scherer (HPE), Munir Ahmad (Lattice Semiconductor)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9216/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EDExp2vPzyc | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Server/MHS/DC-MHS-Specs-and-Designs#Latest_DC-MHS_Specifications
The OCP MHS Plug-and-Play (M-PNP) workstream addresses the challenges of ensuring SCM, HPM and peripheral interoperability via specifying interfaces, APIs, and data models. This presentation will highlight the work from the M-PNP workstream that directly impacts enabling industry adoption. We plan to highlight the set of M-PNP specific tools and working proof-of-concept (POC) prototypes that will showcase how system component suppliers and integrators can more easily adopt M-PNP support.

### Addressing Discovery and Topology Challenges in M-PESTI Systems
Speakers: Tamer GUDU (AMD), Aleksandar Jovanovic (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9476/
The Modular Peripheral Sideband Tunneling Interface (M-PESTI) is an emerging single-wire, half-duplex protocol standardized by the Open Compute Project (OCP) for peripheral communication. As the specification evolves, challenges remain in achieving efficient target discovery and supporting devices behind multiplexers. The current approach recommends a single UART shared in a round-robin fashion to minimize area, but with support for up to 512 targets, discovery latency can exceed 40 seconds. To address this, we propose a time-division multiplexing (TDM) scheme that improves UART utilization and significantly reduces discovery time while maintaining low area overhead. Additionally, we introduce a method for reliable detection of devices behind multiplexers and switches, where no standard solution exists. These techniques improve scalability and enable efficient large-scale deployments. Copyright © 2026, Advanced Micro Devices, Inc., or its affiliates. All rights reserved.

### OCP NIC 2026: Gen 7 and liquid cooling – More speed and power ahead
Speakers: Thomas Ng (Intel), Jason Rock (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8890/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Server/NIC
OCP NIC 3.0 continues to advance to meet next-generation bandwidth, manageability, and thermal requirements for AI/HPC platforms. This update adds PCIe Gen 7 support, introduces FRU model updates aligned with DSP0220, and defines power transient excursion behavior to better handle short-term workload spikes. Thermal guidance has been expanded with a liquid-cooling reference design to support higher-density deployments. Together, these aspects position OCP NIC for a faster, smarter and more thermally capable NIC design.

### SPEC CPU 2026 - What's new, what's improved, and why it matters
Speakers: David Reiner (SPEC (Standard Performance Evaluation Corporation))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9315/ | materials: https://www.spec.org/pressreleases/2026/20260505-spec-releases-cpu-2026-benchmark-suite/
The explosive growth of agentic AI and growing HPC market have elevated the server CPU to a critical element in the datacenter. At the same time, the competitive landscape is shifting—hyperscale cloud providers are designing custom silicon, new vendors are emerging, and multiple ISAs are vying for adoption. In this environment, a trusted, industry-standard benchmark for evaluating CPU, memory, and compiler performance across generations and platforms is indispensable—and it must reflect workloads that matter to real customers. SPEC CPU 2026 is the seventh major release of the SPEC CPU benchmark series, the gold standard for CPU performance measurement since 1989. SPEC CPU results directly influence purchasing decisions, competitive positioning, and long-term CPU design priorities. This talk covers what is new and improved in SPEC CPU 2026, including the applications, datasets and metrics as well as comparisons of microarchitectural performance characteristics compared to SPEC CPU 2017.

### Breaking the Memory Capacity-Bandwidth Tradeoff for Agentic Inference
Speakers: Allan Cantle (Nallasway)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9716/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/wiki/HPC
As AI inference goes agentic, the demand for very large context - and its associated KVCache - needing to be resident in the local HBM is skyrocketing while HBM capacity is fundamentally failing to keep up! The solution in NVidia’s VR NVL72 architecture is to shard with Tensor parallelism, TP, across the 72 Rubin GPUs, which is an exceptionally expensive way to add memory, or fall back to Vera’s local LPDDR5X memory with its 5x greater memory capacity, but at only 5% of the HBM bandwidth! This presentation will introduce a new 140mm x 140mm XPU module concept from the OCP HPC Subproject with support for up to 12.5TBytes @7.5TBytes/s of LPDDR5X to a single reticule sized XPU. Additionally, the module will support 128 112G IO Scale Up & Out IO lanes through co-packaged copper interconnect. The modules memory and IO architecture is built with Credo’s recent OCP contribution of their Lightweight Serial Interconnect standard, called OmniConnect.

### Reducing Parasitics, Improving Performance: The Case for Low Inductance Interconnects.
Speakers: Quresh Bohra (TE Connectivity), Tony Howard (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9727/
The increasing trends in data centers toward higher compute density and elevated rack power levels are driving more power delivery directly to the chip. At such high power levels and rapid switching speeds, transient performance becomes critically important. Connectors and cables responsible for delivering this power must therefore be designed to achieve optimal transient response, which requires minimizing both inductance (L) and resistance (R). Careful optimization of interconnect geometry is essential to reduce the loop inductance seen by the chip. This paper provides system engineers with practical interconnect design guidance to improve transient response in high density power systems.

### MHS: Datacenter-Secure Control Module (DC-SCM) 3.0 Specification Update
Speakers: Tim Lambert (Dell Technologies, Inc.), Javier Lasa (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8961/
OCP MHS - DC-SCM 3.0 Update: This session shows the evolution of next generation infrastructure in terms of common enablement of modular and scalable control plane architectural trends. These are discussed in the context of updates to the evolving DC-SCM 3.0 base and associated OCP base and design specifications (such as Modular Plug-n-Play, Open Boot & Management Framework, LTPI 2.0 protocol, Host Processor Module Common Circuit Design Specifications). We discuss broad control plane hardware trends and workstream goals. Examples include significantly improved HW/FW interoperability, interface consolidation, enhanced scalability in terms of in- & out-of-band bandwidth, fanout, multi-node management.

### Open Silicon Photonics for AI Systems: Extending the MHS Platform Contract
Speakers: Bijan Nowroozi (Lightmatter), Morris Repeta (Dell)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9292/
Next gen AI systems require extreme throughput thus bringing silicon photonics into OCP server platforms. This shift affects escape geometry, faceplate envelopes, internal fiber routing, optical distribution, power delivery, thermal design, and management visibility when optical engines leave the pluggable cage. We present the Open Silicon Photonics for AI Systems architecture vision, being developed collaboratively in OCP’s MHS as a workstream. We introduce a vendor-neutral architecture for front-panel, near-package, and co-packaged optics, and define the MHS tray contract for each class across five dimensions: mechanical, power, high-speed signaling, thermal, and management. Rather than prescribing optical engine implementations, the work keeps the server platform open, interoperable, and ready for multiple silicon photonics approaches. Attendees will learn what a photonics-ready MHS tray must provide, how this extends DC-MHS, and how to participate as specifications mature.

### SBSA: Advancing Arm Server System Architecture in the OCP Ecosystem
Speakers: Dong Wei (ARM), Samer El-Haj-Mahmoud (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8872/ | materials: https://drive.google.com/file/d/1L9kojnqpZ-Y_FePEDxG-aXszfWh0ef8r/view?usp=drivesdk
The Server Base System Architecture (SBSA) workstream establishes a common hardware architecture baseline for Arm-based servers within the OCP Server Project. The CLA workstream has attracted participation from 13 founding companies and is preparing to develop the next specification release, SBSA v8.1. This session provides an update on the collaborative industry effort to define an open, OCP-aligned platform architecture that simplifies software deployment across Arm-based servers. SBSA provides operating systems, hypervisors, and firmware with a consistent platform target by standardizing key architectural features required for server installation, boot, and operation. Attendees will learn how SBSA improves software portability, reduces platform-specific enablement effort, supports multi-vendor ecosystem development, and enables more predictable deployment of Arm servers, including emerging AI and accelerated computing platforms.

### Open Adapter Card (OAC) - Sub-Rackscale Acceleration Lives On
Speakers: Gregory D Sellman (AMD), Brian Aspnes (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8953/
Relying on a belief that AI inference represents the next big hardware wave for Enterprise and Edge AI it is clear that a solution is needed to fill the gap between traditional PCI CEM form factor adapters and the largely unbound rackscale SoC's powering many of today's mega-watt racks; a solution that is equally at home in brown field and green field datacenters, 19" and 21" rack deployments. Formed in early 2026, the Open Adapter Card (OAC) workgroup consisting of AMD, Dell, Intel, Nvidia and Qualcomm, is happy to share an initial specification release.

### Managing Open Adapter Card (OAC)
Speakers: Ross Hamilton (AMD), Marko Bartscherer (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/10281/
The Open Adapter Card (OAC) specification defines a flexible management architecture for accelerator cards, designed to support dynamic signaling assignments, baseboard interoperability, and fine-grained power controls across heterogeneous deployments. At the heart of OAC management is a USB 2.0 interface serving as the primary out-of-band management path, carrying MCTP over USB to enable standardized device discovery, telemetry, and control. OAC's management model is designed to align with established OCP specifications — including the OCP GPU & Accelerator Management Interfaces, RAS Requirements, and FW Update Specification — delivering a coherent, interoperable management story for next-generation accelerator platforms. This presentation explores the key management aspects of OAC and how its design philosophy supports the broader OCP ecosystem.

### Copper Interconnect Innovations for Realizing 400G Electrical
Speakers: Andrew Kim (Luxshare-Tech), Jinhua Chen (Luxshare-ICT)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9840/ | materials: https://dcon26.mapyourshow.com/8_0/sessions/session-details.cfm?scheduleid=252 | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BLQCVEAzvuQ
This presentation will cover advances in interconnect technologies to enable high performance 400G electrical interconnects including the potential support of PAM4 modulation. The primary components of focus here are the CPC connector and a novel HSIO connector. Since the 2024 OCP Global Summit, the Koolio CPC has continually pushed the frontier of SI performance and beachfront density of a CPC connector using a stubless, elastomeric interface. This presentation provides updates on the development of the 400G version including achievements in: connector loss, bandwidth, ILD, and consistency. We will also present a new HSIO connector complementing Koolio. Simulations and measurements of this interconnect will be presented. We will show how this can be incorporated into a front panel and backplane connector design for DAC assemblies. Additionally, a front panel plug design employing a PCB-mounted right-angle-connector will also be presented for AEC, ACC, and AOC applications.

### Co-Packaged Copper at 448G: Balancing Electrical Efficiency with System Level Complexity
Speakers: Sakina Naqvi (TE Connectivity), Usman Saeed (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9780/
As data center bandwidth scales toward 448G per lane, Co‑Packaged Copper (CPC) is emerging as a compelling architecture for extending electrical connectivity in tightly integrated systems. By minimizing channel length and bringing copper interconnects closer to the ASIC, CPC reduces insertion loss, improving signal fidelity and lowering latency compared to traditional pluggables. This makes it well suited for high‑density AI and switching platforms. As speeds increase to 448G, CPC density continues to rise as the available package space remains the same, intensifying integration challenges. Beyond signal integrity, thermal management becomes more complex, while mechanical constraints such as cable routing and bend radius limit scalability. Ultimately, CPC offers strong electrical and architectural benefits for short‑reach links but shifts complexity into thermal, mechanical, and system domains, positioning it as both an enabler and a transitional step toward co‑packaged optics (CPO).

### Scaling Hyperscale Cloud Networking from 400G to 800G: System Integration Lessons and Implications for OCP Platforms
Speakers: Lucy Yu (MSFT), Faye Yang (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8888/
As hyperscale cloud platforms move from 400G to 800G networking, the key challenge is no longer bandwidth alone, but managing the rapid rise in power, thermal, firmware, security, and validation complexity. Drawing on practical lessons from multiple generations of SmartNIC integration, this presentation offers a deployment-grounded view of what changes materially as platforms shift from 400G to 800G readiness. It highlights the key bottlenecks that now determine platform viability, including rising power density, liquid cooling, multi-host and multi-NIC architecture, and more complex coordination across hyperscalers, silicon vendors, and ODM partners. SmartNIC form factors are evolving toward more customized designs to support multiple platform generations. We highlighted critical trade-offs across compatibility, scalability, mechanical, and thermal constraints. These lessons provide actionable guidance for OCP platform evolution, interoperability, and scalable validation.

### Architecting Openness: How Switches Enable the Future of Open Adapter Card Platforms
Speakers: Chris Blackburn (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9826/
While much of the focus in AI system infrastructure has been on massive row-scale clusters, sub-rack solutions are still very important. Many data center deployments have limited power and cooling infrastructure that prohibits adoption of those exotic solutions. A new form factor being introduced in OCP, the open adapter card or OAC, is being developed to support sub-rack architectures where these constraints exist. It builds upon the PCIe AIC form factor and systems but addresses areas limited by this legacy form factor. The system architecture developed around the OAC requires multiple switching technologies, including PCIe switches for connectivity to NICs and host CPUs and scale-up switches for scale-up fabrics. Several system topologies will be explored, and proposals for a common baseboard will be shared to help the OCP community work toward a common solution across the ecosystem.

### Reimagining Data Center Power: High-Density Architectures for Next-Gen AI Compute
Speakers: Gration Dsilva (AMD), Luocheng Wang (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8987/ | materials: https://drive.google.com/file/d/1STfTxyg-2QXeKSMSARhzG4wrtSux1_en/view
Next-generation AI and HPC GPUs are driving major changes in data center power delivery due to increasing power density demands. This presentation highlights advancements in second-stage multiphase integrated power modules achieving over 50% higher current density—exceeding 1.5 A/mm² (up to 2 A/mm²) compared to ~0.8 A/mm² in standard two-phase designs. Reduced module height enables vertical power delivery, improving efficiency in space-constrained, high-power systems. An 8:1 intermediate bus converter (IBC) further enhances end-to-end efficiency by optimizing downstream GPU regulators. These modules adopt a Common Footprint (CFP) aligned with OCP principles, enabling interoperability and multi-vendor sourcing. A scalable vertical power architecture supports efficient multi-kilowatt delivery while addressing rack density, thermal, and integration challenges. As a call to action, a 3 A/mm² current density target is outlined for future AI GPUs.

### Accelerating High-Power Server Design Through Board-Level Electro-Thermal Co-Simulation of VRMs and PDNs
Speakers: Steven Chien (Wiwynn Corporation), Zi Ping Wu (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9870/
As server power continues to increase, predicting the thermal behavior of voltage regulator modules (VRMs) and power delivery networks (PDNs) during the early design stage has become increasingly challenging. Conventional board-level thermal simulations often rely on simplified boundary assumptions, resulting in limited correlation with measurements. This presentation introduces an Electro-Thermal (ET) Co-Simulation approach that integrates power integrity analysis with board-level thermal simulation to improve thermal prediction accuracy for VRMs and PCB power rails. The methodology is validated using IR imaging and PCB thermocouple measurements. Compared with conventional board-level thermal simulations, ET Co-Simulation delivers comparable temperature prediction results while reducing mesh count and simulation runtime. This approach enables a more efficient early-stage VRM and PDN thermal assessment, helping accelerate server thermal design and development.

### From Fragmentation to Footprint: The OCP Standard for AI Signal Conditioners
Speakers: Casey Morrison (Astera Labs), Liang Xue (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9729/
As AI infrastructure scales to thousands of high-speed serial links per rack, signal conditioners have become essential for maintaining signal integrity between accelerators and switches. However, proprietary package sizes, pinouts, and routing geometries increase engineering complexity, limit multi-vendor flexibility, and create supply chain bottlenecks. This presentation introduces the OCP Signal Conditioner Standard Footprint Base Specification v1.0, which standardizes the physical interface between signal conditioners and system boards. Supporting up to 256 Gbps PAM4 across multiple protocols, and proprietary AI fabrics, the specification improves manufacturability, interoperability, scalability, and multi-vendor design enablement for next-generation AI infrastructure.

## Server: Composable Memory Systems

### Tiered Memory for LLM Inference: A Modeling Framework for KV-Cache Offload
Speakers: Khayam Anjam (Micron Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8880/
LLMs have made memory capacity a critical datacenter bottleneck alongside compute and bandwidth. Inference with large context windows and high concurrency drives adoption of tiered memory architectures spanning HBM, LPDDR and storage. However, predicting the performance impact of KV-cache offload across these tiers remains an open challenge for both analytical and simulation-based approaches. This work presents a system-level analytical modeling framework that quantifies the benefits and trade-offs of KV-cache offload in tiered memory systems. It decomposes inference into hardware-calibrated operator primitives augmented with a two-population working-set decomposition for system-prompt and session-context reuse, policy-agnostic eviction bounds, and tier-aware bandwidth/latency modeling. Validated on NVIDIA GH200 with LPDDR offload, it captures capacity thresholds, miss-rate scaling, and concurrency-driven pressure, and is extensible to CXL-attached DRAM and storage-class memory.

### Managing Orchestration Memory with the CMS Memory Fabric Manager
Speakers: Hongjian Fan (Seagate), Han Deok Lee (Samsung)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9153/
Composable memory offers a viable approach to addressing memory wall challenges in data centers, where some servers experience memory stranding while others require additional memory for cache offloading. Like any dynamic resource, composable memory requires coordinated management software aligned with workload demands. Today, many workloads run in containers managed by orchestration platforms such as Kubernetes. This presentation reviews OCP contributions on composable memory management software requirements and demonstrates how CXL memory is enabled through orchestration using fabric manager software.

### CXL in Hyperscale Environment: Fleet-Scale Challenges, Failure Modes, and the Path to Better Debuggability
Speakers: Vineet Parekh (Meta), Markandeya Mudigonda (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9558/
We present real-world data on CXL errors observed across our production fleet, including device-level failures, link errors, and protocol-level anomalies — their frequency, distribution, and impact on system availability. A significant portion of this talk focuses on debugging challenges unique to CXL at fleet scale. Unlike traditional memory or PCIe devices, CXL introduces a multi-layered protocol stack spanning host, device, and fabric that complicates root-cause analysis. Limited device-internal visibility, inconsistent vendor error reporting, and difficulty reproducing intermittent issues at scale all extend time-to-resolution. Finally, we propose solutions to advance CXL debug and diagnostics — including enhanced error telemetry, standardized debug interfaces, improved RAS features, and tooling for faster fault isolation at scale. We aim to drive discussion on what OEMs, device vendors, and hyperscalers can do together to make CXL production-ready.

### Cost-Efficient LLM Inference via Peer-to-Peer GPU-to-Memory Expansion Datapath
Speakers: Moshe Twitto (Astera Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9741/ | materials: https://www.asteralabs.com/inference-tokenomics-how-cxl-memory-expansion-improves-ai-economics/
Most LLM Key-Value (KV) cache offloading solutions today rely on host-attached DRAM, which introduces PCIe hop bottlenecks and CPU overhead into the critical execution path. This presentation introduces an architectural blueprint for a high-performance, peer-to-peer (P2P) datapath directly between GPUs and memory expansion cards operating in native PCIe mode, bypassing the lack of native CXL support in current GPUs. By leveraging a PCIe switch and memory expander, this architecture enables direct GPU-to-memory P2P access over the switch fabric. This unlocks massive KV cache capacity, while freeing host DRAM for agents and other workloads. We share real-world telemetry and results demonstrating measurable gains in GPU utilization, throughput, and tokens-per-dollar efficiency. Additionally, we show how a single GPU paired with PCIe-mode memory expansion can efficiently run Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models and discuss how these enable open, composable AI infrastructure at scale.

### Addressing Memory Wall challenge for AI and General Compute platforms (CMS Sub-Project Technical Update)
Speakers: Anjaneya "Reddy" Chagam (Microsoft Corporation), Grant Mackey (XCENA), Mohamad El-Batal (Seagate), Samir Rajadnya (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8902/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/composable-memory-system
Memory wall for capacity is becoming a challenge for General Compute as well as AI platforms (with KV Cache driving memory needs for Inference). The OCP Composable Memory Systems (CMS) sub-project is developing scalable memory architectures and hardware/software co-designs to address cloud service providers’ needs for memory expansion, pooling, and sharing - critical for both general-purpose computing and AI workloads. This session will provide an update on the CMS Sub-Project goals, highlight progress over the past year, and outline next steps toward enabling efficient, disaggregated memory solutions within the OCP ecosystem.

### Disaggregated CXL Memory Architecture for Agentic AI: Dynamic Pooling,  Sharing, and PNM Offloading
Speakers: Kyumin Park (Samsung Electronics (Semiconductor)), Harry Kim (XCENA), Jinin So (Samsung Electronics)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9127/ | materials: https://www.terrapinn.com/conference/future-memory-storage/agenda.stm?trc=sfmc&j=345463&sfmc_sub=151981538&l=282_HTML&u=99567099&mid=536001174&jb=1007&utm_source=sfmc&utm_medium=email&utm_campaign=TT_11147_CONFMC_Future+of+Memory+and+Storage+2026_-11+Nurture+Agenda+released&utm_id=345463&sfmc_id=151981538&cid=a4b043ad-8c21-4814-85ed-af30d83ba313 | pdf: https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/document/11298523/
Agentic AI workloads, driven by multi-agent collaboration, dynamic tool calling, and expanding long-context KV caches, create severe memory capacity and bandwidth bottlenecks. Traditional fixed allocations lead to substantial stranded memory and excessive TCO. To address these challenges, we present a holistic HW-SW co-designed architecture tailored for data-centric AI infrastructure using Compute Express Link (CXL). First, we characterize memory usage profiles across Agentic AI services to quantify resource inefficiencies. Next, we propose a disaggregated infrastructure combining CXL-based Dynamic Memory Pooling and LMCache-driven hierarchical management. This setup enables zero-copy, host-to-host KV cache sharing across nodes via GPU DMA, eliminating duplicate memory overhead and bounce buffer latency. Finally, we integrate CXL Processing-Near-Memory (PNM) to offload repetitive, memory-bounded tasks like Vector Search and Attention directly to the memory tier to overcome memory bandwidth bottleneck. Empirical results demonstrate significant TCO reduction, eliminated stranded memory, and optimized token throughput.

### XPU Scale Composable Smart Memory Fabric
Speakers: Gaurav Agarwal (Marvell), Satananda Burla (Marvell)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9177/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vmKxYi4iPgY | pdf: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.14000
This presentation introduces Smart Memory Fabrics , a data-centric architecture that combines composable memory and Near-Data Processing to transform the memory fabric into an intelligent, rack-scale data-processing layer. Smart Memory Fabrics operate directly on AI data objects—including KV-cache blocks, vector embeddings, and graph data—where they reside, reducing data movement, mitigating far-memory latency, and improving infrastructure efficiency. We explore design options enabled by emerging technologies, including photonic fabrics for disaggregated HBM, hybrid PCIe/CXL switching, and tiered memory architectures. Drawing from a HW/SW co-designed implementation, we present insights from large-scale AI serving workloads—including long-context LLM inference, distributed KV-cache services, vector databases, and recommender systems—and demonstrate how composable memory infrastructure improves scalability, resource utilization, and total system efficiency.

### Beyond DRAM: Byte-Addressable Persistent Memory via Compute Interconnects for AI
Speakers: Feng Zhu (Alibaba Cloud Computing), Shu Li (Alibaba Cloud Computing)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9282/ | pdf: https://dl.acm.org/doi/epdf/10.1145/3805632
Large language models and other memory-intensive AI workloads demand vast memory capacity, exacerbating the so-called “memory wall.” While CPU-side DRAM can be expanded, DIMM-based solutions are fundamentally constrained by slot count and cost per gigabyte. In this work, we present a byte-addressable persistent memory product built on phase-change memory (PCM) technology and connected via the CXL interconnect, delivering scalable, cost-effective memory expansion with performance approaching DRAM. We evaluate CXL persistent memory for LLM KV cache offloading, demonstrating up to 50% higher throughput compared to a DRAM-only baseline. Additionally, we explore CXL switch technology to enable rack-scale memory pooling and sharing of persistent memory resources across multiple hosts. Looking ahead, we discuss UALink as a future pathway to directly attach persistent memory to GPUs, extending memory capacity beyond HBM while leveraging low-latency scale-up interconnects.

## Server: Open Chiplet Economy

### The Open Chiplet Economy: Progress, Challenges, and the Road Ahead
Speakers: Anu Ramamurthy (Microchip), Jawad Nasrullah (Palo Alto Electron), Mark Knight (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9110/
The Open Chiplet Economy (OCE) initiative was established to accelerate adoption of interoperable chiplet-based systems through open collaboration across design, manufacturing, assembly, test, and supply-chain ecosystems. Over the past year, OCE workstreams have advanced efforts in design exchange, system integration methodologies, package assembly, optical communications, D2D interoperability, and ecosystem enablement. This session provides an update from OCE leadership on key accomplishments, lessons learned, and emerging challenges as the industry transitions toward increasingly modular semiconductor architectures. The discussion will highlight workstream activities, ecosystem participation, and opportunities for broader industry engagement. It will also explore the evolution of chiplet ecosystems from component interoperability toward system-level composability and identify priorities that can help establish a sustainable, scalable open chiplet ecosystem.

### Custom HBM as a System: Co-designing the Base Die, Die-to-Die Power, and Package Thermals for AI Accelerators
Speakers: Hsin-wei Tseng (Marvell)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9493/ | materials: https://www.marvell.com/company/newsroom/marvell-announces-breakthrough-custom-hbm-compute-architecture.html
HBM is advancing faster than any memory in the data center — HBM3, 3E, 4, 4E, 5 in just a few years — each generation pushing bandwidth, capacity, and power higher. Yet the JEDEC HBM5 standard is still maturing, opening a gap between what AI accelerators need and what the standard delivers. This session presents a custom HBM (cHBM) architecture that closes that gap: the HBM memory controller and PHY move off the compute die onto a custom logic base die, with the host connected by a die-to-die (D2D) interface replacing the standard HBM PHY. Customizing the base die frees compute-die area, adds capacity, and lowers I/O power — and lets teams ship ahead of the standards cadence. We then tackle the two hard problems it creates: D2D power (a bidirectional link with adaptive power management) and package thermals (co-designing TIM, lid, and integrated cooling). We close on the open, multi-vendor die-to-die interfaces the OCP community needs to make custom memory real.

### Scaling up AI Compute, Memory and Connectivity
Speakers: Igor Elkanovich (GUC)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8918/
Growing demand for AI performance drives scaling compute power, memory capacity and bandwidth, Scale-up bandwidth and radix with maximal power efficiency. 3D integration is a main approach to increase compute density. High bandwidth, low power and latency 3D interface allows flexible architectural partitioning across top and bottom dies. To support high current demand with minimal loss, vertical power delivery combines Voltage Regulator (VR) integration in PCB cavity under the chip, VRs in package substrate and in CoWoS-L interposer. 3D stacking of multiple DRAM layers over compute enhances architectural flexibility and memory bandwidth, reduces memory latency and improves power efficiency. Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) enables high bandwidth and radix power efficient system scaling, XPU platform with HBMs and 102Tbps Slow-Wide CPO optical engines is presented. Integration of advanced packaging technologies enables high performance and power-efficient AI processors of the future.

### Building Interoperable AI Systems with Chiplets: Arm Neoverse and Cadence in an Open Ecosystem
Speakers: Michael Posner (Cadence Design Systems), Saif Elsaady (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9539/ | materials: https://www.chipestimate.com/The-Power-of-Shifting-Left-Cadence-Accelerating-Innovation-with-Arm/Cadence/Technical-Article/2025/12/09
AI infrastructure is accelerating adoption of modular, chiplet-based architectures that require interoperability and ecosystem alignment. This session highlights joint Arm–Cadence innovation enabling AI-ready silicon using Arm Neoverse platforms integrated with Cadence Design IP (PCIe, UCIe, DDR) within an FCSA-compliant chiplet framework . A shift-left methodology is central— leveraging virtual prototyping alongside emulation to enable early software development and pre-silicon interoperability validation. This reduces integration risk, shortens debug cycles, and accelerates system bring-up. The solution supports scalable, composable architectures across compute, memory, and I/O chiplets, enabling heterogeneous integration. By aligning to open standards and reusable IP , Arm and Cadence demonstrate how ecosystem-driven collaboration improves silicon readiness , ensures interoperability, and drives adoption for next-generation AI and datacenter platforms.

### Security of Multi-chip ICs & Security Chiplets with Caliptra: Building a Hardware Root of Trust
Speakers: Matt Holdrege (Microchip)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8733/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/wiki/Server/OCE/FCSA
Heterogeneous Integration (HI) enables multiple chiplets from various vendors to be combined into a single package, improving scalability and yield for advanced applications like AI, mobile and high-performance computing while maintaining yield for advanced process technologies. While widely adopted and many products have been already out, the security of these multi-chip architectures is still developing, with new vulnerabilities emerging due to interconnected components and global supply chains. As a result, system and security architects need guidance on potential risks and mitigation strategies. The Foundation Chiplet System Architecture (FCSA) initiative aims to provide such guidance, and the discussion highlights both the benefits and security challenges of multi-chip designs. This talk will cover security both positive and negative aspects of multi-chip architectures incorporating both FCSA and the security chapter the of the Heterogenous Integration Roadmap.

### Die-to-Die Solutions for Optical Chiplets
Speakers: Kevin Donnelly (Eliyan)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8907/
During the last two decades, increases in compute performance in FLOPs has far outpaced the increases in interconnect bandwidth, leading to limitations in system performance often described as the I/O Wall. System designers are evaluating a wide range of optical technologies to enable optical scale-up and scale-out solutions for datacenters. Optical solutions connect to XPUs via electrical interfaces, and as optics move ever closer to the XPU with co-packaged optics (CPO), designers are evaluating different die-to-die (D2D) interfaces for these ASIC-to-optical connections. This talk will describe several electrical-to-optical connection examples with D2D solutions including UCIe, BoW, and 3D PHY interfaces, and show how these D2D interfaces can enable different system-level solutions. D2D interfaces can efficiently connect ASICs to optical devices, providing improved performance at lower power than traditional off-package SerDes interfaces to optical modules.

### AI-native Virtual Chiplet Eco-systems with industry best practices to accelerate Chiplet adoption
Speakers: Matt Holdrege (Microchip), Marc Meunier (Arm), Nandan Nayampally (Baya Systems), Lichen Weng (Tenstorrent)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9060/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/open-chiplet-economy/open-framework-for-chiplet-eco-system-virtual-prototyping
Despite the advantages of chiplets, broad adoption addressing a wider range of market opportunities by smaller organizations remains a major challenge. We need to reduce friction at the front-end of design cycle that includes architecture and business planning thereby increasing design space explorations in multi-vendor environments. Define AI-native virtual ecosystems built on industry best practices of virtual prototyping that will accelerate chiplet project evaluations. AI must be applied extensively to increase early-stage explorations while reducing mundane and repetitive tasks. These include semantic and keyword search of specs, rank ordering and scoring top matches, highlighting gaps, code generation, harness to EDA tools, test benches, and benchmarks. Incorporate best practices including encrypted models, containerized models, fast models, fixed virtual platforms, cloud, local and remote verification IP for interoperability validation, IP libraries, and scenario-based modeling.

### Bunch of Wires Flexi 1.0 : a low cost, low power D2D interface
Speakers: Morgan Whately (Infineon Technologies), Kash Johal (YorChip)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8928/
BoW Flexi targets systems that benefit from heterogeneous integration but require a simple, low-cost interface between chiplets. A representative use case is a sensor chiplet connected to a microcontroller, where data rates are low (tens of KB/s) and traffic is often asymmetric. Existing die-to-die (D2D) PHY solutions are optimized for high-performance computing, supporting extremely high data rates and dense interconnects, which increases complexity, area, and cost. In contrast, BoW Flexi provides a streamlined D2D interface tailored for lower bandwidth applications, reducing implementation overhead while maintaining efficient latency and power. This makes it well-suited for cost- and energy-constrained edge and embedded systems where simplicity and right-sized performance are critical

### Advancing Chiplet Flexibility:  Updates in 2.0 OCE Transaction and Link Layer Specification
Speakers: Brian Murray (VeriSilicon)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8906/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Server/OpenChipletEconomy/Link-Layer
This talk will cover an overview and new features of the 2.0 OCE Transaction and Link Layer Specification, which describes how SoC-native bus protocols can be transported over a chiplet D2D connection. Updates include a universal PHY layer allowing designers to implement the same bus transport over UCIE, BoW, and the new BoW FLEXI physical layers. This gives chiplet implementations much more flexibility in changing their D2D strategy without impacting the rest of the SoC design. This also expands the options chip makers have in SoC disaggregation of subsystems into chiplets. In addition, the talk will cover a variety of new 2.0 performance and efficiency features, changes to profiles, and tips for building new profiles for the standard.

### System level standards for Multi vendor chiplet interoperability
Speakers: Itai Nadler (GSOC LTD), Rob Pelt (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9158/
The chiplet revolution is well underway, yet composing systems from chiplets across multiple vendors remains largely unsolved.
