Physical interface standards like UCIe and BoW are necessary, but address only one layer of a much deeper challenge. Without system-level standards covering management, software abstraction, and security, every multi-vendor integration remains a custom effort - expensive and slow. This session presents the progress of the OCP OCE Chiplet Systems working group in defining the full system stack for chiplet interoperability: from high-level software abstraction down to the precise API structure and messages exchanged across the chiplet fabric. We cover the Chiplet Management Protocol - built on SCMI principles, extended for cross-die routing and security - and a layered model mapping services such as power and RAS to transport mechanisms and physical interfaces.

### Securing the Open Chiplet Ecosystem: Defining a Common Security Architecture for FCSA
Speakers: Dominic Brown (Arm), Sylvain Guilley (Secure-IC)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9148/
As chiplet-based systems become increasingly heterogeneous and multi-vendor, security must evolve beyond individual devices to address trust across the entire system. Open chiplet ecosystems introduce new challenges around device identity, secure communication, attestation, lifecycle management, and supply-chain assurance. This session presents joint work between Arm and Cadence to develop a security architecture for the Foundation Chiplet System Architecture (FCSA). The work aims to establish a common framework for security in interoperable chiplet-based systems while preserving implementation flexibility and vendor differentiation. Attendees will gain insight into the architectural principles being developed within the FCSA, and the session will also discuss how standards-based approaches can improve trust, simplify integration, and accelerate adoption of open chiplet ecosystems.

### Agile Silicon--Will Open Chiplet Architectures Keep Pace with Fast-Evolving AI Dataflows
Speakers: Luisa Gonzalez-Guerrero (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9111/
Chiplet-based architectures create new opportunities for system optimization while increasing design complexity. Engineers must evaluate tradeoffs across architecture, packaging, power delivery, thermal performance, manufacturability, testability, and software interactions. Traditional methodologies struggle to efficiently explore these multidimensional design spaces. This presentation examines emerging applications of AI throughout the chiplet development lifecycle, including architecture exploration, package optimization, interconnect planning, power and thermal prediction, design-space exploration, digital twins, verification acceleration, and design closure. The session discusses both the opportunities and limitations of AI-assisted engineering workflows and how AI can augment engineering expertise. The goal is to stimulate discussion around AI-native methodologies that can accelerate development and improve outcomes for heterogeneous semiconductor systems.

### Federated Chiplet Design and Simulation
Speakers: Kevin Cameron (Cirrus Logic), David Ratchkov (Palo Alto Electron)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9112/
The success of an open chiplet ecosystem depends not only on physical interoperability but also on the ability to exchange design intent, constraints, interfaces, and integration requirements across organizational boundaries. As semiconductor systems become increasingly modular, efficient design exchange becomes critical for enabling collaboration among chiplet providers, integrators, EDA vendors, manufacturers, and end users. This session provides an update on Design Exchange activities within the Open Chiplet Economy and reviews progress toward standardized methods for describing chiplets, packages, interfaces, constraints, and integration metadata. The discussion will examine current challenges, ongoing industry collaboration, and opportunities to improve portability, automation, and interoperability across heterogeneous development flows. The session will focus on ecosystem enablement and practical adoption considerations for scalable chiplet integration.

### An Open Lightweight Serial Interconnect for Composable AI Architectures
Speakers: Vishal Shah (Credo), Allan Cantle (Nallasway)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9635/
AI workload diversity is driving a shift toward modular, domain-specific architectures, exposing limitations in traditional high pin-count, short-reach chiplet interconnects. The OCP OCE Lightweight Serial Interconnect Workstream defines an open, low-power Very Short-Reach (VSR) serial interconnect to enable flexible, lower pin-count connectivity across chiplets, memory, and accelerators. This approach improves system composability, scalability, and manufacturability while enabling new partitioning models for AI systems. Credo’s contribution of lightweight AXI-based framing architecture simplifies integration, enables efficient serial transport of standard interfaces, and supports modular system composition. Contributed as an open specification to OCP, OmniConnect accelerates ecosystem interoperability while delivering scalable, power-efficient connectivity for next-generation AI platforms.

### Introduction to a new OCP FCSA Sub-Project Workstream
Speakers: Junie Um (Cadence Design Systems), Kurt Herremans (imec)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9703/
The new workstream serves as a specialized sub-project of the OCP FCSA initiative, dedicated to advancing chiplet-based architectures for physical AI applications. This workstream, led by Cadence and championed by Arm and IMEC, will spearhead and coordinate efforts to standardize the implementation of chiplets in Physical AI systems, driving the development of specifications and best practices back into the FCSA standard. Its charter encompasses the coordination of research and fostering innovation. The group will provide expert guidance on compliance and safety considerations for deploying physical AI in practical, real-world environments. By working closely with other OCP project teams, the workstream aligns its initiatives with overarching standards and strategic priorities, ultimately strengthening innovation and interoperability throughout the OCP ecosystem.

### Physical AI: Addressing the barriers to interoperable chiplets in automotive and robotics
Speakers: David Glasco (Cadence Design Systems), Bart Plackle (IMEC), Mukund Chavan (Rivian), CP Hung (ASE)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9509/
Chiplets enable re-use and scale in physical AI systems, but distributing functionality across independently designed dies introduces new architecture challenges. In such systems, safety can no longer be inferred from isolated components alone; it must be established across chiplets, die-to-die interconnects (D2D), and with system-level supervision. As OCP’s Open Chiplet Economy initiative launches a new workstream to define chiplet interface standards for advanced automotive and robotics applications, we’re assembling a panel to discuss the opportunities and challenges posed by advanced packaging and composable multi-die systems beyond the data center. Speakers from Arm, ASE, Cadence, Imec, and Rivian will discuss the priorities and role of open chiplet standards to accelerate innovation while ensuring reliability, safety, and serviceability.

## Storage

### Storage Track Introduction
Speakers: Ross Stenfort (SanDisk)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8786/
Introduction for the storage Track

### Comprehensive Testing for OCP Datacenter NVMe SSD Specification Compliance
Speakers: Timothy Sharp (Microsoft Corp), Nick Kriczky (Teledyne Lecroy), Rick Walsh (SANBlaze)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9099/
Comprehensive Testing for OCP Datacenter NVMe SSD Specification Compliance. This presentation summarizes the enhancements made to the OCP SSD testing framework introduced by Microsoft at the 2024 OCP Global Summit SSD Testing panel. It highlights new and expanded compliance test coverage for OCP 2.5 and OCP 2.6, updates to the Adaptation Data specification to improve test coverage in NVMe environments, and the joint work between Microsoft and third-party automation vendors to establish industry-standard test suites that help SSD suppliers improve reliability and qualification consistency.

### SNIA SFF spec updates to the Storage Form Factor
Speakers: Anthony Constantine (Micron Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9434/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2xF4Pg6oRtU&pp=ygUVT0NQIHN0b3JhZ2UgdGVjaCB0YWxr | materials: https://www.snia.org/node/19651
Over the last year, several changes to the EDSFF form factors have started to support the next generation of industry needs. Liquid cooling support for the form factors was introduced. Security features were added to the interface to allow for certain operations. Lastly, the form factors need to support the next generation of faster signaling. The goal of this talk is to present on the changes that have occurred to the EDSFF form factors and their related cables and connectors, review any changes that are currently in process, and discuss what future changes may be needed to support future storage use cases.

### QLC Storage in Hyperscalefleet
Speakers: Vineet Parekh (Meta), Joseph Cirnigliaro (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9504/
As hyperscale data centers continue to scale capacity to meet ever-growing storage demands, Quad-Level Cell (QLC) NAND has emerged as a compelling solution offering significant density and cost-per-bit advantages over TLC. This talk examines the practical realities of deploying QLC SSDs at fleet scale, including workload characterization and qualification strategies, endurance management in mixed read/write environments, performance tail-latency considerations, and the operational tooling required to maintain reliability at scale. We share real-world data on wear-leveling patterns, read-disturb mitigation, and fleet health metrics that inform drive lifecycle management.

### Near-Line SSD: the Path to the Future
Speakers: David Palmer (Micron Technology), Steve Hetzler (Micron Technology)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9536/
The recent shortages and price increases in the storage market have made it difficult to plan for storage shifts. However, the underlying issues around storage have not changed - the vast majority of the EB are used for warm data. We will discuss the opportunity, advantages and decision points involved in making the transition to QLC NAND flash as technology choice for warm data and how those decision points relate to the requirements in the OCP DC NVMe SSD specification - including performance, form-factor, firmware architecture, fixed requirements across capacity and blast radius.

### QLC Writes the end of SLC caching
Speakers: Stephen Gold (SanDisk)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8788/
As QLC SSD performance increases every generation, the problem of how much power is used for writes becomes increasingly important. QLC NAND typically exhibits slower write performance than TLC NAND (which can mean increased write power). QLC writes are normally a two-step program called “foggy-fine”, but generally the first “foggy” write is not readable and that data must be protected against power failure. The standard protection method is to copy the data into an SLC cache until the data is fully programmed into the NAND, but this has material write power/performance impact.​ ​ We present QLC Direct Write – a novel approach that eliminates the need for an SLC cache to protect the host data until the QLC program is complete. This technology means that the data is in a readable state after the first step program, so no additional PLP protection is needed.​

### Optimizing SSD Performance Testing using FIO Sprandom
Speakers: Vincent Fu (Samsung), Dan Helmick (Samsung)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8923/
To optimize enterprise SSD performance testing, the Sprandom feature within Flexible I/O Tester (FIO) was introduced to accelerate random write preconditioning. This session explores how to maximize the tool's utility by analyzing its extensibility under varying test parameters. We explain a methodology for Over-Provisioning (OP) values to reliably be extrapolated across different Protection Information (PI) values. Further, some hosts will only partially fill an SSD during usage, and this is known as changing the utilization of an SSD. This presentation will extrapolate an initial OP value to various utilization settings. Attendees will learn how to ensure a more consistent preconditioned state and leverage Sprandom across a broader array of performance test cases to increase performance testing efficiency.

### High Capacity QLC Innovation
Speakers: Yang Liu (ScaleFlux), Rory Bolt (Kioxia America Incorporated), Sebastien Jean (Phison Electronics), Mark Dancho (SanDisk)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8774/
This panel will cover innovation involving high capacity QLC SSDs.

### NVM Express State of the Union: NVMe® Technology Roadmap for Hyperscale Infrastructure
Speakers: Ross Stenfort (SanDisk), Chris Sabol (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9206/ | materials: https://nvmexpress.org/
As data-intensive workloads continue to drive demand for scalable, high-performance storage, the NVMe technology ecosystem is evolving to address the needs of hyperscale deployments and next-generation infrastructure. This session will provide a State of the Union update on the NVMe technology landscape, highlighting recent achievements, ecosystem momentum, and ongoing industry collaboration. Attendees will gain insight into the NVMe specification roadmap, including the latest Technical Proposals (TPs) designed to enhance performance, efficiency, and manageability in hyperscale environments.

### IO Prioritization for Optimized Command Handling
Speakers: Yuliya Tarnikova (Google), Dan Helmick (Samsung), Chaitanya Kulkarni (NVIDIA)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9642/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?app=desktop&v=fzEFi45ak7E
NVMe® TP4221 IO Prioritization aims to refine the mechanism for prioritizing commands within specific Submission Queue (SQ) groups. In this panel session, the cosponsors will describe the various development priorities and discuss a deployment use-case centered on differentiated command handling. Technical objectives will be highlighted, specifically the extensibility of the prioritization framework to facilitate streamlined integration from the storage through the software stack. Furthermore, the session will address the migration path for SCSI HDDs employing Command Duration Limits (CDL) into the NVMe ecosystem. The panel will include a discussion of graceful proportional degradation when the IO priorities cannot be met. This session provides a comprehensive overview of the cosponsors' diverse technical preferences during the TP development process, and audience members will have an opportunity to bring up their own concerns as TP4221 deploys into the ecosystem.

### Recent Advancements in OCP Telemetry for SSDs
Speakers: Kevin Brandt (Micron Technology), Dhankaran Ajravat (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8980/
The OCP Datacenter NVMe SSD 2.8 specification introduces significant new capabilities for field debug and telemetry. The most impactful is the expansion of human-readable debug coverage. While previous versions required Data Areas 1 and 2 to be decodable by the nvme-cli OCP plugin, OCP 2.8 extends this to Data Areas 3 and 4. This creates a clear migration path toward OCP Telemetry becoming a single collection point for all human-readable debug data — reducing dependence on vendor-specific tools for root-cause analysis. This presentation will explore the full set of telemetry changes in OCP 2.8. The presentation will also discuss advancements in standardizing the toolchain across vendors and operating systems. Finally, the presentation will discuss opportunities for future collaboration to improve OCP telemetry and proposals to map industry-standard log pages into OCP Telemetry formats, enabling all human-readable debug data to be captured in a single pull with a single decode tool.

### The Open Flash Platform: Serverless Exabyte-Scale Flash Storage with Xsight Labs and Hammerspace
Speakers: Floyd Christofferson (Hammerspace), Gary Grider (Los Alamos National Lab), Ted Weatherford (Xsight Labs)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9307/ | materials: https://openflashplatform.org/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=k3WUJ2P-UO4
As AI clusters scale, traditional x86 storage architectures are collapsing under the sheer volume of unstructured data. This panel introduces the Open Flash Platform (OFP), a new architecture for building flash storage at exabyte-scale. The panel will detail how the integration of Xsight Labs’ E1 800G 64-core Arm DPU into an ultra-dense OFP flash storage system eliminates x86 storage servers to reduce power and rack space. By combining the OFP architecture with Hammerspace’s standards-based parallel file system, AI companies, research institutions, and neoclouds can build exabyte-scale flash storage at densities up to 10x today’s flash systems. We will share real-world insights from LANL on how open standards are building the future of exabyte-scale, serverless AI storage

### Vertical Optimizations for KV$ Infrastructure
Speakers: Dongjoo Seo (Samsung), Javier Gonzalez (Samsung)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9161/ | materials: https://github.com/lmcache/lmcache
KV Caches (KV$s) are at the core of modern AI infrastructure given their importance in Inference and Agentic AI workloads. Current deployments and ongoing research focus on using SSDs as the density tier to improve TCO while maintaining acceptable SLAs. This presentation will dive on specific SSD optimizations targeted at KV$ workloads and platforms (e.g.,Nvidia CMX). Specifically, we will focus on how the ephemeral nature of KV$ allow us to relax SSD guarantees to provide better IOPS, latencies, and DWPD. In the process, we will showcase how these SSD optimizations integrate end-to-end with LMCache, which is the most popular KV$ engine in the industry.

### Increasing Block Sizing Changes Data Placement Economics
Speakers: Mike James (SanDisk)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8920/
As block sizes in the underlying NAND media in SSDs increase to accommodate higher capacities there are tradeoffs that significantly change the cost structure for implementing small vs large unit data placement strategies. These tradeoffs have both performance and cost implications that are complex due to changes in WAF, SLC cache required and data protection schemes. In this presentation we examine the current techniques and requirements for small vs large unit data placement and apply those to assumed future NAND block sizes to understand the economics of leveraging the same techniques in the future. This analysis should help inform the industry of potential changes that should be made to accommodate their data placement infrastructure as drive capacities begin to exceed 256TB. This is important to begin aligning host data placement techniques with data placement available in future SSDs

### OCP Storage Specifications Update and What Customers Care About
Speakers: David Black (Dell Technologies), Timothy Sharp (Microsoft Corp.), Chris Sabol (Google), Jeff Wolford (HPE), Vineet Parekh (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8797/
Update on OCP specs and what customers care about.

### Adopting high-capacity HDDs, navigating throughput and IOPS Limits using SAS/NVME interfaces for the AI Cloud
Speakers: Hariharan Kumar (Microsoft), Balaji Natrajan (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9096/ | pdf: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3767295.3803599 | materials: https://investor.wdc.com/static-files/c4cec531-0128-4c17-b9c2-f25644c658db
Hyperscale operational data reveals a widening mismatch between HDD capacity growth and achievable IO performance. As areal density increases and IO density per TB declines, both throughput and IOPS constraints are becoming first-order design limitations. This talk examines how real-world cloud workloads drive the evolution of HDD interfaces and architectures, highlighting emerging limits of SATA throughput scalability and single-actuator IOPS. We explore trade-offs across faster SATA, SAS, and NVMe interfaces, and their implications on cloud platform design. Finally, we discuss broader ecosystem impacts and standardization opportunities—framing areas for collaboration within the OCP community to enable the next generation of high-capacity, performant storage systems.

### Storage as a Performance Tier for LLM Serving
Speakers: Joohee Lee (SK hynix)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9339/ | materials: https://github.com/skhynix/LMCache/wiki/Storage-as-a-Performance-Tier-for-LLM-Serving
Prompt caching reduces LLM inference latency by reusing previously computed KV states. As reusable KV working sets rapidly exceed practical DRAM capacity, SSD-backed cache tiers are becoming essential for LLM serving. We show that prompt caching fundamentally shifts the prefill bottleneck from GPU computation to KV transfer bandwidth. Scaling from one to four SSDs reduces TTFT by 42%, while SSD I/O optimizations provide an additional 45% reduction. Together, these techniques enable SSD-backed prompt caching to approach DRAM-class latency at low cost. While these optimizations bring SSD-backed caching close to DRAM-class latency, a gap remains between storage and accelerator bandwidth. We argue that software optimization alone cannot fully bridge this gap. This presentation discusses emerging architectures, including KVSSD-based designs, faster interfaces, and flash-near-GPU architectures. We propose storage as an active performance tier through tighter storage-accelerator co-design.

### Storage Track Close

## Sustainability

### Updates from an exciting year with the OCP Sustainability Project, DCF Sustainability Project  & Roadmap ahead
Speakers: Alexander Rakow (Schneider Electric), Shruti Sethi (Microsoft), Priya Chhiba (Google), Sung A Kim (bp Castrol)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9989/
At a time when sustainability is again becoming a necessity for community support and regulatory approval, the OCP Sustainability Project has been busy across many domains. Come hear about our workstreams tackling decarbonization of equipment and construction materials, circularity, air emissions, advanced energy and water efficiency, and other topics. We will discuss how we’re collaborating with other organizations, and our goals for the year ahead.

### Cooling the Cloud: Regional Tradeoffs in U.S. Data Center Cooling Architectures
Speakers: Sammy Houssainy (Daikin Applied Americas), Karim Shalash (Daikin Applied Americas)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9350/
Cooling systems account for 30–40% of data center energy use and drive major impacts on emissions, water consumption, and grid demand. As hyperscale and AI driven facilities expand, selecting cooling architectures that balance efficiency, water use, cost, and sustainability across diverse climates is increasingly critical. This study compares key cooling system types—including air-cooled chillers and water-cooled chillers with wet cooling towers. Hourly simulations quantify annual energy use, peak demand, water consumption, operating cost, and emissions using regional utility data, EPA eGRID emission factors, and future grid scenarios through 2050 from NLR’s Cambium dataset. Results show strong regional variation and provide actionable guidance for selecting efficient, low impact cooling strategies under current and future grid conditions.

### Performance-Normalized Embodied Rack Carbon Intensity for Next-Generation Hardware
Speakers: Kali Frost (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9647/
Performance-normalized metrics, alongside absolute metrics, provide a more complete view of sustainability progress: intensity metrics show whether infrastructure is becoming more carbon-efficient as performance and capacity scale, while absolute metrics track whether total emissions are making progress toward company climate goals. Microsoft developed a rack-level embodied carbon intensity methodology to better normalize, compare, and communicate supply chain emissions across rapidly evolving IT hardware systems. The approach translates upstream lifecycle emissions associated with compute, storage, and AI hardware into performance-normalized views, enabling more consistent comparison across architecture generations and deployment mixes, while accounting for capacity growth. This approach ensures that advancements in semiconductor technology scaling, hardware architectural design, and Azure utilization and efficiency can be evaluated more consistently over time.

### DCEM workstream update: Case studies of metric measurements from servers to data centers
Speakers: Babak Falsafi (Swiss Datacenter Efficiency Association), Murugasamy Nachimuthu (Oracle), Sadas Shankar (SLAC)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9718/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/community/sustainability
This presentation will provide an update on the DCEM Metrics workstream activities. In OCP EMEA we presented detailed measurements and validation of PSU loss, fan power vs. load and fan speed, idle power, and the impact of including fan power as part of IT in PUE when comparing PUE between air cooled and liquid cooled servers. This presentation will continue presenting results on case studies across the entire stack including compute, cooling and power conversion with varying load and will also present a characterization of server noise. In addition, it will include measurements with controlling temperature on PUE for both air-cooled and liquid-cooled servers. Finally, we will also include results on exploring the pareto-optimal frontier for CPU design based on throughput/power and throughput/silicon area for datacenter workloads including CloudSuite, DCPerf and DeathStar microservices benchmarks.

### Sustainability and best practices for water efficiency
Speakers: Rishab Vardhan Harikrishnan (Oracle), Derek Kauneckis (Vertiv)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8629/ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1gYkt7SacOX6WepUU5agfu-0qUAzYeCgdNXRCvxNcytY/edit?tab=t.0 | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Qvw0OF_GCe0
As AI clusters scale rapidly, data center cooling architectures are evolving beyond traditional air-cooled systems toward liquid cooling, immersion, hybrid adiabatic systems, and advanced facility water loops. Existing sustainability metrics such as WUE and PUE provide useful directional guidance but fail to capture the full operational, geographic, and lifecycle complexity of water consumption in modern AI infrastructure. This panel brings together contributors from the OCP water efficiency workstream to discuss emerging best practices for holistic water accounting across cooling technologies, facility loops, and regional water conditions. Topics include stress-weighted water metrics, cooling loop boundaries, reuse and replenishment strategies, embodied water in power generation, operational resilience, and normalization across diverse cooling architectures.

### Climate Volatility in Daily Operations,  A Community Conversation with Operators, Hyperscalers, and Vendors
Speakers: Maheshwari Neelam (USRA), Miranda Gardiner (iMasons Climate Accord), Emily Wasley (Meta), Jessica Gullbrand (Eaton)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8857/
Climate intelligence exists yet it rarely enters daily data center operations. It surfaces in risk assessments and project planning, but not in real-time load management, workload shifting, or DCIM workflows. This session is a structured community conversation exploring three barriers: the technical gap preventing climate forecast data from being actionably integrated into DCIM platforms; the institutional gap that keeps climate as a planning concern rather than an operational one; and the community tension, when climate events strike, data center load competes directly with community needs for water and grid capacity. What does responsible operation look like in that moment, and what stops operators from building that into daily decisions? The session centers operator and hyperscaler experience to map where this conversation stands, identify barriers the OCP community is best placed to address. Vendors, DCIM developers, and sustainability leads welcome.

### Next-Generation Sustainability Dashboards: AI-Powered Optimization for Data Centers
Speakers: Arun Mahendran (Lenovo), Manish Baing (Lenovo)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9511/
As data centers face increasing environmental scrutiny, transitioning from passive reporting to proactive optimization is critical. This presentation introduces a next-generation Data Center Sustainability Dashboard powered by forecasting telemetry and predictive analytics. We detail the core metrics essential for holistic environmental tracking, including Power, Water, and Carbon Usage Effectiveness (PUE, WUE, CUE), alongside realtime renewable energy ratios. Moving beyond static historical data, the session explores how forecasting telemetry captures high-fidelity thermal, power, and workload metrics to model future resource consumption accurately. We will demonstrate how predictive analytics and machine learning leverage this data to automate cooling optimization, dynamically shift workloads based on renewable grid availability, and preemptively eliminate resource waste. Attendees will learn to build a dashboard that not only visualizes compliance but actively drives net-zero goals.

### Scaling Digital Infrastructure in Hardware Manufacturing: Real-Time Telemetry, Automated Carbon Reduction, and Dynamic LCA
Speakers: Malcolm Hegeman (Google), Brandon Weilbacher (Flex)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9491/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aQG0MN4LmUU | materials: https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/sustainability/google-cloud-measures-its-climate-impact-through-life-cycle-assessment
To support the transition to a net-zero future, real-time, empirical operational telemetry serves as a powerful complement to static carbon emission estimates, enabling faster and more actionable supply chain insights. Building on the 2025 proof-of-concept that monitored a single Google manufacturing line at Flex, this presentation outlines an enterprise-grade digital infrastructure designed to ingest, contextualize, and operationalize real-time operational and information and communication technology (OT/ICT) data. We demonstrate how this scaled architecture supports test engineering through empirical power profiling, drives immediate carbon savings through facility-level optimization, and adds fidelity, such as real-time updates, to carbon emissions accounting through a dynamic Life Cycle Assessment (LCA) framework.

### Enabling Scalable Waste Heat Recovery: Operational Realities, Design Principles, and Value from the Datacenter Perspective
Speakers: Emily Matys (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9452/
Waste heat recovery (WHR) can create shared value—improving efficiency for operators, providing thermal energy for offtakers, and reducing emissions. But scaling WHR requires alignment across parties with distinct constraints and economic models. Today, many assumptions shaping WHR frameworks are defined outside the datacenter industry and don’t reflect operational reality. Grounded in real-world engineering as a datacenter operator pursuing scalable WHR beyond district heating and contributions to the OCP Heat Reuse Subproject, we examine what heat is recoverable, at what temperature, how stable the output is, and how to integrate without compromising uptime. We level-set on WHR fundamentals, design principles for efficiency and positive ROI, and operational realities — giving all parties the shared foundation to build WHR that scales by design, not just for compliance.

### Standardizing Data Center Operational Carbon Tracking: Supplier Metrics vs. Operator Needs
Speakers: Sheng Zhong (ABB)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8925/
This presentation addresses the challenges data center operators face in accurately estimating the operational carbon footprint of their facilities. While equipment suppliers routinely provide environmental documentation, such as Life Cycle Assessments (LCAs), Life Cycle Inventory Analyses (LCIs), and Environmental Product Declarations (EPDs), these documents are frequently made following different assumptions depending on program operators, inconsistent across vendors, or misinterpreted by end-users. Consequently, operators lack a standardized approach to leverage vendor data for reliable operational carbon estimation. Based on a white paper by the Intelligent Lifecycle Management workstream, this session presents the methodologies and documentation required for consistent, transparent assessments. By defining vendor deliverables and application frameworks, this presentation establishes a unified approach enabling operators to make comparable, data-driven footprint decisions.

### Operationalizing the PCR: LCA at Scale and the Open Enablers Program
Speakers: Josue Campos (Meta), Mackenzie Clark (Google)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9484/
The OCP PCR Working Group (Fraunhofer IZM, Meta, Google, Microsoft, AWS) presents LCA at Scale, an implementation companion to the OCP Product Category Rule (PCR) style OCP spec for Life Cycle Assessment (LCA) Studies of ICT hardware. While the PCR defines measurement rules, LCA at Scale operationalizes them at enterprise scale through emission factor assignment, bottom-up aggregation, and data quality reporting. It enables auditable Scope 3 reporting for complex portfolios, in the face of incomplete supplier data. We introduce ‘Open Enablers’, an open-source program by Google, Meta, Microsoft, and AWS to reduce friction for those adopting these frameworks. It provides shared datasets, open tooling, and technical specifications. We present its initial capabilities, governance, and how to contribute.

### From Silicon to System: Embedding Power Efficiency as a Core Design Principle for Scalable AI and Compute Infrastructure
Speakers: Devang Soparkar (Microsoft), Nilanjan Palit (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9633/
As AI and compute platforms rapidly evolve, innovation is driving unprecedented performance gains—often at the cost of increased power consumption. This creates a critical challenge: how do we ensure power efficiency is treated as a fundamental design principle rather than an afterthought? Why does this matter to the broader OCP community as organizations pursue carbon neutral or negative goals , and what practical approaches can enable consistent power reduction across silicon and systems? In this session, Microsoft and Intel will share how we embed power efficiency as a design-time requirement , enabling optimization from silicon to system . We will introduce a structured approach across CPU, memory, storage, accelerators, and NICs, and invite the industry to adopt and contribute toward sustainability as a key design tenet , reducing energy and carbon while sustaining performance.

### Old Batteries, New Intelligence: Powering AI Sustainably
Speakers: Arya Sasne (Crusoe Energy Systems), Sharmila Bokka (Crusoe Energy Systems)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9813/ | materials: https://www.crusoe.ai/resources/newsroom/crusoe-and-redwood-materials-expand-strategic-partnership-scaling-to-7x-the-original-ai-infrastructure-density
The binding constraint on AI buildout is power and the years-long wait for grid interconnection, and not chips. To bypass this bottleneck without relying on fossil fuels, Crusoe and Redwood Materials co-developed a radical alternative: a 12 MW / 63 MWh off-grid microgrid combining on-site solar with repurposed, second-life EV batteries to power factory-built modular AI data centers. This session delivers a transparent operational breakdown of our first full year. We’ll share what energy-first, vertically integrated design unlocks, and how we partner with new energy technologies for a cleaner AI future. A circular, lower-carbon power path for AI is not a concept and it has run for a year, and the data is in.

### Closing the Loop: Structural Steel Reuse in Hyperscale Datacenter Construction — A Scalable Blueprint from Microsoft's Newport Imperial Park Datacenter
Speakers: John O’Sullivan (Microsoft), Anjali Bhagat (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9625/ | materials: https://www.linkedin.com/posts/eoin-doherty-0204413_teamwork-newport-datacentres-activity-7351914067474685952-zsj3/
This session presents Microsoft's first end-to-end structural steel reuse pilot in datacenter construction. At CWL01 in Newport, South Wales, a legacy industrial complex was selectively deconstructed and ~10% of structural steel was reclaimed (249 tonnes), tested to SCI P427 and EN 1090 standards, and reintegrated into a new datacenter build. Combined with 76% recycled-content steel, the strategy saved an estimated 4,400 tCO₂e (520 tonnes of CO2 from the reclaimed steel) and achieved over 80% waste diversion during construction. We detail the cross-disciplinary workflows spanning design, deconstruction, testing, certification, and construction that made reuse viable at scale. The session introduces a repeatable, standards-aligned framework — intended to inform a broader OCP Circularity Ecosystem contribution and support industry adoption of circular materials in datacenter infrastructure. Presenters: Anjali Bhagat & John O’Sullivan

### Closing the Visibility Gap: An AI-Driven Supply Chain Intelligence Framework for Water Stewardship in Mission-Critical Data Center Infrastructure
Speakers: Ojonimi Bako (Kaya AI), Michael Obradovitch (Ecolab)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9587/
Water accounts for an estimated 77% of corporate water consumption occurring upstream and outside direct operational visibility - yet the industry lacks a standardized tracking framework across the data center build-and-operate lifecycle. This paper presents a joint framework by Kaya AI and Ecolab combining AI-driven supply chain intelligence with water treatment science to: (1) map water touchpoints across construction and operational supply chains, (2) deliver predictive analytics for leak detection and treatment optimization, and (3) generate auditable ESG and Net Zero transparency reporting. An active four-country pilot across Kenya, Ghana, Nigeria, and Ethiopia has achieved a 35% reduction in industrial wastewater discharge and 72-hour predictive leak detection windows. We propose this framework as a candidate for OCP community discussion on standardized water-reuse accounting for next-generation data center facilities

### Product Category Rule-style Specification for Life Cycle Assessment on Datacenter ICT-Equipment: Collaborative Progress & Draft publication
Speakers: Lisa Rivalin (Meta), Stephan Benecke (Google), Ines Sousa (Microsoft), Vincenzo Ferrero (Amazon)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9618/ | materials: https://datacenters.microsoft.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/Whitepaper_Cloud-hardware-emissions-methodology.pdf | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HS9Dz3ksOcU
The OCP Product Category Rule (PCR) Working Group (Fraunhofer IZM, Meta, Google, Microsoft, AWS) soft-published in June, the first PCR-style OCP Specification (Spec) for datacenter ICT hardware LCAs. Since OCP EMEA 2026, the program has advanced on 5 fronts: a formal consultation with supplier feedback shaping the November publication; an HDD commodity pilot progressing toward validation; a 3-level component taxonomy (PCBA, tray, rack) structuring the Spec; Data Quality Scoring embedded as the default methodology for data quality assessment and carbon aggregation; and Open Enablers building open-source tooling for Spec-compliant assessment. This panel discusses consultation findings, how supplier feedback is reshaping the standard, and where the HDD pilot validates or challenges our assumptions. We need input on which commodities to prioritize next, what modeling guidance is missing, how Open Enablers can support adoption, and the path to formal publication. Join us to shape the Spec!

### The Green Tokens: From Green Computing to Sustainably Intelligent
Speakers: Yen Haw Chen (Taiwan Institute of Economic Research (TIER))
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9123/
The AI Token is the basic unit of the AI world—AI's smallest semantic unit for language—reflecting GPU and power use and enabling cost forecasting. As AI pursues greater intelligence and throughput, models keep scaling toward larger, higher-power systems. Higher-quality AI thus comes with higher cost and environmental impact, risking sustainability. This session explores "Green Tokens," building on how renewable energy certificates and green mileage transformed energy and transport into green growth economies. A Green Tokens mechanism could raise environmental awareness among AI providers and users, enable eco-friendly offerings, and ensure AI delivers financial and environmental value alike. A "Green Token" is produced via Green Software—using clean energy and efficient resources—making it more sustainable than a standard token: the output of Green Computing, or "Sustainably Intelligent." Taiwan is developing this mechanism and invites community input toward future pilots.

### Increasing Rigor in Data Center Carbon Disclosure
Speakers: Andrea Desimone (Schneider Electric), Lalit Joshi (Microsoft), Malcolm Hegeman (Google), Miranda Gardiner (iMasons Climate Accord)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9585/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/carbon-disclosure-ocp-base-specification-20250730-pdf
An update to the OCP Base Specification for Embodied Carbon Disclosure will help improve the validity and comparability of carbon data for data center procurement. The joint effort between OCP and the iMasons Climate Accord has made progress toward this update through an investigation into the availability and quality of Product Category Rules (PCR) for the products purchased for data center construction & operations. Workstream leadership will share the results of this investigation, and will provide an updated roadmap for version 2.0 of the Base Specification for Embodied Carbon Disclosure. This next iteration will enable Data Center Owner/Operators to require more consistent and higher-quality data in their procurements. The panel will discuss how this investigation is providing value to data centers looking to reduce the carbon impact of their data center builds, and how it is helping vendors to further their low-carbon solution designs.

## Systems Management

### Hardware Management Project Journey
Speakers: Hemal Shah (Broadcom), Jeff Hilland (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9874/
The hardware management project started almost 10 years ago in OCP. It has continued to expand its scope and provide a foundation for all other OCP projects. In this talk, we will provide an overview of hardware management project and its history. We will also summarize the latest work happening in the work streams of system GPU, RAS API, fleet memory fault management, manageability profiles, scalable cloud infrastructure management (SCIM), auxiliary management controller (AMC), system debug, and open boot management framework.

### OCP Hardware Fault Management Workstreams Status and Relationship
Speakers: Theodros Yigzaw (NVIDIA), Vilas Sridharan (AMD), Janusz Jurski (Intel), Shubhada Pugaonkar (NVIDIA), Samer El-Haj-Mahmoud (Arm)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9865/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/wiki/Hardware_Management/Hardware_Fault_Management
As a collaboration of the who's who of contemporary AI-sale data-center fleet innovation roaster the OCP Hardware Fault Management (HWFM) subproject and its workstreams have organically created a comprehensive fault management framework to cater to the scalability of the state-of-the-art heterogeneous AI fleet. This session brings out this innovative work in a unique combination of status followed by moderator and audience probed exposition of how these workstreams tie together to form the end-to-end OCP HWFM Framework. The panelists span across HWFM, Fleet Memory Fault Management, RAS API, GPU and CPU RAS. The major theme is the comprehensive end-to-end scalable AI Fleet fault management and topics include industry standards based vendor agnostic compute, memory, PCIe, and CXL error telemetry for AI-driven predictive fault analysis and discovery, configuration, RAS action and error injection for automation. Join us and learn about the workstreams and how you can get involved.

### Prescribing remote manageability for IT/OT
Speakers: John Leung (Intel), Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9450/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/HWMgmt-OCP-Profiles
OCP prescribes the remote manageability of IT platforms and OT equipment components via the OCP-defined Redfish profiles. In the last year, a baseline profile, service baseline profile, and Ethernet NIC profile have been contributed. This session describes these profiles and summarizes the profiles that will be contributed soon.

### Hierarchical Device Managers to manage complex Multi-Rack systems
Speakers: Avinash Natarajan (Celestica), Navinsennivel Nallasivam (Celestica)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9929/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/HWMgmt-DeviceMgr-DeviceManager | video: https://youtu.be/JBU99RIkqtY?si=Urzh9UCM9sTeHQaB
Open Rack Management Controller–Device Manager(OpenRMC-DM/DM) provides an abstraction layer to access multiple BMCs within a rack over Redfish.We extrapolate this to build a hierarchy for complex multi-rack systems.We introduce a hierarchy of nested DMs.A Level-1 DM would be equivalent to the existing DM instance that abstracts a single Rack.A Level-2 DM would be a higher order DM (say, a pod level DM) that would talk to multiple Level-1 DMs.A Level-3 DM would talk to multiple Level-2 DMs.The notion of nested DMs would necessitate a hierarchy of redfish end point URIs that are in conformance with the redfish standards.We achieve this by attributing a UUID to a DM at each level, and using it in the URI to demultiplex across DM levels.The talk will get into the specifics of how OpenRMC-DM works today,how exactly the demultiplexing happens ,and how it all comes together to offer a clean way to expand on OpenRMC-DM to manage complex multi-rack systems.

### System-Level Manageability for GPU Platforms — Progress in the OCP System GPU Management Workstream
Speakers: John Leung (Intel), Hari Ramachandran (Microsoft Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9455/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-hm-system-gpu-management/wiki
The OCP System GPU Management workstream focuses on system-level manageability for GPU-based platforms. Building upon the contributions of the hyperscale GPU management workstream, this workstream addresses key challenges in aggregating GPU subsystems into a unified system management model. This includes defining approaches for system-level aggregation, message standardization, and state rollup to enable consistent visibility and control across heterogeneous GPU platforms.

### Creating Aggregation Pipelines to Conquer Large Telemetry Challenges
Speakers: Jim Harford (Broadcom)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9381/
As data centers grow in size and complexity, it becomes even more important to manage their hardware components in an effective, efficient, and timely manner; such that data center service levels and financial goals can be realized. Today’s environment can be characterized by gathering mostly irrelevant metrics into a “data swamp”, with little hope of providing timely, valuable insights. A key part of our work in 2026 has been the modeling of building blocks to aggregate metrics across time, multiple sources, and contextual inclusion or exclusion. The building blocks will allow data center operators to dynamically tailor and deploy aggregation pipelines that efficiently monitor equipment health, support investigative deep dives into perceived anomalies, and provide real-time signals for integration with control automation. These building blocks may be distributed among original sources of metrics, specialized aggregation servers, and the ultimate nodes that store or report metrics.

### OCP Auxiliary Management Controller overview
Speakers: Gregg Shick (HPE), Jeff Hilland (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9658/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-HM-AMC-Auxiliary_Management_Controller
This presentation examines the evolution of the Auxiliary Management Controller (AMC) specification from its v1.0 baseline to the 1.1 Version. It highlights key architectural refinements, expanded capabilities for multi-controller and composable environments, and improvements in scalability, interoperability, and manageability across diverse hardware platforms. The session also covers clarifications to protocol behavior, alignment with adjacent OCP and industry standards, and updates that better support modern deployment and serviceability models. Attendees will gain a concise understanding of why these changes were introduced, how they impact system design and integration, and what they enable for next-generation OCP hardware management implementations.

### DMTF PMCI Enhancements for OCP Hardware Management
Speakers: Patrick Caporale (Lenovo), Hemal Shah (Broadcom)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9174/ | materials: https://www.dmtf.org/standards/pmci
OCP has been leveraging DMTF MCTP, PLDM, and NC-SI specifications for platform management communications. MCTP Base and Binding specifications have been extended to cover new capabilities that are instrumental in supporting the latest hardware architecture and infrastructure. PLDM specifications recently added new support for advanced modeling use cases and new methods of describing hardware components at large scale. A new open source repository for PMCI tools has been made available to streamline the validation and testing across all elements of a Platform Management Subsystem. In this talk, we will describe the latest enhancements to MCTP, PLDM, and NC-SI specifications, and how they can be leveraged for advanced OCP use cases for diagnostics and operational management of OCP platform components.

### Runtime SBOMs: Extending DMTF Redfish to Connect Build-Time and Live Software State
Speakers: Harsh Bhuwania (Microsoft), Prashant Dewan (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9214/ | materials: https://www.dmtf.org/sites/default/files/standards/documents/DSP0266_1.23.0.pdf
Software Bills of Materials (SBOMs) provide build-time transparency into software composition but remain largely static, with limited operational value in hyperscale environments. Once deployed, there is no consistent way to correlate SBOM data with live system state. At scale, this gap leads to blind spots in CVE assessment, opaque firmware drift, and compliance workflows driven by assumptions rather than verifiable runtime data. The industry lacks a standardized control-plane model to expose, query, and validate software composition during operations. This talk explores how SBOMs can evolve into runtime signals. It examines approaches to bind SBOM artifacts to live systems through management interfaces, metadata services, and hardware-rooted measurement frameworks. Drawing from OpenBMC and Redfish-based extensions, it highlights a path toward operationalizing SBOMs while calling for broader ecosystem alignment.

### GPU & CPU Diagnostics and RAS Standardization Advances for Hyperscale Infrastructure
Speakers: Rama Bhimanadhuni (Microsoft), Taniya Siddiqua (Advanced Micro Devices, Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9160/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-gpu-and-accelerators-ras-requirements-v1-7-10-23-2025-pdf
This session presents advances from the Hyperscale GPU and CPU OCP workstream — a hyperscaler/silicon-vendor collaboration — in two areas: diagnostics standardization and RAS improvements. As accelerator fleets scale faster than failures can be managed, fine-grained AI parallelism turns single-device faults into cluster-wide interruptions, and checkpoint-restart alone can't sustain efficiency. Diagnostics & Screening: Targeting the No Fault Found problem driving high MTTR, the standardized framework delivers a repeatable screening/diagnostics recipe, actionable pass/fail dispositions replacing raw logs, and full lifecycle coverage (manufacturing, Out-of-Repair, production validation). Blast Radius Reduction: Limiting a single fault's impact via a recovery mechanism taxonomy, workload-aware recovery for AI training vs. inference, and actionable telemetry enabling automated FRU isolation and root-cause analysis without manual intervention.

### Graph Query Language APIs for OpenBMC Metrics; OpenRMC-DM as a Proxy GraphQL-Server
Speakers: Avinash Natarajan (Celestica), Navinsennivel Nallasivam (Celestica)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9954/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/HWMgmt-DeviceMgr-DeviceManager | video: https://youtu.be/JBU99RIkqtY?si=Urzh9UCM9sTeHQaB
Redfish APIs are modeled after REST, and the API count scales proportional to the number of sensors.While Redfish has Telemetry Services to facilitate access to a specific set of metrics,what we see in practice is that Vendors default to implementing just the scalar GETs/SETs,requiring GET loops to get multiple data pointsThis necessitates OEM APIs for any kind of consolidation leading to onboarding delays.This talk proposes a GraphQL style API for OpenBMC.GraphQL lets clients request exactly the data they need through a single API.The talk proposes a middle path,where we extend our implementation of OCP’s OpenRMC-DM to act as a Proxy Graph-QL server bridging requests between GraphQL Clients and existing Redfish Servers, while also optimizing Redfish access, thus giving administrators GraphQL benefits without an overhaul of existing Redfish server implementations.

### From Lab to Fleet: A Secure, Unified Debug Framework for Cloud Systems
Speakers: Suresh Duthiraru (Microsoft), Rolf Kuehnis (Lauterbach Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8804/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-HM-Hardware_Management/wiki/Wiki-%E2%80%90-System-Debug
Modern data centers struggle with debug at scale: traditional JTAG and probe-based approaches require physical access, lack security controls, and cannot meet hyperscale operational demands. This work presents a unified, standards-based debug framework spanning the full product lifecycle, from pre-silicon validation to in-field diagnostics. Unlike current OCP debug efforts focused on data models and file formats, this framework also addresses orchestration and execution: the BMC acts as a debug proxy, handling run-control, memory access, and trace capture without external probes. A modular Debug and Test Controller mitigates network latency through local execution. Secure, multi-tenant access is enforced via SPDM-based transport with authentication and access control. The result is a vendor-neutral architecture that extends beyond the BMC to host CPUs and accelerators. We present real latency and bandwidth measurements from a live data center deployment and outline future directions.

### OBMF Specifications and Workstream Status Update
Speakers: ofer yehielli (Google), MARIUSZ ORIOL (NVIDIA), Janusz Jurski (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8940/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-obmf/wiki
Presentation provides an update on the developments in OBMF WS, with a particular focus on release of v1.0 of the OBMF-ICP (Interface Consolidation Protocol) spec. It highlights key platform use cases and topology optimizations enabled by the spec, & outlines core elements of the protocol stack, including the discovery process, channel definitions, & important flows. It also covers the status of the rest of the protocol stack: OBMF-ICP over MCTP specification (rev 0.8WIP published by DMTF in cooperation with OBMF WS) and OBMF-ICP over USB specifications (rev 1.0 released by OCP). Additionally, we highlight how OBMF WS work relates to, reuses, & contributes to the OCP PNP and DC-SCM WSs. It also serves as an intro to related presentations/demos submitted by OBMF WS members, which explore specific subtopics in greater detail. Request to extend time with 3 presenters: Google, Intel, NVIDIA. In the past (e.g. EMEA) we did multiple submissions resulting in a merge. Submitting merged now.

### Operating OpenBMC at Hyperscale: What Works, What Breaks, and What Needs to Change
Speakers: Thirupathaiah Annapureddy (Microsoft), Hari Ramachandran (Microsoft Corporation)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9498/
OpenBMC has enabled flexibility, faster innovation, and stronger ecosystem collaboration in hyperscale environments. However, operating OpenBMC at scale introduces challenges that are not visible in smaller or more homogeneous deployments. As infrastructure spans multiple SoC generations, ODM implementations, and backend control planes, maintaining consistency, reliability, and lifecycle manageability becomes increasingly complex. This session shares lessons from large-scale OpenBMC deployments, highlighting where it delivers real value, where operational complexity emerges, and how scale exposes gaps in manageability, observability, and integration across the ecosystem. Rather than focusing on a single implementation, we frame the broader challenges hyperscalers face and identify areas where the OCP community can drive better alignment for scalable, interoperable management in modern datacenters.

### From Scale to Security: Evolving GPU Management Standards in 2026
Speakers: Krishna Sugumaran (AMD), Sujoy Sen (Google), Akkiah (Choudary) Maddukuri (Microsoft), Qian Wang (NVIDIA), Balaji Vembu (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9705/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-gpu-fw-update-specification-v1-1-pdf
Scaling AI and HPC demands robust, standardized GPU management interfaces and firmware update mechanisms across multi-vendor fleets. The OCP GPU Management Interfaces and Firmware Update workstreams (AMD, Google, Meta, Microsoft, NVIDIA) present our 2026 advancements to further defragment hyperscale deployments. This presentation details strides in observability, security, and lifecycle management. We highlight updates to: high-frequency telemetry architectures for low-latency data, a refreshed SPDM security profile, vendor-agnostic Redfish resource naming, and new mechanisms for Redfish event discovery and synthetic injection to improve test coverage. Additionally, we detail firmware update progress: standardizing the Minimum Secure Version Number (Min SVN) via Redfish for strict rollback protection, a fail-fast update approach for rapid error mitigation, and prescriptive PLDM bundle semantics. Together, these efforts ensure reliable, secure fleet-wide GPU management at hyperscale.

## Test & Validation

### CTAM: Three Years of Bridging Hyperscalers and Suppliers Through GPU Accelerator Compliance
Speakers: Aditya Mishra (NVIDIA), khader Basha (AMD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8812/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-diag-ctam
The Compliance Tool for Accelerator Management (CTAM) is an open-source framework enabling GPU accelerator compliance verification across OCP specifications for Firmware Update, Manageability Interfaces, and RAS. Over three years, CTAM has evolved from addressing nascent compliance needs of AI platforms to becoming the de-facto yardstick suppliers use to validate firmware quality before delivering to hyperscalers. This presentation chronicles CTAM's journey — the collaborative engineering between hyperscalers and suppliers, the evolution of compliance scenarios and scoring schemes, and measurable firmware quality improvements that have accelerated time-to-market. Through real-world examples, we present unique perspectives from both sides of the ecosystem: how suppliers leverage CTAM as an early quality gate to reduce integration defects, and how hyperscalers use it to streamline accelerator qualification at scale.

### T&V Update: Wind Tunnels & Bonsai servers
Speakers: Jean-Marie Verdun (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Arun Darlie Koshy (Hewlett Packard Enterprise)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8939/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-diag-windtunnel
We cover updates regarding T&V effort at large including: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-diag-windtunnel updates Bonsai servers Streaming firmware

### AI-Native Hardware Validation: Building a Local-Context Agentic Test and Debug Platform
Speakers: Wenxi Yan (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9880/ | materials: https://github.com/microsoft/mcconsole
Hardware validation is iterative: define tests, run them on systems-under-test, collect logs, analyze failures, file bugs, refine tests, and repeat. AI can help only when it has local-first context across logs, command history, bug records, decoded signatures, specs, knowledge bases, and prior debug knowledge. This session presents an AI-native, local-data-first validation architecture for the OCP Test & Validation community. The platform connects an agentic test harness, SUT execution, log capture, bug database awareness, log indexing, signature search, spec and knowledge-base retrieval, and conversational control into a closed loop. Instead of treating tests, logs, bugs, and specs as disconnected systems, the agent uses them together to plan tests, analyze failures, and feed lessons back into the source of truth. We show how open-source components and local or enterprise-hosted LLMs reduce log searching, preserve debug history, and turn each run into reusable root-cause context.

### From Spec to Sign-off - How Microsoft is transforming the platform validation workflow leveraging AI-powered, agent-driven solutions.
Speakers: Rahul Shah (Microsoft), Vikram Vemulapalli (Microsoft)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9724/
Modern hardware platforms have outgrown traditional validation workflows. Engineers spend significant time translating specifications into test plans, coordinating execution, analyzing failures, and assembling sign-off evidence. NPI and sustaining validation have traditionally relied on fragmented tools, manual processes, and extensive coordination. As hardware and firmware ecosystems become more complex, these approaches struggle to keep pace with faster development cycles and rising quality expectations. This session explores how Microsoft is transforming platform validation with AI and agent-driven workflows. By combining intelligent document understanding, automated requirement extraction, validation planning, execution orchestration, anomaly detection, and evidence-based sign-off recommendations, Microsoft is reducing manual effort, accelerating validation, and improving quality, traceability, and engineering productivity across the lifecycle.

### Fan Reliability Qualification: A standardized framework across the server fan supplier base
Speakers: Mahendra Lokhande (Meta), Kalpak Dhake (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9576/
As server tray designs become more dense, fans are increasingly difficult to service and lack hot-swap capabilities. Currently, the industry lacks a consistent framework for qualifying fan reliability across different suppliers. Problem Statement Frequent spikes in fleetwide fan failure rates ( multiple failure modes: solder defects, impeller cracks, electrical shorts)— causing substantial server repair downtime. Root Cause Analysis Failures primarily driven by inconsistent design and reliability qualification procedures among suppliers. Action Established a uniform reliability qualification standard for all suppliers which includes enhanced stress testing, design requirements and lifetime validation procedures. Key Outcomes Fleet fan failure rates have dropped significantly. Application on GPU server fans highlighted that switching impeller materials from PPT (Polytrimethylene-Terephthalate) to PPS (Polyphenylene-Sulfide ) leads to superior durability.

### High Speed Cable Cartridge AI System Validation
Speakers: Granthana Rangaswamy (Meta), Yuvin M. Weerasinghe (Meta)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9117/
Cable Cartridges can make or break AI systems. As AI workloads demand unprecedented bandwidth, minimizing unplanned unavailability is critical. Marginal Cable Cartridge performance leads to significant downtime from undiagnosed repairs. This talk presents a phase-gated validation framework for cable cartridges addressing the lack of standardized test methodologies. The framework defines stages (EVT, DVT, PVT, MP) with 4 corner testing for all platforms. Meta’s tool called INSPECT exemplifies this with a vendor-agnostic collection of SerDes metrics including SNR, FEC histograms, receiver coefficients, and BER. The framework defines metrics including DPPM per differential pair, Reseat Success Rate, and Cpk (≥1.33). It covers mating cycles, thermal cycling, and shock/vibration tests with standardized exit criteria. Fleet monitoring with AI-driven anomaly detection is also included. We want to share the enhanced validation methodology with the OCP community.

### From Tray to Training Job: L10–L12 Manufacturing Test
Speakers: Meghan Kachhi (Cisco), Sunny Dugar (Supermicro)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9101/
In AI infrastructure, manufacturing test only meaningfully begins at L10 - levels 1-9 cover components invisible to the operator on the floor. Grounded in joint Cisco-Supermicro work (the cross-vendor collaboration OCP was built to enable), this session walks the three levels that decide whether a AI cluster deployment commissions cleanly or stalls: L10 (Server): Tray assembly and isolated validation - POST, OS boot, CPU/GPU/DPU bring-up, BCM provisioning, firmware, burn-in. L11 (Rack): Validate the NVLink domain - cartridge seating, TOR/PDU, CDU leak checks, full NCCL all-reduce. L12 (Cluster): Multi-rack - Ethernet fabric, GPUDirect RDMA, BCM+Slurm/K8s+ DCGM, training job as acceptance gate.

### Product Intercept learnings and Compliance measurement for Diagnostics Standardization
Speakers: Rajat Madhusudan (Microsoft), Jennifer Lee (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9074/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-diag-cpact/tree/dev
Since its introduction, CPACT (Cloud Processor Accessibility Compliance Tool) has matured into a standardized, spec-driven framework that reduces integration overhead and eliminates ambiguity while welcoming a broader ecosystem of diagnostic providers. At its core, CPACT now interprets OCP diagnostic specifications unambiguously, programmatically selects the appropriate recipes for each use case, executes them consistently, and generates or verifies clear, actionable reports . This enables both silicon vendors and hyperscalers to rely on a common, machine-readable contract for diagnostics and debug workflows. For suppliers, CPACT provides a precise compliance path that accelerates release readiness. For hyperscalers, it ensures predictable, interoperable, and deployment-ready diagnostics at scale. This session highlights CPACT’s evolution into a specification-aligned compliance engine and its impact on improving diagnostic consistency, portability, and trust across the supply chain.

## Time Appliances

### Bounding Time Uncertainty in Distributed Systems
Speakers: Gary Giust, PhD (SiTime), Kishan Shenoi (SiTime Corp)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9240/
The talk addresses the need for precise and bounded time synchronization in distributed systems. Modern data centers and AI clusters rely on coordinating many compute elements whose efficiency depends on accurate temporal alignment. Misalignment introduces ambiguity in event ordering, degrades observability, and leads to system inefficiencies such as stragglers and idle resources. Time synchronization enables accurate performance diagnosis and fine-grained network control. Inherent clock uncertainties — arising from oscillators and network effects —force systems to adopt conservative guard bands. The central challenge is to quantify and bound time uncertainty in a way that can be trusted, enabling more efficient, distributed system operation. We describe a methodology to bound time uncertainty attributed to the oscillator considering various inherent and environmental factors and explain how to account for time uncertainty contributions from the time distribution network.

### Precision Time for AI Infrastructure: Deploying PTP Boundary Clocks at Scale
Speakers: Vikram Chandra (Nokia), Bhaskar Chinni (Broadcom)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9532/
As distributed AI and cloud workloads become increasingly latency sensitive, precise time synchronization is critical to application performance and infrastructure efficiency. This session explores deployment of IEEE 1588 Precision Time Protocol (PTP) in modern Ethernet fabrics, with Boundary Clock architecture, hardware timestamping, and operational considerations. It also highlights how SAI enables portable, interoperable PTP implementations across switch platforms, delivering sub-microsecond synchronization at scale for AI and distributed computing environments.

### Timing Recovery Using PTP within NIC and Distribution by ePPS to Computing Modules
Speakers: Sean Liu (Skyworks Solution), David Spencer (Skyworks Solutions Inc.)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9015/
Data center servers integrate network interface cards (NICs) and computing modules (CPUs/NPUs), requiring precise time synchronization. In NICs, Precision Time Protocol (PTP) recovers a reference clock, which is distributed via PCIe Precision Time Measurement (PTM) or physical-layer signals such as 10 MHz, 1PPS, and embedded PPS (ePPS). Compared to PTM, ePPS offers higher timing precision and is supported by Flex IO interfaces in the OCP NIC 3.0 specification. Timing error from the grandmaster (GM) to the NIC is mainly driven by PTP performance and packet delay variation. From the NIC to computing modules via Flex IO, error is dominated by hardware factors such as drivers, buffering, and level shifting. Accurate delay measurement and compensation are essential. This presentation examines factors impacting PTP and ePPS performance, supported by real-time lab measurement and analysis.

### A timecard as a resilient A-PNT custom solution
Speakers: Yair Neugeboren (Oscilloquartz), Anil Reddy (Oscilloquartz)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/8808/
The talk will discuss the evolution of a timecard as a resilient A-PNT custom solution. I will show how the OCP-TAP based Timecard design can support resilient solutions, including multi-receivers design, including a combination of GNSS and LEO based receivers. This design of a TC can meet and support Assured PNT resiliece level of 5 as specificed in IEEE p1952, and combined with anti-spoofing capabilities such as Galileo OSNMA. The talk will also present real data perfomance from field testing deploying an OCP-TAP based timecard, and demonstrate how this architecture can turn a COTS server into a PRTC-B resilient device with minimal integration effort (e.g. PTM performance when comparing the timecards PLL (PHC) to the system clock on the Server's CPU and to other NICs PHC on the same server). lastly I will discuss how different verticals such as finance/enterprise/mobile/ADG can use this Timecard design to meet their unique demands and topologies

### Multidomain Time Synchronization in the Modern Datacenter: From Network to Workload
Speakers: Christopher Hall (Intel)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9626/
There is no single, one-size-fits-all clock for datacenters. Different jobs need different time accuracy. Multisite infrastructure needs a common clock accurate enough for logging, auditing, and debugging. The AI training cluster wants something else entirely: rock-steady, sub-microsecond synchronization within the rack. Miss it and collective operations stall at their sync barriers while expensive, power-hungry silicon sits idle. Most operators compromise on the second, but they do not have to. This talk shows how multidomain PTP carries both on the same wires, off the same silicon, side by side: a loosely synced UTC domain for fleet-wide logging alongside a tight, free-running domain that keeps local clusters in lockstep. Then the hard part. Pushing hardware-backed time into VMs and containers. That is what IDPF-based virtual PTM solves. The result is one end-to-end multidomain story that runs from the time reference, through silicon, to the workload that needs it.

### Raspberry Pi Compute Module based Open Time Server Cluster (OTS-C) for Resilient Timing at the Edge
Speakers: Wil Myrick (ENSCO)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9902/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/w/index.php?title=Open_Time_Server_Cluster
ENSCO presents an Open Time Server Cluster (OTS-C) architecture for creating resilient timing servers at the edge combining open source timing software and inexpensive commercial-off-the-shelf (COTS) hardware. The architecture leverages open-source timing software Chrony, Raspberry Pi compute modules, and an array of timing receivers to create a flexible resilient timing server at the edge. The OTS-C approach is part of the Open Compute Project providing resilient timing solutions with respect to the Time Appliances Project (TAP). The Open Time Server Cluster (OTS-C) Specification is a timing reference architecture that builds on top of the Open, Scalable and Validated reference architecture of Open Time Servers (OTS) with respect to cluster configurations. The OTS-C timing reference architecture is built on a PTP network configuration that integrates with open time server deployments for Data Centers (DCs) and edge environments.

### High Radix Switches and Precise Time
Speakers: Dan Biederman (Eridu AI)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9871/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=opgWpLbUrg0
As the number of ports in the switch increase substantially, it introduces novel and interesting time-related scenarios. This paper will discuss some aspects of these scenarios to share some of the finding.

### Clock-Aligned Telemetry for AI Clusters: From BMC to GPU to Fleet
Speakers: Ahmad Byagowi (TBD), Lukas Landgraf (turbalance)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9129/ | materials: https://github.com/ahmadexp-turba/Analytics
AI infrastructure operators have many dashboards but no common evidence model linking GPU counters, host pressure, BMC power/thermal, scheduler placement, NCCL behavior, benchmarks, and clock skew. This session shares a lab-built reference architecture for clock-aligned AI telemetry: a source-bundle contract, live agents, a conservative benchmark ladder, a Redfish/BMC lane, and a lakehouse path that preserve provenance and never synthesize unsupported metrics. Prototyped on a heterogeneous lab fleet, it links DCGM/NVML, host counters, Prometheus, Kubernetes, Redfish, eBPF/OTel evidence, and PTP/NTP clock state. Per-datacenter ingestion without global shared state feeds one Iceberg layer over cold and fresh stores. We show a six-level benchmark ladder (metric to global baseline), auto-discovery, credential-gated agents, 1s freshness, capability labels, and how clock alignment changes cross-machine trust. Attendees gain schema patterns, tradeoffs, and an OCP collaboration path.

### Precision-Time Circuit Switching for AI Training Fabrics: When Packet Switching Is Enough, and When It Isn’t
Speakers: Sahas Munamala (OCP TAP), Ahmad Byagowi (TBD)
page: https://2026ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/9845/ | materials: https://github.com/sahasam/ocs-experiments
Distributed AI training stresses datacenter interconnects through repeated collectives such as all-reduce, reduce-scatter, all-gather, and all-to-all. While network activity can consume much of step time, only critical-path communication affects job completion time. We ask whether precision time synchronization, paired with hybrid packet/circuit switching, can improve workload performance. Precision time enables deterministic launch windows, endpoint pacing, and circuit reconfiguration; circuits help when traffic is large, predictable, repeated, and poorly hidden by compute. Using analytical bounds, trace replay, and packet-level simulation, we analyze Llama-3 8B and find a sharp split: data-parallel all-reduce hides behind computation and is mostly unaffected, while pipeline traffic congests by up to 15.2x as over-subscription and data-parallel degree grow. The framework shows when precision-time scheduling and hybrid fabrics reduce step time, and when packet switching is sufficient.
